4.3  How much are the tomatoes?
 1   Get ready
We SAY… | We WRITE…
How much is the cheese? | 1 kg tomatoes
How many lemons do you want? | 2 bottles of water
Anything else? | a bunch of parsley
How much is that altogether? | Money in the drawer: 60,000
b  a kilo of tomatoes · a bottle of water · a packet of biscuits · a bag of rice · a piece of cheese · a can of tuna
 2   Listen and read
a  B (vegetables)   b  1 Six.  2 34,000 SYP.
Language box – Practise:  1 many  2 much  3 much  4 much  5 many
 3   Practise – sample with shopping list A
A | Good morning! What would you like?
B | How much are the potatoes?
A | Six thousand a kilo.
B | I'd like two kilos, please. And six eggs and half a kilo of cheese.
A | Here you are. Anything else?
B | No, thanks. How much is that altogether?
A | Twelve, twelve and twenty – that's forty-four thousand.
 3R   Perform
List A total: potatoes 12,000 + eggs 12,000 + cheese 20,000 = 44,000. List B total: rice 15,000 + water 15,000 + biscuits 8,000 = 38,000.
 4   Your turn – sample
Mansaf for six: 2 kg lamb · 1 big bag of rice (3 kg) · 2 kg jameed (dried yoghurt) · 2 packets of Arabic bread · 200 g almonds · a bunch of parsley. Seller's prices: about 450,000 SYP altogether.
 4R   Report – sample
"Tareq is making mansaf for six people. He needs two kilos of meat, a big bag of rice and a lot of yoghurt. It costs about four hundred and fifty thousand pounds."
 5   Read
1 On Thursday after work.  2 Next to the spice shop.  3 60,000 SYP.
 6   Write with help – sample
Hiba – I'm cooking for my parents on Sunday! Can you go to the supermarket after college? Here's the list:  • 1 kg chicken  • 2 kg potatoes  • 1 bag of rice  • a packet of butter  • 6 eggs  • a bottle of oil   The supermarket on the main street has the best chicken. Don't buy the small potatoes. There's 150,000 in my bag. Thanks!  Rasha
 6P   Check together – sample
Checklist | ✓ / ✗
Capitals and full stops | ✓
Quantity + of + food | ✗ – a packet butter → a packet of butter
Plural -s after numbers | ✓
All parts | ✓
 6R   Share – sample
Before: a packet butter  →  After: a packet of butter
 7   Write freely – sample (≈65 words)
Mum – the guests are coming on Friday and I'm making mansaf! Can you buy these things in the souq on Thursday, please?• 2 kg lamb (from Abu Khaled – his meat is the best)  • a big bag of rice  • 2 kg jameed  • 2 packets of Arabic bread  • 200 g almonds  • a bunch of parsleyDon't buy the bread early – it isn't fresh on Friday. There's 500,000 in the kitchen drawer. Thank you! Tareq
 7P   Respond – sample slip
★ Clear list!   ✓ Can you add how much jameed we need for six people?   ? Which shop sells good almonds?
 7R   Share – sample
Class guesses the dish from the list: lamb + rice + jameed = mansaf.
 HW   Homework – sample text message
I'm at the souq. There isn't any jameed at Abu Khaled's today. Shall I buy yoghurt instead? And how many packets of bread do you want – two or three?

4.4  First, cut the bread
 1   Get ready
We SAY… | We WRITE…
First, wash the vegetables. | Ingredients: 2 tomatoes, 1 onion…
Then fry the onion. | Method:
Don't add the salt now! | Boil for 10 minutes.
How long? | Serve with yoghurt.
b  cut – a knife · fry – a pan with oil · boil – water + 100°C · mix – a spoon · add – put in more · wash – water from the tap
 2   Listen and read
a  B (fattoush)   b  1 Cut it into small squares and fry it (or bake it) until it's brown.  2 The dressing and the bread, just before you eat.
Language box – Practise (Arabic tea):  1 First, boil some water.  2 Then put the tea in the pot and add the water.  3 After that, wait for three minutes.  4 Next, pour the tea into small glasses.  5 Finally, add sugar if you like.
 3   Practise – sample with card A (hummus)
B | What do I do first?
A | First, boil the chickpeas for one hour – or use a can.
B | OK. Then?
A | Then put the chickpeas, tahini, lemon juice, garlic and salt in a blender. After that, mix until it's smooth.
B | Is it too thick?
A | Then add a little cold water.
B | And then?
A | Finally, serve it with olive oil on top. Don't add too much garlic!
 3R   Perform
Listeners write: First → Then → After that → (Next) → Finally → Don't…
 4   Your turn – sample (five steps)
Hiba's grandmother's lentil soup: 1 Wash the lentils. 2 Fry a chopped onion in oil until it's golden. 3 Add the lentils, water, salt and cumin. 4 Boil for thirty minutes. 5 Finally, mix it well and serve it with lemon.
 4R   Report – sample
"Hiba's grandmother's lentil soup: first, wash the lentils. Then fry an onion in oil until it's golden. After that, add the lentils, water, salt and cumin and boil them for thirty minutes. Finally, mix it and serve it with lemon."
 5   Read
1 Four people.  2 Olive oil, lemon juice, pomegranate molasses, sumac and salt (the dressing).  3 Some fried halloumi cheese.
 6   Write with help – sample
Rasha's Lentil Soup (for 4 people · 40 minutes)  Ingredients: 2 cups of red lentils · 1 onion · 2 spoons of oil · 1 teaspoon of cumin · salt · 1 lemon  Method: First, wash the lentils. Then chop the onion and fry it in the oil until it's golden. After that, add the lentils, the cumin, the salt and six cups of water. Next, boil it for thirty minutes. Finally, mix it well and serve it with lemon. Don't add the lemon early!  Tip: add fried bread on top.
 6P   Check together – sample
Checklist | ✓ / ✗
Capitals and full stops | ✓
Each step starts with a base verb | ✗ – After that, you adding the lentils → After that, add the lentils
Sequencers in order | ✓
All parts | ✓
 6R   Share – sample
Before: After that, you adding the lentils.  →  After: After that, add the lentils.
 7   Write freely – sample (≈90 words)
Fadi's Quick Shakshuka (for 2 people · 20 minutes)Ingredients: 1 onion · 4 tomatoes · 4 eggs · 2 spoons of olive oil · salt and pepper · a little cumin · bread to serveMethod: First, chop the onion and fry it in the oil for five minutes. Then cut the tomatoes into small pieces and add them with the salt, pepper and cumin. Next, cook everything for ten minutes until the sauce is thick. After that, make four small holes in the sauce and break an egg into each one. Finally, cover the pan and cook for five minutes until the eggs are white. Don't mix the eggs!Tip: eat it with fresh Arabic bread – no forks!
 7P   Respond – sample slip
★ I can make this!   ✓ Can you add how big the pan is?   ? Can I use green pepper instead of cumin?
 7R   Share – sample
The class cookbook has one recipe per student; each student signs their name next to the recipe they will try.
 HW   Homework – sample message
I made your shakshuka on Friday morning. It was delicious! My brother ate three eggs. The sauce was a little thin, so I cooked it for five more minutes. One question: can I add cheese on top?

4.5  Would you like to come for dinner?
 1   Get ready
We SAY… | We WRITE…
Would you like to come for dinner? | You're invited! Dinner at our flat
I'd love to! What time? | Friday at 7 pm
I'm afraid I can't. | Please let me know by Wednesday.
Shall I bring something? | Thank you for inviting me.
b  Yes: I'd love to. · That sounds great! · Thanks, I'd like that.   No: I'm afraid I can't. · Sorry, I'm busy. · Maybe another time.
 2   Listen and read
a  B (Friday)   b  1 She's going to Latakia for her father's birthday.  2 Some fruit.
Language box – Practise:  1 to go  2 to  3 afraid  4 inviting  5 shame
 3   Practise – sample (card A)
A | Would you like to come for lunch at my mother's house on Friday?
B | Oh, I'd love to! What time?
A | About two o'clock.
B | Shall I bring something?
A | You don't need to bring anything.
B | I'll bring some sweets, then. Thank you!
Card B sample refusal: "I'm really sorry, I'm afraid I can't. I'm working on Saturday morning." – "Oh, that's a shame. Never mind – maybe next time."
 3R   Perform
All six phrases should appear across the two role-plays (one yes, one no).
 4   Your turn – sample
Hiba | Would you like to come for a walk in the Old City on Saturday morning?
Fadi | That sounds great! I'd love to. What time?
Hiba | Ten o'clock, at Bab Touma.
Hiba | Tareq, would you like to come?
Tareq | I'm really sorry, I'm afraid I can't. I'm working on Saturday. Thank you for inviting me!
 4R   Report – sample
"I invited four people to a walk in the Old City on Saturday. Fadi and Hiba are coming, and Hiba is bringing her camera. Tareq can't come because he's working, and Rasha is visiting her aunt."
 5   Read
1 The dessert.  2 So he can buy enough food.  3 Save her some kibbeh.
 6   Write with help – samples
Invitation: Hi Rasha! Would you like to come for lunch at my flat on Friday at 2 pm? We're making maqluba and my sister is making a cake. The address is Building 5, Al-Zahra Street, Jaramana, 2nd floor. Please let me know by Thursday. Hiba
Reply: Thank you so much for the invitation, Hiba! I'd love to come. Shall I bring some juice? Have a lovely week – see you on Friday! Rasha
 6P   Check together – sample
Checklist | ✓ / ✗
Capitals and full stops | ✓
Would you like to + base verb | ✗ – Would you like come → Would you like to come
What, when, where + reply date | ✗ – add the reply date
Reply has thanks + answer + reason/offer | ✓
 6R   Share – sample
Before: Would you like come for lunch?  →  After: Would you like to come for lunch?
 7   Write freely – sample invitation + reply (≈70 words)
Invitation: Hi everyone! Would you like to come for a picnic in Tishreen Park on Saturday at 11 am? I'm bringing sandwiches and tea, and Fadi is bringing his guitar. We'll meet at the main gate, near the fountain. Please let me know by Thursday evening so I can buy enough food. Hiba
Reply (no): Thank you so much for the invitation, Hiba! I'm really sorry, but I'm afraid I can't come. I work every Saturday morning until two. Please take a lot of photos! Maybe next time – and tell Fadi to practise! Tareq
 7P   Respond – sample slip
★ Thanks for your kind reply!   ✓ Can you tell me if you're free next Saturday?   ? Can you come after two?
 7R   Share – sample
Class check: a polite refusal has thanks + sorry + reason + a friendly ending.
 HW   Homework – sample second reply (now yes)
Hi Hiba, good news – my manager changed my shift, so I can come to the picnic after all! I'd love to. Shall I bring some fruit and a big bottle of water? See you at the main gate at eleven. Tareq

4.6  This is delicious!
 1   Get ready
We SAY… | We WRITE…
This is delicious! | Thank you for a wonderful evening.
What a lovely dinner! | The food was amazing.
Can I have the recipe? | Next time, come to my place!
No, thank you – I'm full. | Thanks again,
b  😀 delicious, tasty, fresh, amazing, perfect (and spicy for people who like it)   🙁 salty, burnt, too sweet, cold
 2   Listen and read
a  C (It's really good.)   b  1 The kibbeh recipe.  2 The muhallabiyeh (dessert).
Language box – Practise (sample answers):  2 What a nice garden!  3 This cake is really soft!  4 What an interesting book!  5 Your English is very good!
 3   Practise – sample with card A
A | Please, help yourself.
B | This mansaf is delicious! It's really tasty.
A | Thank you! It's my grandmother's recipe.
B | What a beautiful garden!
A | You're very kind. Have some more rice!
B | No, thank you – I'm full. Thank you for a wonderful lunch.
A | Thank you for coming!
 3R   Perform
Two compliments per performance, e.g. This mansaf is delicious! and What a beautiful garden! Listeners also judge the intonation: did the voice go high on the stressed word?
 4   Your turn – sample
Fadi | This is my mother's maqluba.
Hiba | What a beautiful dish! The colours are amazing.
Lina | It looks so good!
Fadi | Thank you! She makes it every Friday.
 4R   Report – sample
"Fadi showed a photo of his mother's maqluba. Everyone said 'What a beautiful dish!' Hiba said the colours were amazing."
 5   Read
1 The kibbeh (so crispy) and the fattoush (really fresh) – and Rami's muhallabiyeh.  2 Send her his mother's recipe.  3 To invite them to her place and cook mujaddara.
 6   Write with help – sample
Dear Umm Samer,   Thank you so much for Friday lunch! The food was wonderful – the maqluba was so tasty, and the salad was really fresh. It was lovely to meet all your family. Next time, I'd like to invite you to my flat!   Thanks again,   Hiba
 6P   Check together – sample
Checklist | ✓ / ✗
Capitals and full stops | ✓
very / really / so + adjective | ✗ – so much tasty → so tasty
Thank you for + noun / -ing | ✓
All parts | ✓
 6R   Share – sample
Before: The maqluba was so much tasty.  →  After: The maqluba was so tasty.
 7   Write freely – sample (≈70 words)
Dear Abu Nabil and Umm Nabil,Thank you so much for dinner last night! Everything was delicious – the kibbeh was so crispy and the muhammara was really special. I'd love the recipe! It was also lovely to meet your grandchildren; they taught me a new card game. What a warm evening! Next month, please come to our flat – Rami will cook and I promise to make the dessert.Thanks again,Omar
 7P   Respond – sample slip (as host)
★ What a kind message!   ✓ Can you add which recipe you want?   ? When is good for you next month?
 7R   Share – sample
Best compliment (sample): "What a warm evening!" – because it compliments the people, not only the food.
 HW   Homework – sample host's reply
Dear Omar, Thank you for your lovely message! I'm so glad you liked the kibbeh – I'll write the recipe for you this week. The children ask about you every day! We'd love to come next month; any Friday is good for us. You're always welcome. Umm Nabil

4.7  Review & Mediate: Does this have nuts in it?
 1   Get ready – quiz
1 I have bread for breakfast.  2 How much sugar do you want?  3 I'd like a coffee, please.  4 Would you like to come to dinner?  5 First, cut the bread.  6 Thank you for inviting me.
 2   Listen and read
b  Nuts (he's allergic) and meat (he doesn't eat it).
 3   Practise – sample (visitor card A: vegetarian, no dairy, likes spicy)
Visitor | I'm vegetarian and I can't eat dairy. I love spicy food!
B | Then the muhammara is perfect for you – it's a red pepper dip, it's a little spicy, and there's no meat and no milk in it. But it has walnuts in it – is that OK?
Visitor | Yes, nuts are fine.
B | Good. For your main course, the mujaddara is vegetarian – rice and lentils with fried onions. Don't order the shish barak: it has meat and yoghurt in it.
Visitor | And dessert?
B | The muhallabiyeh is a milk pudding, so it's not for you. Let's ask the waiter for a fruit plate.
 3R   Perform
Card A: muhammara, mujaddara, fruit plate (avoid shish barak, muhallabiyeh). Card B: muhammara or fattoush, shish barak or mujaddara, muhallabiyeh (avoid the grilled fish; muhammara is spicy, so fattoush may be better).
 4   Your turn – sample
Tareq | Kibbeh nayyeh is raw meat with bulgur and spices. We eat it with olive oil and onion.
Hiba (visitor, vegetarian) | Oh! Not for me.
Tareq | Then try yalanji – vine leaves with rice, tomato and herbs. There's no meat in it. And sheikh el-mahshi has meat in it, so not for you.
 4R   Report – mediation sample
"Tareq explained kibbeh nayyeh to me. It's raw meat with bulgur, so it's not for vegetarians. He recommended yalanji – vine leaves with rice – because there's no meat in it."
 5   Read – A2 Key Reading Part 1
1 A  2 B  3 C  4 B
 6   Write with help – sample
Hi Sami,   You can eat at Beit Al-Yasmin! The fattoush is vegetarian – it's a fresh salad with crispy bread. The mujaddara is also fine – it's rice and lentils with fried onions, and there's no meat and no dairy in it. Don't order the shish barak because it has meat and yoghurt in it, and the muhammara has walnuts in it. For dessert, ask for a fruit plate – the muhallabiyeh is made with milk.   Omar
 6P   Check together – sample
Checklist | ✓ / ✗
Two safe dishes, explained | ✓
Two dishes to avoid + reasons | ✓
Dessert advice | ✗ – add the fruit plate
Own words | ✓
 7   Write freely – A2 Key Writing Part 6 sample (≈45 words)
Hi Emma,Please come to my home on Saturday at seven o'clock. I'm going to cook fattoush, grilled chicken with rice and a milk pudding for dessert. Is there any food you don't eat? Tell me and I'll make something else.See you on Saturday!Lina
Marking note: all three content points (time, what you'll cook, question about food she doesn't eat) must be present for full content marks.
 ✓   Unit 4 check
A  1 c  2 b  3 b  4 a  5 b  6 c  7 a  8 b
B  1 B  2 B  3 C  4 B  5 C
C  Sample message (≈45 words):
Hi Fadi! Would you like to come to Beit Al-Yasmin with me on Thursday after class? Their fattoush is really fresh and the grilled fish is delicious. It isn't expensive and the Old City is beautiful in the evening. Let me know!  Maya
Scoring guide: A = 8 marks, B = 5 marks, C = 5 marks (content 2, organisation 1, language 2). Total 18. 13+ = ready for Unit 5.

Answer Key · Unit 5  Shopping & Money
Proactive English · A2 · Book 1 · Syria Edition
Every task has an answer or a full sample answer. Samples show one good A2 answer; learners' own answers will differ.
5.1  Have you got this in a larger size?
 1   Get ready
We SAY… | We WRITE…
Have you got this in blue? | Size: medium · Colour: black
I'm looking for a jacket. | Free delivery over 300,000 SYP
Can I try it on? | Price: 480,000 SYP
I'll take this one. | Message us on WhatsApp.
b  Clothes: shirt, shoes, trousers, scarf, jacket, dress, gloves, socks (not: towel). Always plural: shoes, trousers, gloves, socks (also jeans, glasses).
 2   Listen and read
a  C (a shirt)   b  1 Large.  2 Light blue.
Language box – Practise:  1 these  2 those  3 yours; mine  4 are
 3   Practise – sample with card A
A | Good afternoon. Can I help you?
B | Yes, please. I'm looking for a jacket for work.
A | Of course. We've got these grey ones here, and those dark blue jackets are new.
B | I like this one. Have you got it in a medium?
A | Let me check… Yes. Would you like to try it on?
B | Yes, please. (later) It fits well, but I'm not sure about the colour. I don't really like grey.
A | We've got it in dark blue too.
B | That's better. I'll take the blue one.
 3R   Perform
Card A: a jacket, medium, dark blue. Card B: shoes, size 42, black. Typical board-clinic errors: this shoes → these shoes; Is this bag your? → …yours?
 4   Your turn – sample
Hiba | Is this pen yours?
Fadi | No, it isn't mine. I think it's Rasha's. Are these keys yours?
Hiba | Yes, they're mine, thank you! Last week I bought a long grey coat at Al-Hamra Centre. I take a medium. It was 400,000 pounds – expensive, but it's very warm.
 4R   Report – sample
"Hiba bought a long grey coat at Al-Hamra Centre. She takes a medium. It was 400,000 pounds – she says it was expensive, but it's very warm."
 5   Read
1 Soft wool.  2 They are small, so you should order one size larger.  3 For orders over 300,000 SYP (in Damascus).
 6   Write with help – sample
WOMEN'S WOOL SCARF – HIBA'S SHOP  A warm scarf for winter. It's made of soft wool, with long thin ends. It's perfect for work or for a present.  Colours: grey, dark red, cream  Sizes: one size (180 cm – quite long)  Price: 120,000 SYP. Delivery: 20,000 SYP in Damascus.  Message us on WhatsApp: 0936 145 870.
 6P   Check together – sample
Checklist | ✓ / ✗
Capitals and full stops | ✓
Adjective order; made of | ✗ – a wool soft scarf → a soft wool scarf
this / these correct | ✓
All parts | ✓
 6R   Share – sample
Before: It's made from a wool soft.  →  After: It's made of soft wool.
 7   Write freely – sample (≈70 words)
USED BICYCLE FOR SALE – MEZZEHA blue city bicycle, three years old. It's made of light metal, so it's easy to carry up the stairs. There are two new tyres and a small bag behind the seat. It's perfect for students and short journeys in the city.Honest problem: the bell doesn't work and there's a mark on the frame.Price: 900,000 SYP. You can see it near Al-Yasmin Centre after 5 pm.Call or message: Omar, 0944 318 260.
 7P   Respond – sample slip

 7R   Share – sample
Class shop: the bicycle and the wool scarf get the most names.
 HW   Homework – sample message

5.2  This one's cheaper
 1   Get ready
We SAY… | We WRITE…
This one's cheaper than that one. | Phone A: 2,500,000 · Phone B: 3,900,000
The battery is better. | The camera is much better.
Which one is better? | It costs one million more.
It's a little smaller. | I think the Star X is the better choice.
b  cheap – expensive · big – small · old – new · fast – slow · heavy – light · good – bad
 2   Listen and read
a  B (Omar's cousin, Nabil)   b  1 The newer, more expensive one.  2 The cheaper one.
Language box – Practise:  1 bigger  2 more expensive  3 easier  4 better  5 lighter  6 more comfortable  7 cheaper  8 better
 3   Practise – sample with card A
A | Which fridge is better?
B | Let me see. This one is bigger and newer, but it's more expensive.
A | How much more?
B | It's one million three hundred thousand more than that one.
A | Is it quieter too?
B | Actually, no. The smaller one is quieter and it uses less electricity.
A | OK, I'll take the smaller one.
 3R   Perform
Sample sentences: This fridge is bigger than that one. · The old car is cheaper, but it uses more petrol.
 4   Your turn – sample
"Suwayda is quieter and cleaner than Damascus, and the air is colder in winter. But Damascus is bigger, more interesting and the shops are better. Houses are cheaper in Suwayda, but there are more jobs in Damascus."
 4R   Report – sample
"Tareq says Suwayda is quieter and cleaner than Damascus, but Damascus is more interesting and the shops are better. He says the air is colder in Suwayda in winter."
 5   Read
1 The Nova 7.  2 Because the parts are cheaper in Syria (and it's easier to repair).  3 People who love photography.
 6   Write with help – sample
Which café for studying?  Last week I compared two cafés near the university.  Al-Bustan is bigger and more comfortable. But it's noisier and the coffee is more expensive.  Café Nour is smaller and quieter, so you can study there for hours. The Wi-Fi is faster, and the coffee is cheaper. The chairs are a little harder, but it isn't a problem.  I think Café Nour is better for students. But if you want to meet a big group of friends, go to Al-Bustan!
 6P   Check together – sample
Checklist | ✓ / ✗
Capitals and full stops | ✓
Comparatives (-er / more, not both) | ✗ – more cheaper → cheaper
than after the comparative | ✓
All parts | ✓
 6R   Share – sample
Before: The coffee is more cheaper that Al-Bustan.  →  After: The coffee is cheaper than at Al-Bustan.
 7   Write freely – sample (≈85 words)
Which school for my son? Two schools in MezzehMy sister asked me about two schools near her house, so I visited both.Al-Amal School is newer and the building is more modern. The classes are bigger – about thirty children – and it's more expensive.Al-Nahda School is older, but the classes are smaller and the teachers are more experienced. It's also closer to my sister's flat, so her son can walk. The playground is smaller, but the children go to the park on Wednesdays.I think Al-Nahda is better for a young child. But if you want modern computers, choose Al-Amal.
 7P   Respond – sample slip
★ Good advice!   ✓ Can you add the price of each school?   ? How many children are in a class at Al-Nahda?
 7R   Share – sample
Class discussion: most agree small classes matter more than a new building.
 HW   Homework – sample reply
I agree with you about the small classes, but for me the distance is the most important thing. My children are young and I work, so a school near the house is better – even if the classes are bigger.

5.3  It's the cheapest shop in town
 1   Get ready
We SAY… | We WRITE…
It's the cheapest shop in town. | The best shop for shoes is…
Where's the best place to buy…? | It has the biggest choice.
The quality isn't the best. | My tip: go in the morning.
It's the easiest shop to find. | The best thing about the shop is…
b  the cheapest · the biggest · the easiest · the most expensive · the best · the worst
 2   Listen and read
a  A (a present for a child – her niece Jana)   b  1 Al-Hamra Centre.  2 To Abu Yarub's little shop opposite the main gate of the university.
Language box – Practise:  1 the cheapest  2 the best  3 the most expensive  4 the shortest  5 the worst
 3   Practise – sample with card A
A | Where's the best place to buy a bicycle?
B | The big shop in Mezzeh has the biggest choice, but it's the most expensive.
A | What about the street market?
B | It's the cheapest, but the quality is the worst.
A | What's the most popular shop?
B | There's a small shop in Qassaa – it's got the best repairs in the city and good prices.
A | Thanks! That sounds perfect.
 3R   Perform
Three superlatives, e.g. the biggest choice · the most expensive · the best repairs (card A); the most famous · the cheapest · the freshest (card B).
 4   Your turn – sample group list
Category | Our choice | Why
best cheap clothes | Souq Al-Hamidiyah | the cheapest prices and you can ask for a discount
best café | Al-Nawfara | the oldest café in Damascus and the storyteller in the evening
best family day out | Tishreen Park | the biggest park, cheap, good for children
most beautiful street | Straight Street (Bab Sharqi) | the most famous old street – beautiful at night
worst traffic | Mezzeh highway at 5 pm | it's the slowest road in the city
 4R   Report – sample
"For cheap clothes, the best place is Souq Al-Hamidiyah. The best café is Al-Nawfara because it's the oldest in Damascus. For families, Tishreen Park is the best – it's the biggest park and it isn't expensive. And the worst traffic is on the Mezzeh highway at five o'clock!"
 5   Read
1 Opposite the main gate of Damascus University.  2 Because Abu Yarub makes many of them himself from wood (not plastic).  3 It's the quietest time and he has more time to help you.
 6   Write with help – sample
The best bakery in Mezzeh  If you want fresh bread, go to Abu Ali's bakery on Al-Ward Street.  It isn't the biggest bakery in the area, and it isn't the most modern. But it has the best ka'ak in Mezzeh, and the bread is always hot.  The best thing about the bakery is the price: it's the cheapest in the street.  My tip: go before eight o'clock – after nine the ka'ak is finished!
 6P   Check together – sample
Checklist | ✓ / ✗
Capitals and full stops | ✓
the + -est / the most (+ in / of) | ✗ – it has best ka'ak → the best ka'ak
One honest negative | ✓
All parts | ✓
 6R   Share – sample
Before: It has best ka'ak in Mezzeh.  →  After: It has the best ka'ak in Mezzeh.
 7   Write freely – sample (≈80 words)
The best place for a family day out in DamascusIf you have children and only one free day, go to Tishreen Park.It isn't the newest park in the city, and the café isn't the cheapest. But it's the biggest green space in Damascus, and it has the best playground for small children. On Fridays it's the busiest place in the city – but that's part of the fun!The best thing about the park is the old trees: even in August it's the coolest place I know.My tip: go early and take your own food and tea.
 7P   Respond – sample slip
★ I want to go!   ✓ Can you add how much the entrance costs?   ? Is it open on Fridays in winter?
 7R   Share – sample
Class guide: the park and the old café are the most popular choices.
 HW   Homework – sample reply
I know Tishreen Park – my children love it! For books, the best place is the Sunday market near the university. It's the cheapest place for second-hand books and the sellers are the friendliest in the city.

5.4  Can I pay by card?
 1   Get ready
We SAY… | We WRITE…
Can I pay by card? | Please keep your receipt.
How long does it take? | Opening hours: 8 am – 3 pm
Could you fill in this form, please? | Fill in the green form.
Here you are. | Payment: cash or card
b  send a parcel → the post office · change money → the bank (or an exchange office) · buy medicine → the pharmacy · pay your internet bill → the internet company office (or online / an app)
 2   Listen and read
a  B (send a parcel)   b  1 Express takes four or five days (normal post about two weeks).  2 In cash (the card machine isn't working).
Language box – Practise:  1 Can I  2 Can you  3 Can I  4 Can you  5 Can I
 3   Practise – sample with card A
B | Next, please. How can I help you?
A | Hello. I'd like to change two hundred dollars, please.
B | OK. Could you fill in this form and show me your ID, please?
A | Of course. How long does it take?
B | About ten minutes.
A | Can I pay by card?
B | I'm sorry, for this service we only take cash.
A | No problem. Here you are.
B | Here's your receipt. Please keep it.
 3R   Perform
All six phrases appear in a complete performance.
 4   Your turn – sample
Customer | I'd like to send this parcel to Aleppo, please.
Clerk | Could you put it on the scale? It's one kilo – that's 40,000.
Customer | Can I pay by card?
Clerk | Yes, of course. Here's your receipt.
 4R   Report – sample
"At the bank I changed a hundred dollars. It took ten minutes and I had to fill in a form. At the post office the machine wasn't working, so I paid in cash. The pharmacy was the fastest – two minutes!"
 5   Read
1 On Fridays.  2 Because staff must see what is inside.  3 You need the number on it to follow your parcel online.
 6   Write with help – sample
AL-NOUR CLINIC – INFORMATION FOR PATIENTS  Opening hours: Saturday – Thursday 9 am – 6 pm · Friday closed  Appointments: Please call one day before. Bring your ID card and your old reports.  Prices: First visit 60,000 SYP · Second visit 40,000 SYP  Payment: cash only. Important: come ten minutes early.  Questions? Call 011 445 2233.
 6P   Check together – sample
Checklist | ✓ / ✗
Capitals and full stops | ✓
Short sections with headings | ✓
A clear warning | ✗ – add "Important:" before "come ten minutes early"
All parts | ✓
 6R   Share – sample
Before: come ten minutes early please.  →  After: Important: Please come ten minutes early.
 7   Write freely – sample (≈75 words)
TECHCARE COMPUTER REPAIRS – INFORMATION FOR CUSTOMERSOpening hours: Sunday – Thursday 9 am – 5 pm · Saturday 10 am – 2 pm · Friday closedRepairs: Please bring your charger with the computer. Small repairs take one day; bigger repairs take three to five days. We call you before we start any repair over 100,000 SYP.Prices: Checking your computer is free. Cleaning: 50,000 SYP. New screen: from 400,000 SYP.Payment: cash or card. Important: please keep your ticket – we cannot give you the computer without it.Questions? Ask at the desk or call 011 667 8899.
 7P   Respond – sample slip
★ Very clear!   ✓ Can you add the price for changing a battery?   ? Can I pay in instalments?
 7R   Share – sample
Question the notice doesn't answer (sample): "Do you repair phones too, or only computers?"
 HW   Homework – sample email
Dear TechCare, Do you repair phones too, or only computers? My laptop screen is broken – how much is a new screen for a Dell laptop, and how long does it take? Thank you. Hiba Khalil

5.5  It doesn't work
 1   Get ready
We SAY… | We WRITE…
It doesn't work. | I would like a refund, please.
It's too small. | The dress is green, not blue.
Have you got the receipt? | My order number is 4471.
Can I change it? | Could you tell me how to return it?
b  It's broken / It doesn't work – a kettle · There's a hole in it – a shirt · It's too small – shoes · It's broken (a crack) – a screen · It's the wrong colour – an online order
 2   Listen and read
a  A (it's broken – the water doesn't get hot enough)   b  1 Last week.  2 A new kettle – a bigger, different model (for 20,000 more).
Language box – Practise:  1 too  2 enough  3 enough  4 too  5 enough
 3   Practise – sample with problem card A
A | Excuse me, I bought this jacket here three days ago, but there's a hole in the pocket.
B | I'm sorry to hear that. What exactly is the problem?
A | Look – the pocket isn't strong enough. My keys went through it!
B | I see. Have you got the receipt?
A | Yes, here it is.
B | I can change it for a new one, or you can have your money back.
A | I'd prefer a refund, please.
 3R   Perform
Card A: hole in the pocket → refund. Card B: one side of the headphones doesn't work → new pair.
 4   Your turn – sample
"I bought a phone charger in the market for 30,000. It worked for two days and then it stopped. I went back to the shop, but the man didn't change it because I didn't have the receipt. Now I always keep my receipts!"
 4R   Report – sample
"Fadi bought a phone charger in the market. It worked for two days and then it stopped. He went back, but the shop didn't change it because he didn't have the receipt. Now he always keeps his receipts!"
 5   Read
1 The dress is the wrong colour (green, not blue) and it's too small.  2 The correct dress in blue, size M – or a refund.  3 How to return the dress.
 6   Write with help – sample
Subject: Problem with order 2290   Dear Sir or Madam,   I ordered a black office chair from your website on 10 October. The chair arrived on Monday, but there are two problems. First, one wheel is broken. Second, the chair isn't high enough – the photo on your website shows a taller chair.   I would like to return the chair and receive a new one. If you haven't got the same model, I would prefer a refund.   My order number is 2290 and my phone number is 0947 220 518. Could you tell me how to return it, please?   Yours faithfully,   Tareq Mansour
 6P   Check together – sample
Checklist | ✓ / ✗
Capitals and full stops | ✓
Polite forms | ✓
too / not … enough correct | ✗ – isn't enough high → isn't high enough
All parts | ✓
 6R   Share – sample
Before: The chair isn't enough high.  →  After: The chair isn't high enough.
 7   Write freely – sample (≈85 words)
Subject: Problem with order 6132 – winter jacketDear Sir or Madam,I ordered a dark blue winter jacket (size L) from your website on 2 October, and it arrived yesterday. Unfortunately, there are two problems. First, there is a mark on the left sleeve. Second, the jacket is too small – it is a large, but it fits like a medium.I would like to return the jacket and receive a size XL in the same colour. If you haven't got an XL, I would prefer a refund.My order number is 6132 and my phone number is 0944 318 260. Could you tell me how to send it back, please?Yours faithfully,Omar Darwish
 7P   Respond – sample slip (as the shop)
★ Clear and polite!   ✓ Can you add a photo of the mark?   ? Did you wash the jacket?
 7R   Share – sample
Class decision: yes – the email is polite, gives the order number and says exactly what the customer wants.
 HW   Homework – sample shop reply
Dear Mr Darwish, Thank you for your email and I am very sorry about the problem. We have the jacket in XL in dark blue. Please put the jacket in its bag with the receipt and call 011 556 7788; our driver will collect it on Thursday and bring the new one. There is no extra cost. Best regards, Beit Store Customer Service

5.6  She's wearing a blue jacket
 1   Get ready
We SAY… | We WRITE…
He's wearing a dark suit. | Please wear comfortable shoes.
What are you wearing? | He's quite tall, about fifty.
I'm carrying a black suitcase. | He's carrying a laptop bag.
I can't see you! | You can't miss him!
b  wear: a jacket, glasses, a scarf · carry: a bag, an umbrella, a box
 2   Listen and read
a  B (she's meeting a visitor)   b  1 A grey jacket, jeans, glasses and a red scarf.  2 A black suitcase and a laptop bag.
Language box – Practise:  1 wears  2 is wearing  3 'm carrying  4 wears
 3   Practise – sample with card A
A | Hello? I'm at the station, but I can't see you!
B | I'm just outside the main door. What are you wearing?
A | I'm wearing a black coat and a white scarf. I'm carrying a big bag and a plant!
B | A plant? Then I can find you easily. Are you quite short, with long dark hair?
A | That's me!
 3R   Perform
Card A: short, long dark hair, black coat, white scarf, big bag + a plant. Card B: tall, glasses, short grey beard, brown jacket, dark trousers, suitcase + umbrella.
 4   Your turn – sample
Fadi | This person is wearing a green jumper and dark jeans. They're carrying a red notebook.
Hiba | Is it Tareq?
Fadi | Yes! And you? What do you usually wear to college?
Hiba | I usually wear a long skirt and a jacket, but today I'm wearing jeans because I have no classes – only our English lesson!
 4R   Report – sample
"Hiba usually wears a long skirt and a jacket to college, but today she's wearing jeans because she has no classes – only our English lesson!"
 5   Read
1 About fifty.  2 His bright red scarf.  3 Tea with no sugar.
 6   Write with help – sample
Rana – I'm at the station. My aunt's train arrives at four and we'll come straight to the office.   She is quite short, about sixty-five, with grey hair and glasses. She is wearing a long brown coat and a green scarf. She is carrying a small suitcase and a bag of sweets for everyone!   Please tell Mr Khaled we'll be there at five. Can you put the kettle on? She drinks tea with a lot of sugar.   Thanks! Lina
 6P   Check together – sample
Checklist | ✓ / ✗
Capitals and full stops | ✓
is wearing / is carrying | ✗ – She wearing a coat → She is wearing a coat
Adjective order | ✓
All parts | ✓
 6R   Share – sample
Before: She wearing a long coat brown.  →  After: She is wearing a long brown coat.
 7   Write freely – sample (≈65 words)
Hi Sami, thanks for collecting my brother from the airport tomorrow! His plane lands at 2.15.He's quite tall, about thirty, with short black hair and a short beard. He's wearing a dark blue jacket, a white shirt and jeans, and he's carrying a big grey suitcase and a guitar case – you can't miss the guitar!His phone doesn't work in Syria yet, so please wait near Gate 2. He doesn't speak much English, so speak Arabic with him!Thanks again, Maya
 7P   Respond – sample slip
★ I can find him – the guitar is a great detail!   ✓ Can you add what time I should be there?   ? How tall exactly – taller than me?
 7R   Share – sample
Drawings usually differ on hair and the bag – the clearest messages give one "you can't miss…" detail.
 HW   Homework – sample reply
I found him! He was next to Gate 2 with the guitar, exactly as you said. His suitcase is very heavy – what's in it? We're on our way to your flat now. See you in thirty minutes!

5.7  Review & Mediate: How do I order online?
 1   Get ready – quiz
1 These shoes are too small.  2 My phone is cheaper than yours.  3 It's the biggest shop in town.  4 Is this bag yours?  5 The water isn't hot enough.  6 Today she's wearing jeans.
 2   Listen and read
b  She doesn't want to put her card number on the internet.
 3   Practise – sample answers
Set 1: Click on the product, then choose your size and colour. · Click the basket icon at the top right – you can see everything there and change it. · Standard delivery is 25,000 and takes three to five days; fast delivery is 60,000 and takes 24 hours. · Yes – standard delivery is free for orders over 500,000.
Set 2: Yes, choose "cash on delivery" and pay the driver. · It costs 10,000 more. · You can return it in fourteen days if you haven't used it. · Keep the order number from the confirmation message.
 3R   Perform
Listeners write the four answers; the class checks them against the page.
 4   Your turn – sample
Tareq | This is the Yalla app. First you choose the restaurant, then you add the food to your basket.
Hiba | How do I pay? Is it safe?
Tareq | You can pay in cash when the driver comes – you don't have to use a card.
Hiba | How much is the delivery?
Tareq | About fifteen thousand. And you can follow the driver on the map.
Hiba | What if the food is cold?
Tareq | Call the restaurant – the number is in the app.
 4R   Report – mediation sample
"Tareq explained the Yalla delivery app. First you choose the restaurant, then you add food to the basket. You can pay in cash. The delivery costs about fifteen thousand, and you can follow the driver on the map."
 5   Read – A2 Key Reading Part 5
1 most  2 than  3 of  4 than  5 are  6 best
 6   Write with help – sample
Hi Ziad,   It's easy to order from Beit Store! First, click on the product and choose your size and colour, then click "Add to basket". Then click the basket at the top right and check your items. After that, write your name, phone number and address and choose your delivery: standard takes three to five days and costs 25,000, or fast comes in 24 hours for 60,000. Don't worry about your card – you can pay cash to the driver, but it costs 10,000 more. Finally, click "Confirm order" and keep the order number. If something is wrong, you can return it in fourteen days, but call them first.   Lina
 6P   Check together – sample
Checklist | ✓ / ✗
Steps in order with imperatives | ✓
Both delivery options | ✗ – add the price of fast delivery
Answer to his worry | ✓
Returns information | ✓
 7   Write freely – A2 Key Writing Part 6 sample (≈45 words)
Dear Sir or Madam,I bought a black winter jacket from your website on 5 October. There is a hole in the left pocket and the zip doesn't work. I would like to return it and receive a new one, or have my money back. My order number is 7788.Yours faithfully,Hiba Khalil
Marking note: all three content points (what and when, the problem, what you want) must be present for full content marks.
 ✓   Unit 5 check
A  1 c  2 b  3 b  4 a  5 b  6 b  7 c  8 a
B  1 B  2 B  3 C  4 B  5 C
C  Sample message (≈50 words):
Hi Lama! If you need clothes, go to Moda Clothes in Al-Hamra Centre. It isn't the cheapest shop, but the quality is much better than the market and the staff are the friendliest in the centre. My tip: go on Sunday morning – Thursday evening is the busiest time. Maya
Scoring guide: A = 8 marks, B = 5 marks, C = 5 marks (content 2, organisation 1, language 2). Total 18. 13+ = ready for Unit 6.

Answer Key · Unit 6  Getting Around
Proactive English · A2 · Book 1 · Syria Edition
Every task has an answer or a full sample answer. Samples show one good A2 answer; learners' own answers will differ.
6.1  How do I get to the university?
 1   Get ready
We SAY… | We WRITE…
I go to work by minibus. | Take the number 5 bus.
Does this bus go to…? | Get off at the pharmacy.
How much is it? | Journey: about 25 minutes
It's a long way on foot. | The traffic is terrible in the morning.
b  Students' own answers. Typical: the service (minibus) is the cheapest; a taxi is the fastest (but the service can be faster in heavy traffic).
 2   Listen and read
a  B (to the Old City)   b  1 Two thousand pounds.  2 Because it's about an hour on foot and it's very hot.
Language box – Practise:  1 by  2 to  3 on  4 –  5 by
 3   Practise – sample with card A
A | Excuse me, does this bus go to the airport?
B | No, it doesn't. This one goes to the centre. You need the airport bus from the garage.
A | Where do I get it?
B | At the garage – platform one.
A | How much is it? And how long does it take?
B | Fifteen thousand. About forty-five minutes. A taxi is a hundred thousand, but it's faster.
A | Thank you so much!
 3R   Perform
Card A: airport bus, 15,000, 45 minutes. Card B: number 7 bus, 2,000, 20 minutes (or 30 minutes on foot). Typical board-clinic errors: I go with bus → by bus; How I get to…? → How do I get to…?
 4   Your turn – sample
"I walk for ten minutes to the main street. Then I take the Mezzeh service – it costs 2,000 and takes about twenty minutes. I get off at the pharmacy and walk for five minutes to the centre. The whole journey takes about forty minutes."
 4R   Report – sample
"Hiba walks for ten minutes to the main street. Then she takes the Mezzeh service – it costs two thousand. She gets off at the pharmacy and walks for five minutes. It takes about forty minutes."
 5   Read
1 From the first platform (at the bus garage).  2 Because in the morning the journey can take 40 minutes instead of 25.  3 A taxi from the garage – about 35,000, 15 minutes.
 6   Write with help – sample
Hi Lana! Here's how to get to my flat from the bus garage.   Take the Jaramana service – it leaves from the third platform and costs 2,500. The journey takes about 30 minutes, but in the evening it can take 50, so leave early.   Get off at the Al-Zahra bakery. Then walk down the small street for about three minutes. My building is on the right, next to a pharmacy. I'm on the second floor.   If it's late, a taxi is about 40,000 and takes 20 minutes.   See you on Friday! Hiba
 6P   Check together – sample
Checklist | ✓ / ✗
Capitals and full stops | ✓
by bus / on foot | ✗ – with the service → by service / take the service
Instructions start with a verb | ✓
All parts | ✓
 6R   Share – sample
Before: You go with the service to Jaramana.  →  After: Take the Jaramana service.
 7   Write freely – sample (≈85 words)
Hi Khaled! I'm so happy you're coming on Thursday. Here's how to get to my house from the Damascus garage.Take the Qudsaya service – it leaves from platform four and costs 3,000. Tell the driver you want the last stop. The journey takes about 35 minutes, but after 3 pm the highway is very slow, so try to come earlier.Get off at the small square with the fountain. Then walk up the hill for five minutes. My house is the one with the blue gate, opposite a bakery.If you arrive late, take a taxi (about 60,000) and call me. See you soon! Tareq
 7P   Respond – sample slip
★ Very clear – I like the blue gate!   ✓ Can you add the price of the taxi from the airport?   ? What if I arrive at night – is there a service after 9 pm?
 7R   Share – sample
Listeners agree the clearest messages give a landmark and a price for each option.
 HW   Homework – sample reply
Thanks, Tareq! My bus arrives at the garage at 2.15, so I'll take the service before the traffic. One question: do the services go every ten minutes, or do I have to wait? If I get lost at the square, can you come and find me?

6.2  A return to Aleppo, please
 1   Get ready
We SAY… | We WRITE…
A return to Homs, please. | Ticket: Damascus – Homs · 20,000
Single or return? | Seat 14 · Platform 3
Can I change the time? | Please arrive 20 minutes early.
Is there a place for luggage? | One suitcase free
b  single – one way only · return – there and back · platform – where the bus waits · seat number – the number of your place · luggage – a bag or suitcase
 2   Listen and read
a  B (tomorrow morning, the nine o'clock bus)   b  1 A return.  2 Eighty thousand.
Language box – Practise (order):  1 Where are you travelling to?  2 Latakia, please.  3 Single or return?  4 A return, please.  5 Here's your ticket. Platform 2.  6 Thank you!
 3   Practise – sample with customer card A
A | Good morning. Where are you travelling to?
B | Latakia, please. Have you got a bus tomorrow afternoon?
A | Yes, we have buses at two and at five.
B | The two o'clock one, please. How much is a return?
A | A single is thirty thousand and a return is fifty-five.
B | A return, then. I'm coming back on Friday. Is there a place for luggage?
A | Yes, under the bus. Here's your ticket – seat 9, platform 2.
B | Thank you!
 3R   Perform
Card A: Latakia, 14:00, return, 55,000. Card B: Homs, 07:00 (the earliest), single, 20,000; journey about 2 hours.
 4   Your turn – sample
Tareq | A return to Suwayda, please, for Friday morning.
Clerk | I'm sorry, the eight o'clock bus is full. There are seats on the two o'clock.
Tareq | OK, the two o'clock, then. How much is it?
Clerk | Sixty thousand return. When are you coming back?
Tareq | Sunday evening, please.
 4R   Report – sample
"Tareq wants to go to Suwayda for his cousin's wedding. He bought a return for sixty thousand. The bus leaves at eight on Friday and comes back on Sunday evening. There were no seats on the morning bus, so he takes the afternoon one."
 5   Read
1 Seat 22.  2 5,000 SYP.  3 You don't get your money back (no refunds after the bus leaves).
 6   Write with help – sample
SHAM AIR – BOOKING CONFIRMED   Passenger: Hiba Khalil   Booking number: SA-9032   Out: Damascus → Cairo · Monday 3 November · 07:40 · Gate 6 · Seat 18A   Back: Cairo → Damascus · Sunday 9 November · 21:00 · Seat 12C   Price: 2,400,000 SYP – PAID   Please arrive at the airport three hours early. One suitcase of 23 kg is free; extra luggage costs 300,000. To change your ticket, call 011 998 7766 at least two days before.
 6P   Check together – sample
Checklist | ✓ / ✗
Capitals (days, months) and full stops | ✗ – monday 3 november → Monday 3 November
Short lines + full sentences for rules | ✓
Dates and times clear | ✓
All parts | ✓
 6R   Share – sample
Before: out: damascus to cairo monday 3 november  →  After: Out: Damascus → Cairo · Monday 3 November · 07:40
 7   Write freely – sample (≈60 words)
AL-AMIR TRAVEL – BOOKING CONFIRMEDPassenger: Tareq Mansour   Booking number: AM-5512Out: Damascus → Suwayda · Friday 24 October · 14:00 · Platform 1 · Seat 7Back: Suwayda → Damascus · Sunday 26 October · 18:30 · Seat 15Price: 60,000 SYP (return) – PAIDPlease arrive 20 minutes before the bus leaves.  HW   Homework – sample message
Dear Al-Amir Travel, My booking number is AM-5512 (Damascus–Suwayda, Friday 24 October at 14:00). I have a meeting on Friday afternoon, so I would like to change my ticket to the Saturday morning bus. Is there a seat at 8 am? My phone number is 0947 220 518. Thank you. Tareq Mansour

6.3  How long does it take?
 1   Get ready
We SAY… | We WRITE…
How long does it take? | Journey time: about 2 hours
How far is it? | Distance: 130 km
How often do the buses go? | Every hour
What time does it leave? | Departure 08:00 · Arrival 10:00
b  How often…? – every 20 minutes · How far…? – 45 kilometres · How long…? – 90 minutes · How much…? – 30,000 SYP
 2   Listen and read
a  C (Bosra)   b  1 About 130 kilometres.  2 Two or three hours is enough.
Language box – Practise (sample questions):  1 How long does it take?  2 How far is it?  3 How often do the buses go?  4 What time does it arrive?  5 How much does it cost?
 3   Practise – sample with trip card A
A | I'd like to visit Maaloula. How far is it?
B | About fifty-six kilometres, in the north.
A | How long does it take?
B | About an hour by car.
A | How often do the buses go?
B | Every two hours.
A | And how much does it cost?
B | Fifteen thousand for a single.
 3R   Perform
Card A: 56 km, 1 hour, 15,000 single. Card B: 160 km, 2.5 hours, taxi 250,000 for the day.
 4   Your turn – sample
"We want to go to Maaloula. It's fifty-six kilometres from Damascus and it takes about an hour by car. The buses go every two hours and a single is fifteen thousand. Let's leave at eight and stay three hours, so we're back at two."
 4R   Report – sample
"We want to go to Maaloula. It's fifty-six kilometres from Damascus and it takes about an hour by car. We're leaving at eight and staying three hours. It costs about fifteen thousand each by bus."
 5   Read
1 About twelve kilometres.  2 Once (she changes to a second service).  3 Because she leaves at four (there is less traffic).
 6   Write with help – sample
My journey to college   I live in Dummar and I study in the centre. It's about eight kilometres and it takes me fifty minutes.   First, I walk for five minutes to the bus stop. Then I take the number 11 bus – it costs 2,000 and takes thirty minutes. After that, I walk through the park. Finally, I arrive at the gate.   I usually leave at seven and arrive at ten to eight. On rainy days I take a taxi because the bus is full.
 6P   Check together – sample
Checklist | ✓ / ✗
Capitals and full stops | ✓
It takes (me)… | ✗ – It takes to me → It takes me
leave / arrive (in / at) | ✓
All parts | ✓
 6R   Share – sample
Before: It takes to me fifty minutes.  →  After: It takes me fifty minutes.
 7   Write freely – sample (≈90 words)
My journey to workI live in Jaramana and I work at a bank in Baramkeh. It's only nine kilometres, but it takes me more than an hour in the morning.First, I walk for eight minutes to the main road. Then I take a service to the Abbasid Square – it costs 2,500 and takes about twenty-five minutes, but after 7.30 it can take forty-five. After that, I change and take a second service to Baramkeh. Finally, I walk for four minutes to the bank.I leave home at seven and arrive at ten past eight. The journey home is faster, but on Thursdays everybody travels, so I read a book and wait!
 7P   Respond – sample slip
★ I didn't know Jaramana was so far in the morning!   ✓ Can you add how much you pay every month?   ? Why don't you leave at half past six?
 7R   Share – sample
Longest journey (sample): 70 minutes each way; monthly cost about 100,000 SYP.
 HW   Homework – sample reply
My journey is shorter than yours – about twenty-five minutes – but I change twice, so it isn't easy. My advice: leave at half past six for one week and see the difference. I did it last year and I arrived relaxed every day!

6.4  I take the bus because it's cheaper
 1   Get ready
We SAY… | We WRITE…
I take the bus because it's cheaper. | I walk to save money and keep fit.
Why do you drive? | There isn't enough parking.
Because the traffic is terrible. | Petrol is very expensive.
To take my daughter to school. | So I need the car.
b  Students' own answers. Typical: I take a taxi when it's late / when I have heavy bags. · I never drive in the centre because there's no parking. · I walk to get some exercise.
 2   Listen and read
a  B (Nadia)   b  1 Because the service is much cheaper (and faster at that time).  2 To take her daughter to school first – the school is in the other direction.
Language box – Practise:  1 I walk to work to save money.  2 I don't drive in the centre because there's no parking.  3 It was raining, so we took a taxi.  4 She studies English to get a better job.  5 I like the train because it's comfortable.
 3   Practise – sample with card A
A | Why do you walk to work?
B | To save money – and because the traffic is terrible in the morning.
A | Really? And why don't you take the service?
B | Because it's always full at eight o'clock.
A | That's true. I get up early to go to the gym before work.
 3R   Perform
Listeners should find two because reasons and two to purposes, e.g. because there's no parking / to save money.
 4   Your turn – sample
Hiba | Why do you come to this class?
Tareq | To get a better job at my bank. We work with foreign companies.
Hiba | Why do you take the service?
Tareq | Because parking in the centre is impossible.
Hiba | What's the best transport in Damascus?
Tareq | The service, because it's cheap and it goes everywhere.
 4R   Report – sample
"Tareq comes to this class to get a better job at his bank. He takes the service because parking in the centre is impossible. He thinks the best transport in Damascus is the service because it's cheap and it goes everywhere."
 5   Read
1 Parking – there isn't enough space near her pharmacy.  2 To get some exercise and to think.  3 Families and people who work far away.
 6   Write with help – sample
Why I walk to work   I walk to work every day, and my colleagues always ask me why.   The first reason is the traffic. The buses are full at eight, so I wait twenty minutes for a place. The second reason is money: a service twice a day costs a lot every month.   But the most important reason is my health. I walk because I sit at a desk all day. And I walk to start my morning slowly and think about my work.   Of course, in the rain it isn't fun. But for me, thirty minutes on foot is the best part of my day!
 6P   Check together – sample
Checklist | ✓ / ✗
Capitals and full stops | ✓
because + subject + verb; to + base verb | ✗ – for save money → to save money
Reasons in order | ✓
All parts | ✓
 6R   Share – sample
Before: I walk for save money.  →  After: I walk to save money.
 7   Write freely – sample (≈95 words)
Why I'm learning English at thirty-fivePeople sometimes ask me why I study English now, with a full-time job and two children.The first reason is my work. I'm an IT technician, and all the manuals and videos are in English. I read them slowly with a dictionary, so I lose a lot of time.The second reason is my children. My daughter asks me English questions and I want to help her.But the most important reason is for me. I want to understand films without subtitles and talk to people from other countries.Of course, it isn't easy after a long day. But every lesson I understand a little more – and that feeling is worth it.
 7P   Respond – sample slip
★ Good reasons – especially the children!   ✓ Can you add an example of a difficult manual?   ? How many hours do you study every week?
 7R   Share – sample
Strongest reason (class vote, sample): learning English to help your children.
 HW   Homework – sample comment
I agree with you about work. I'm a nurse and the medicine information is always in English, so I study for the same reason. But for me the most important thing is travelling – I want to go to Jordan and talk to people without my brother!

6.5  Why don't we go on Friday?
 1   Get ready
We SAY… | We WRITE…
Let's go to the park! | How about Friday morning?
Shall we take the bus? | Meeting at the main gate at 10.
Why don't we leave early? | Bring a hat – it's sunny.
Good idea! | Sorry, I can't come – I'm working.
b  Yes: Good idea! · Why not? · Let's do that. · That sounds great!   No / not sure: I'm not sure. · I'd rather not.
 2   Listen and read
a  B (Maaloula)   b  1 Because Saturday is a working day for Lina.  2 In Omar's car (four people, one car – cheaper).
Language box – Practise:  1 Let's go to the cinema tonight.  2 Shall we take the bus?  3 Why don't we meet at six?  4 How about going by taxi?  5 Let's leave early.
 3   Practise – sample with card A
A | Shall we take him to the Old City?
B | Hmm, I'm not sure – it's very hot at midday. How about going to the museum first?
A | Good idea. Let's meet at nine, then.
B | Why don't we meet at half past nine? The museum opens at ten.
A | Nine is better for me, I'm afraid – I have to be home early. Shall we say a quarter past nine?
B | Perfect. And let's take some water with us.
A | Deal!
 3R   Perform
All six phrases appear if the pair uses a different form each time and disagrees once.

6.6  You can't park here
 1   Get ready
We SAY… | We WRITE…
You can't park here. | NO PARKING
What does that sign mean? | KEEP OFF THE GRASS
You must leave your bag at the desk. | Please leave bags at the desk.
It's not allowed. | No smoking. Thank you!
b  NO ENTRY – you can't go in · NO SMOKING – you mustn't smoke · KEEP OFF THE GRASS – you mustn't walk on the grass · QUIET PLEASE – you must be quiet · EXIT – the way out · NO PHOTOS – you can't take photos
 2   Listen and read
a  B (at a museum)   b  1 Take photos (in that room) and eat near the old stones.  2 He must leave it at the desk with the guard.
Language box – Practise:  1 can't  2 can  3 must  4 mustn't  5 Can
 3   Practise – sample with signs card A
A | What does that sign mean?
B | It means "keep off the grass". You can't walk or sit there.
A | I see. Can I have a picnic in the park?
B | Yes, you can, but only on the stones near the trees.
A | And this one?
B | You must leave the park at sunset – it closes then. And you mustn't bring your bicycle inside.
 3R   Perform
Card A – can: have a picnic on the stones, use the bins; mustn't: walk on the grass, bring bicycles. Card B – can: sit anywhere if the bus is empty, keep your ticket; mustn't: eat, drink, talk to the driver.
 4   Your turn – sample
"At my university you must show your student card at the gate. You can eat in the garden, but you mustn't eat in the library. You can't use your phone in an exam, and you must arrive before the teacher – after that the door is closed!"
b  Sample class rules: We must speak English in pair work. · You can use your phone to check a word, but you mustn't answer calls. · If you arrive late, you must bring sweets for everyone!
 4R   Report – sample
"At Hiba's university you must show your student card at the gate. You can eat in the garden, but you mustn't eat in the library. And you can't use your phone in an exam – of course!"
 5   Read
1 Because the driver can't always give change.  2 Children under six.  3 You mustn't eat or drink in the minibus, and you mustn't smoke.
 6   Write with help – sample
AL-YASMIN LANGUAGE CENTRE – CLASSROOM RULES   • Please arrive five minutes early.   • You must bring your workbook to every lesson.   • Please speak English in pair work – it's why we are here!   • You mustn't eat in the classroom, but you can drink water or tea.   • Students can use their phones to check a word, but they must put them away in tests.   • Thank you for helping us keep the room clean!

Friendliest notice: one that says Please… and gives a reason. Strictest: one with four or more mustn't lines.

6.7  Review & Mediate: What did they say?
 1   Get ready – quiz
1 I go to work by bus.  2 How long does it take?  3 I walk to save money.  4 How about going on Friday?  5 You must show your ticket.  6 The bus arrives in Aleppo at twelve.
 2   Listen and read
b  His bus (the 12:30 to Aleppo) is delayed – it now leaves at 13:15.
 3   Practise – sample
B (Latakia) | My bus is at one o'clock from platform two. Is that right?
A | The time is the same – one o'clock – but the platform is different now. It leaves from platform seven, not two.
B | Oh! Where is platform seven?
A | At the end, next to the café. You have twenty minutes, so you're fine.
Suwayda traveller: "I'm sorry – your bus is cancelled. There's no bus at two o'clock. The next one is at four. You must wait two hours, or take a service to Daraa and change."
 3R   Perform
Latakia: 13:00, platform 7 (changed from 2). Suwayda: 14:00 cancelled, next bus 16:00.
 4   Your turn – sample

Scoring guide: A = 8 marks, B = 5 marks, C = 5 marks (content 2, organisation 1, language 2). Total 18. 13+ = ready for the Mid-Book Test.

MID-BOOK TEST  ·  Answer Key, Audio Scripts and Marking
Proactive English · A2 · Book 1 · Syria Edition · Units 1–6
For the teacherBefore the test: print one copy of the test for each student. Do not give out this key. Play each listening recording twice, with a 15-second pause between the two plays and 30 seconds at the end of each part.If you have no recordings: read the audio scripts in this key aloud, twice, at a slow natural speed. For Part 1, pause for 10 seconds after each conversation.Timing: Reading 30 min · Writing 20 min · Listening about 15 min · Speaking in pairs, about 5 minutes per pair (while the others do the Reading paper).Total: 60 marks – Reading 20 · Writing 15 · Listening 15 · Speaking 10.
PAPER 1 · READING  (20 marks – 1 mark per question)
Part | Answers
Part 1 (6) | 1 B · 2 A · 3 A · 4 C · 5 B · 6 A
Part 2 (5) | 7 A (Samira) · 8 B (Jamal) · 9 C (Hiba) · 10 B (Jamal) · 11 C (Hiba)
Part 3 (5) | 12 B · 13 C · 14 A · 15 B · 16 C
Part 4 (4) | 17 so · 18 off · 19 on · 20 on
Part 4 note: accept only these words. "so" (not "because"), "off" (not "down"), "on the left" (not "in"), "on Sunday" (not "in"/"at").

PAPER 2 · WRITING  (15 marks)
Part 1 – message to the teacher (5 marks)
Marking: Content 3 (one mark for each of: reason for being late · arrival time · a question about the lesson) · Language 2 (0 = very hard to understand; 1 = mistakes but clear; 2 = good control of Unit 1–6 language). Under 25 words: maximum 3 marks.
Sample answer (28 words):
Dear Ms Huda, I'm sorry, but I'm late today because my bus is delayed. I think I'll arrive at a quarter past six. Are we doing the listening first? Tareq
Part 2 – email to Anna (10 marks)
Marking: Content 5 (invitation 2 · what food 1 · how to travel 2) · Organisation 2 (greeting, order of ideas, ending) · Language 3 (range and accuracy of Unit 1–6 language: Would you like to…?, food words, transport, It takes…). Under 35 words: maximum 6 marks.
Sample answer (62 words):
Hi Anna,Would you like to come to my home for dinner on Thursday at seven? My mother and I are making fattoush and grilled chicken with rice, and my sister is making a milk pudding.From your hotel, take the Mezzeh service from the main street. It costs 2,000 and takes about twenty minutes. Get off at the big pharmacy and call me – I'll meet you there.Please tell me if there is any food you don't eat!See you on Thursday,Hiba
A weaker answer that still passes (38 words) – 6/10:
Hi Anna. Come to my house Thursday. We make fattoush and chicken. It is very good food. You take the service from your hotel, it is 2000 and take 20 minute. Get off at pharmacy. See you. Hiba
Why 6: all three content points are there (5/5 content), but the organisation is thin (1/2 – no greeting line or proper ending) and there are tense, article and plural mistakes (0/3 → 1/3 as the message is still clear). Show students this answer and ask them to improve it.

PAPER 3 · LISTENING  (15 marks – 1 mark per question)
Part | Answers
Part 1 (5) | 21 A · 22 B · 23 C · 24 B · 25 C
Part 2 (5) | 26 third / 3rd · 27 two / 2 · 28 cooker · 29 900,000 · 30 four / 4
Part 3 (5) | 31 A · 32 B · 33 B · 34 A · 35 C
Part 2 note: accept numbers written as figures or words. Accept "3rd floor" for 26 and "900000" or "900 thousand" for 29. Do not accept "cook" for 28.
🔊 Track MT-1 · Audio script (Listening Part 1)
**21**  Man | You're not eating anything!
        Woman | I had a big breakfast – bread, cheese and some olives. I never drink coffee in the morning, only tea.
**22**  Woman | Do you drive to the office?
        Man | I've got a car, but I leave it at home. There's no parking in the centre, so I take the service. It's cheaper too.
**23**  Customer | What time do you close today?
        Assistant | Usually at eight, but on Thursday we stay open until nine because it's our busiest day.
**24**  Clerk | Where are you travelling to?
        Woman | Homs, please. A return. How much is it?
        Clerk | A single is twenty thousand and a return is thirty-five.
**25**  Assistant | What exactly is the problem with the shoes?
        Man | They fit well and the colour is fine, but look – there's a hole in the left one. I bought them last week!
🔊 Track MT-2 · Audio script (Listening Part 2)
Woman | Hello, I'm calling about the flat in Baramkeh. Can you tell me about it?
Owner | Of course. It's on the third floor – I'm sorry, there isn't a lift.
Woman | That's OK. How many bedrooms are there?
Owner | There are two bedrooms, a living room and a kitchen. In the kitchen there's a fridge and a cooker, but there isn't a washing machine. There's a laundry next to the building.
Woman | And is there a balcony?
Owner | Yes, a small one, with some plants.
Woman | How much is the rent?
Owner | Nine hundred thousand a month, and water is included.
Woman | Can I see it tomorrow?
Owner | Yes, but please call after four o'clock – I work in the morning.
Woman | After four. Thank you very much!
🔊 Track MT-3 · Audio script (Listening Part 3)
Tareq | My name's Tareq and I work in a bank in Baramkeh. I live in Jaramana, and my journey is the hardest part of my day! It's only nine kilometres, but in the morning it takes me more than an hour. I leave home at seven and I arrive at about ten past eight.
 | I have a car, but I never drive to the bank. There's no parking near it, and I don't want to spend twenty minutes looking for a space every morning. So I take two services and I walk for about ten minutes.
 | Some people hate the service, but I like it. I always take a book with me, so I read for half an hour. That's the only time in the day when nobody asks me questions!
 | On Fridays I don't work. My wife and I take the children to the park near our house, and in the afternoon we visit my parents. Well – usually. Sometimes I sleep!

PAPER 4 · SPEAKING  (10 marks)
How to run it: take two students at a time. Part 1: ask each student three or four questions from the list (about 2 minutes). Part 2: give the task, then let the pair talk – don't interrupt (about 2 minutes). Mark with the grid in the test paper.
Part 1 – sample answers (what a good A2 answer sounds like)
Teacher | Where do you live? Tell me about your neighbourhood.
Student | I live in Jaramana, in the east of Damascus. It's a busy area with a lot of shops. There's a big bakery on my street and a park near my building, but the traffic is terrible in the evening.
Teacher | How do you come to this class?
Student | I take a service from the main street. It costs two thousand and it takes about half an hour, but in the morning it can take fifty minutes. Then I walk for five minutes.
Teacher | Tell me about your family.
Student | I've got a big family. My parents live in Homs and I've got two brothers and one sister. My sister is a teacher and she's got two children. I visit them every month.
Note the pattern: answer + one extra detail. A student who only says "Jaramana" or "By service" cannot get more than 2–3 marks for communication.
Part 2 – sample conversation (two students)
A | So, my friend is coming for one day. Shall we take him to the Old City?
B | Good idea. How about going in the morning? It's too hot at two o'clock.
A | OK, let's meet at nine. Why don't we take a service? It's cheaper than a taxi.
B | Hmm, I'm not sure. Three people with bags… a taxi is faster and it isn't very expensive.
A | That's true. A taxi, then. And after the Old City, how about lunch at Beit Al-Yasmin? The fattoush there is the best in Damascus.
B | Perfect. Then we can walk to the museum because it's near the restaurant.
A | So: a taxi at nine, the Old City, lunch at Beit Al-Yasmin, then the museum. Deal?
B | Deal!
This pair would score 9–10: different suggestion forms (Shall we…? How about…? Why don't we…?), a polite disagreement with a reason, comparatives and superlatives, and a clear decision at the end.
Marking summary and what to do next
Paper | Marks | What it tests (Units 1–6)
Reading | 20 | signs and messages (U2, U5, U6), people profiles (U2, U5), a longer personal text (U3), an open cloze (U6)
Writing | 15 | a short message (U1, U6) and an email with an invitation + travel instructions (U4, U6)
Listening | 15 | short conversations (U1–U5), completing notes about a flat (U3), a longer talk about a journey (U6)
Speaking | 10 | personal information and routines (U1, U2, U3, U6) and making suggestions together (U6)

What to do after the test42–60 (70%+): ready for Unit 7. Give back the papers and go through only the questions that more than half the class got wrong.30–41: ready for Unit 7, but spend one lesson on the weakest paper first. Most common weak areas at this point: the present continuous vs present simple (U2, U4), comparatives (U5), and too / not … enough (U5).Under 30: do a review week before Unit 7. Use the "I can…" lists from Units 1–6: the student ticks 😐 items, then you re-teach those cycles' Language boxes and re-run their Stage 4 personalised tasks.For every student: put the Writing papers in their portfolio with the Unit 1–6 free-writing texts. Progress in writing is the clearest evidence of progress at A2.

Answer Key · Unit 7  Free Time & Arrangements
Proactive English · A2 · Book 1 · Syria Edition
Every task has an answer or a full sample answer. Samples show one good A2 answer; learners' own answers will differ.
7.1  I'm quite good at photography
 1   Get ready

b  Students' own answers, e.g. I can cook very well. I can't swim at all. I'm good at fixing things. I'm not very good at singing.
 2   Listen and read
a  B (photography)   b  1 He could draw quite well.  2 Taking photos of people.
Language box – Practise:  1 can  2 couldn't  3 good at  4 Can … can't  5 can't
 3   Practise – sample with card A
A | Three clubs this term: photography, cooking and football. Which one for you?
B | I'm not sure. I can cook quite well, so maybe the cooking club isn't for me.
A | Then come to the football club!
B | But I can't swim at all – is that a problem?
A | It's football, not swimming! Are you good at running?
B | Well… when I was at school I could run very fast.
A | That helps! So, football?
B | OK, you win!
 3R   Perform
Card A: can cook / could run fast at school. Card B: can drive / could sing in the school choir. Typical board-clinic errors: I can to cook → I can cook; good in → good at.
