That's a shame. | I hope the day is beautiful.
Maybe next time. | With best wishes,
b  Good: "I'd love to, but I can't." · "I'm really sorry – I'm away that weekend."   Bad: "No." · "I'm busy." · "Maybe." (These sound cold and the person doesn't know if you're coming.)
 2   Listen and read
a  A (a wedding)   b  1 His brother is arriving from Dubai that weekend.  2 To take the photos at the engagement party.
Language box – Practise (sample answers):  1 Thank you so much! I'd love to, but I'm afraid I can't – I'm travelling that weekend.  2 I'd love to, but I'm afraid I can't. I have my English class on Thursday.  3 I'm really sorry, I can't play on Friday because my knee hurts.  4 Thank you for inviting me! I'm afraid I can't – I'm working on Saturday.
 3   Practise – sample
A | My sister's graduation party is on Saturday. Would you like to come?
B | Oh, thank you so much! I'd love to, but I'm afraid I can't. I'm working on Saturday until eight.
A | Oh, that's a shame.
B | I'm really sorry. I was looking forward to meeting your family.
A | Don't worry. Maybe next time.
B | Listen, can I bring a cake on Sunday instead?
A | That's very kind of you!
 3R   Perform
All four steps must appear: thank · say no · reason · keep the friendship (an offer, good wishes, or "maybe next time").
 4   Your turn – sample refusals
"Thank you so much for inviting me! I'd love to come, but I'm afraid I can't – my parents are arriving that day. I hope you all have a wonderful time.""That's very kind of you! I'm really sorry, but I can't: I'm working every evening this week. Can we do something next weekend instead?"
 4R   Report – sample
"I invited six people to my barbecue. Hiba and Fadi are coming. Tareq can't come because he's visiting his parents in Suwayda, and Rasha is working. Everybody was very polite – nobody just said no!"
 5   Read
1 On the 24th.  2 That the day is beautiful and that everyone dances until the morning.  3 To take the photos at the engagement party next month.
 6   Write with help – sample
Dear Nour,   Thank you very much for inviting me to your son's graduation party on the 12th. It was very kind of you to think of me.   I'm really sorry, but I'm afraid I can't come. I'm travelling to Aleppo that weekend for a family wedding. I was looking forward to celebrating with you all.   I hope the party is wonderful. Please tell Yazan that I'm very proud of him.   If it's useful, I'd be happy to help with the food the day before.   With best wishes,   Rasha
 6P   Check together – sample
Checklist | ✓ / ✗
Capitals and full stops | ✓
All four steps | ✗ – no good wishes for the day
A reason (arrangement or because) | ✓
Good wishes + offer / next time | ✗ → add "I hope the party is wonderful."
 6R   Share – sample
Before: I can't come. Sorry.  →  After: I'm really sorry, but I'm afraid I can't come – I'm travelling that weekend.
 7   Write freely – sample (≈85 words)
Dear Mr Khaled,Thank you very much for inviting me to the company dinner on Thursday the 30th. It's always the best evening of the year.I'm really sorry, but I'm afraid I can't come this time. My wife is having a small operation that week and I'm staying at home with the children. I was looking forward to seeing everyone, especially the new team from Aleppo.I hope you all have a wonderful evening. Please give my best wishes to the team.If it helps, I'd be happy to prepare the photos from last year for the screen.With best wishes,Tareq Mansour
 7P   Respond – sample slip
★ What a kind letter – and a very good offer!   ✓ Can you add your phone number?   ? Can you come to the lunch in December instead?
 7R   Share – sample
Class decision: yes – the writer is still a good friend, because the letter thanks, explains, wishes well and offers something.
 HW   Homework – sample reply
Dear Tareq, Thank you for your letter – of course, family first. We'll miss you, and the photos idea is excellent: please send them by Wednesday. I hope everything goes well for your wife. We're planning a lunch in December, and that one you cannot miss! Best regards, Khaled

7.4  Can we make it seven?
 1   Get ready
We SAY… | We WRITE…
Can we make it seven? | See you at 7 outside the cinema.
Where shall we meet? | Row G, seats 11 and 12
I'll be fifteen minutes late. | Please don't wait outside.
No problem at all! | Take your time.
b  on Friday · at half past six · in the morning · at the weekend · on 24 October · at night
 2   Listen and read
a  B (Maya)   b  1 Half past eight (the late film); they meet at a quarter past eight.  2 Outside the cinema, next to the ticket office, on the first floor.
Language box – Practise:  1 at  2 in  3 on  4 outside  5 at
 3   Practise – sample with card A
B | I'm really sorry – can we change dinner to Friday?
A | Oh! Is everything OK?
B | Yes, but my meeting finishes at half past seven on Thursday.
A | No problem. Can you be there at eight on Friday?
B | Let's say half past eight, to be safe.
A | OK. Where shall we meet?
B | How about outside the restaurant, next to the bakery?
A | Perfect. See you on Friday!
 3R   Perform
Card A: Thursday 7 pm → Friday 8 or 8.30, outside the restaurant. Card B: Saturday 10 am at the library → Saturday afternoon or Sunday, at a café.
 4   Your turn – sample
Hiba | Shall we study on Sunday? How about ten o'clock at the library?
Fadi | Perfect. Outside the main door?
Hiba | (later) Fadi, I'm sorry – the library opens at eleven on Sundays. Can we make it eleven?
Fadi | No problem. Or how about the café next to the centre? It opens at nine.
Hiba | Even better. Eleven at the café, then – I'll get a table near the window.
 4R   Report – sample
"Hiba and I are meeting on Sunday to study. First we said ten o'clock at the library, but the library opens at eleven on Sundays, so we changed it. Now we're meeting at eleven in the café next to the centre."
 5   Read
1 Outside the cinema, next to the ticket office, on the first floor.  2 A delivery arrived late at the pharmacy.  3 Missing the beginning – the film starts with adverts.
 6   Write with help – samples
1 Confirming: Hi Fadi! Just confirming Sunday: the café opens at nine, so let's meet at eleven at the table near the window. Bring your notes from Unit 6.
2 Late: Fadi, I'm so sorry – my bus hasn't come. I'm leaving in five minutes, so I'll be about twenty minutes late. Please order a coffee and start without me!
3 Answer: No problem! I'm at the table near the window with two coffees. Take your time – I'm reading the exercises. 🙂
 6P   Check together – sample
Checklist | ✓ / ✗
Capitals and full stops | ✓
at / in / on correct | ✗ – in Sunday → on Sunday
The place is exact | ✓
Says how many minutes + what to do | ✓
 6R   Share – sample
Before: See you in Sunday at the café.  →  After: See you on Sunday at the café, at the table near the window.
 7   Write freely – sample (≈75 words total)
1 Hi Maya! Just confirming tomorrow: the exhibition opens at ten, so let's meet at a quarter to ten outside Dar Al-Fann, next to the blue door on Straight Street. It's free, so we don't need tickets. 🎨2 Maya, I'm so sorry – my taxi is in terrible traffic near the Citadel. I'll be about fifteen minutes late. Please don't wait outside in the sun; go in and start with the first room.3 No problem at all! I'm inside, in the second room on the left. The photos of the old souq are beautiful – take your time, I'm not going anywhere. ☕
 7P   Respond – sample slip
★ Very clear and kind!   ✓ Can you add your phone number in the first message?   ? How will I know which room you're in?
 7R   Share – sample
The kindest answer gives a reason not to worry ("the film starts with adverts", "take your time, I'm reading").
 HW   Homework – sample fourth message
Good morning! Thank you for yesterday – I really enjoyed the exhibition, and the photos of the old souq were my favourite too. Let's do it again: the Sunday cinema at Dar Al-Fann is at six and the films are old Egyptian ones. Are you free this Sunday?

7.5  I prefer the cinema to TV
 1   Get ready
We SAY… | We WRITE…
I prefer coffee to tea. | Most students prefer the café.
I'd rather walk. | I prefer sitting outside.
I'd rather not eat here. | I prefer eating a little of many things.
Which do you prefer? | For me, six o'clock is the middle of the night!
b  Students' own answers.
 2   Listen and read
a  B (where to go after the film)   b  1 Small places, sitting outside, walking, and mezze.  2 To walk to the café with the garden in Bab Touma and eat mezze.
Language box – Practise:  1 I prefer the sea to the mountains.  2 I'd rather stay at home.  3 She prefers reading to watching TV.  4 I'd rather not go out tonight.  5 Do you prefer tea or coffee?
 3   Practise – sample (a free Friday)
A | Shall we go to the shopping centre on Friday?
B | Honestly, I'd rather not spend the day inside. I prefer the Old City to the centre.
A | Me too, actually. What about the Old City in the morning?
B | Yes! I prefer going early.
A | Do you prefer walking or taking a taxi?
B | I'd rather walk – it's only twenty minutes.
A | Perfect. And a picnic or a restaurant?
B | A picnic, definitely. Let's go!
 3R   Perform
Listeners should tick all six phrases if the pair disagrees once and uses I'd rather twice.
 4   Your turn – sample
"Fadi prefers tea to coffee because coffee keeps him awake. He prefers the village to the city – his family is from a village near Hama. He'd rather read than watch television, and he prefers cooking at home to eating out because restaurants are expensive. But we're the same about the weather: we both prefer winter to summer."
 4R   Report – sample
"Fadi and I are the same about food – we both prefer eating at home to eating out. But he prefers winter to summer because he hates the heat, and I'd rather have forty degrees than cold hands!"
 5   Read
1 After midnight.  2 Getting up at six and studying on her balcony.  3 Are you a morning person or a night person? And how do you wake up?
 6   Write with help – sample
City or village?   I'm definitely a city person. I prefer living in Damascus to living in a village, and I like the noise, the shops and the cafés.   But my work is hard! I start at eight and the traffic is terrible, so I'd rather live near my office one day.   My father is the opposite. He prefers the village. He says the air is clean and everybody knows everybody. For me, that's the problem – everybody knows everybody!   So: city or village? And where do you want to live when you're sixty?
 6P   Check together – sample
Checklist | ✓ / ✗
Capitals and full stops | ✓
prefer A to B; prefer + -ing | ✗ – prefer the city than the village → …to the village
I'd rather + base verb | ✓
All parts | ✓
 6R   Share – sample
Before: I prefer the city than the village.  →  After: I prefer the city to the village.
 7   Write freely – sample (≈90 words)
Big parties or quiet evenings?I'm a quiet-evening person. I prefer sitting with two or three friends to going to a big party, and I'd rather talk than dance.But I'm Syrian and my family is enormous! Every month there's a wedding or an engagement, and nobody understands when I say I'm tired. "You're twenty-eight," my aunt says. "You can sleep when you're fifty."My sister is the opposite. She prefers a room with a hundred people, and she's always the last to leave. She says I'm "a grandmother in a young body".So: big parties or quiet evenings? And what do you say to an aunt who doesn't accept "no"?
 7P   Respond – sample comment
★ "A grandmother in a young body" made me laugh!   ✓ Can you add how many weddings you went to this year?   ? My answer: I prefer quiet evenings too – and I tell my aunt I have an exam. Always.
 7R   Share – sample
Typical class result: more night people than morning people, but more quiet-evening people than party people.
 HW   Homework – sample comment
I'm completely the opposite! I prefer big family parties to quiet evenings because I live alone and the flat is too silent. But I agree about dancing – I'd rather carry the plates than dance in front of everyone. Next wedding, we can sit together in the kitchen!

7.6  What's on this week?
 1   Get ready
We SAY… | We WRITE…

What time does it finish? | Tickets: 30,000 · Students 15,000
Do I need to book? | Every day until 30 October
Is anything else on? | Closed on Fridays
b  the first of May · the third of June · the twelfth of October · the twenty-first of December · the twenty-fourth of April
 2   Listen and read
a  A (a concert)   b  1 At half past seven (the doors open at seven).  2 Fifteen thousand.

B | Al-Jaish Stadium, good morning.
A | Good morning. I'd like to ask about the match on Friday. What time does it start?
B | The gates open at three and the match starts at four.
A | How long does it last?
B | About ninety minutes with a break.
A | And how much are the tickets?
B | Twenty-five thousand, and ten thousand for children.
A | Do I need to book?
B | No, you can buy them at the gate.
 3R   Perform
Card A: Friday 24th, 4 pm, 25,000 (children 10,000). Card B: every day 10–8 until the 31st, free, talks at 5 pm on Saturdays.

7.7  Review & Mediate: Let's plan Saturday
 1   Get ready – quiz
1 I can swim.  2 She's good at cooking.  3 What are you doing on Friday?  4 I prefer tea to coffee.  5 I'd rather stay home. (or stay at home)  6 The film starts at eight every day.
 2   Listen and read
b  The Citadel tour at ten, lunch at Beit Al-Yasmin at half past twelve, then the exhibition at Dar Al-Fann at two. They meet at the Citadel gate at a quarter to ten.
 3   Practise – sample (visitor b: two students with very little money)
A | Shall we do the Citadel tour?
B | I'd rather not – it's forty thousand, and they've got very little money.
C | Students pay twenty, but that's still a lot. How about Tishreen Park in the morning? It's free.
A | Good idea. And the exhibition at Dar Al-Fann is free too.
B | Agreed. But Dar Al-Fann is closed on Fridays, so let's go on Saturday.
C | Lunch? A restaurant is expensive…
A | Let's take food to the park. Then the exhibition at three.
B | So: the park at ten with a picnic, then the exhibition at three. We're meeting at the main gate at a quarter to ten.
 3R   Perform
Good plans: family with small children – park (playground, boats, café), avoid the two-hour tour with stairs. Students with little money – park and exhibition (both free), picnic. Older couple – exhibition and Sunday cinema, avoid the Citadel stairs; park café is fine.
 4   Your turn – sample
"In the morning we're visiting the citadel because the light is good early and it isn't hot. Then we're having lunch at my uncle's restaurant – the kebab is the best in the city. The museum is closed on Tuesdays, so in the afternoon we're going to the souq instead. In the evening we're drinking tea in a café near the clock."
 4R   Report – mediation sample
"Fadi planned my day in Aleppo. In the morning we're visiting the citadel because the light is good early. Then we're having lunch at his uncle's restaurant – he says the kebab is the best in the city. The museum is closed on Tuesdays, so we're going to the souq instead."
 5   Read – A2 Key Reading Part 2
1 C (Kareem – free in the evenings, loves old films, lives near Bab Sharqi)  2 B (Rasha – children, outside, food and toilet)  3 A (Nabil – his knee; a lot of walking and stairs)  4 A (Nabil – loves old photographs, free, small place)  5 A (Nabil – he's only free on Fridays and Dar Al-Fann is closed on Fridays)
 6   Write with help – sample
Dear Mr Lang,   We're all free on Saturday! Here's the plan. The Citadel tour starts at ten, so let's meet at a quarter to ten at the Citadel gate. The tour lasts two hours and it costs forty thousand. After that, we're having lunch at Beit Al-Yasmin at half past twelve. In the afternoon we're going to the photography exhibition at Dar Al-Fann – it's free and it's very near. Please wear comfortable shoes because there are a lot of stairs in the Citadel.   See you on Saturday!   Lina
 6P   Check together – sample
Checklist | ✓ / ✗
Present simple for the programme | ✓
Present continuous for arrangements | ✗ – we have lunch → we're having lunch
Time, meeting place and price | ✓
One piece of advice | ✓
 7   Write freely – A2 Key Writing Part 6 sample (≈50 words)
Hi Kate,There's a photography exhibition at Dar Al-Fann and it's free. It opens at ten, so let's meet at a quarter to ten outside the blue door on Straight Street. After the exhibition, which do you prefer – lunch in the Old City or a walk in the souq?See you on Saturday!Lama
Marking note: all three content points (what and when it starts, where to meet, a question about what she prefers) must be present for full content marks.
 ✓   Unit 7 check
A  1 b  2 b  3 c  4 a  5 b  6 b  7 c  8 a
B  1 Thursday  2 7.30 (half past seven)  3 15,000  4 30th  5 Friday(s)
C  Sample message (≈45 words):
Hi Fadi! There's an oud concert at Dar Al-Fann on Thursday the 23rd. It starts at half past seven and students pay only fifteen thousand. Shall we meet outside the centre at seven? Do you prefer going early for a coffee first, or arriving just before it starts?  Hiba
Scoring guide: A = 8 marks, B = 5 marks, C = 5 marks (content 2, organisation 1, language 2). Total 18. 13+ = ready for Unit 8.

Answer Key · Unit 8  The Past
Proactive English · A2 · Book 1 · Syria Edition
Every task has an answer or a full sample answer. Samples show one good A2 answer; learners' own answers will differ.
8.1  What did you do at the weekend?
 1   Get ready
We SAY… | We WRITE…
I visited my parents. | Last weekend I went to Bludan.
I cooked for twenty people! | On Friday my mother cooked for the family.
Did you rest? | I didn't clean the flat.
I took two hundred photos. | We walked in the Old City.
b  visited · watched · studied · stopped   ·   went · had · saw · ate
 2   Listen and read
a  A (to Homs)   b  1 He cooked for the photography club.  2 Because his mother cooked for twenty people and all the family came (and on Saturday he painted the kitchen).
Language box – Practise:  1 visited  2 went  3 didn't come  4 took  5 studied  6 didn't like
 3   Practise – sample with card A
A | You look tired! What did you do at the weekend?
B | I travelled to Latakia. I arrived on Thursday night and I came back yesterday.
A | Nice! Did you rest?
B | No, I didn't! On Friday I swam in the sea and ate fish with my cousins.
A | And on Saturday?
B | I helped my uncle in his shop all day. What about you?
 3R   Perform
Card A verbs: travelled, arrived, came back (irregular), swam (irr.), ate (irr.), helped. Card B: cleaned, washed, studied, watched, didn't go. Typical board-clinic errors: I didn't went → I didn't go; She goed → She went.
 4   Your turn – sample
"On Friday I went to my grandmother's house in Dummar. I helped her cook and we ate together with my cousins. In the afternoon I slept for two hours. On Saturday I studied for my exam and I washed my car. But I didn't finish the exercises and I didn't go out at all."
 4R   Report – sample
"Hiba went to her grandmother's house in Dummar on Friday. She helped her cook and they ate together. On Saturday she studied for her exam, but she didn't finish. She says she needs another weekend!"
 5   Read
1 Twelve.  2 He slept for two hours.  3 He didn't clean the flat, he didn't wash his clothes and he didn't study his English homework.
 6   Write with help – sample
Hi Mum! Sorry I didn't call on Friday – it was a busy weekend.   On Friday I worked until four and then I helped my brother with his car. On Saturday morning I cleaned the whole flat. In the afternoon I visited Abu Samir in the hospital, and in the evening I watched the match with Fadi.   I didn't study and I didn't answer my emails. But I was happy!   How was your weekend? Say hello to Dad.   Tareq
 6P   Check together – sample
Checklist | ✓ / ✗
Capitals and full stops | ✓
Past simple forms | ✗ – I helpped → I helped
didn't + base verb | ✓
All parts | ✓
 6R   Share – sample
Before: I didn't studied and I didn't answered my emails.  →  After: I didn't study and I didn't answer my emails.
 7   Write freely – sample (≈85 words)
Hi Lama! How are you? Sorry I didn't call – what a weekend.On Thursday evening my cousins arrived from Daraa with four children, so the flat was full. On Friday we cooked all morning: my mother made mansaf and I made two salads. Twelve people ate lunch here! In the afternoon we took the children to the park and I played football with them for an hour.On Saturday I slept until eleven. Then I studied for the English test.I didn't clean the kitchen, I didn't wash my clothes and I didn't finish the exercises. But it was a lovely weekend.How was yours? Hiba
 7P   Respond – sample slip
★ Twelve people for lunch – that sounds fun!   ✓ Can you add how old the children are?   ? Did you win the football match?
 7R   Share – sample
Most relaxing weekend (typical): a student who slept and watched films. Hardest work: anyone who hosted family.
 HW   Homework – sample reply
Hi Hiba! My weekend was very quiet – too quiet after your news! On Friday I studied on the balcony and in the evening Maya and I watched an old film. On Saturday I washed everything and bought food for the week. Did you make your mother's shakriyeh, or only the salads?

8.2  Where did you go?
 1   Get ready
We SAY… | We WRITE…
Where did you go? | How was your trip?
Who did you go with? | We took the Thursday bus.
How long did you stay? | We stayed four days.
Did you like it? | The journey home was terrible.
b  Where did you go? · How long did you stay? · Did you like it? · Who did you go with?
 2   Listen and read
a  A (to Latakia)   b  1 With her sister Lama.  2 The journey home – the bus didn't leave on time and it took five hours.
Language box – Practise:  1 Where did you go last summer?  2 Who did she meet?  3 Did you like the film?  4 How long did they stay?  5 What did he buy?
 3   Practise – sample with trip card A
A | Where did you go?
B | To Palmyra.
A | Who did you go with?
B | With two cousins.
A | How long did you stay?
B | Three days.
A | What did you do there?
B | We walked in the old city and we took a lot of photos. One evening we drank tea with a family there.
A | Did you swim?
B | No, I didn't – there's no sea in Palmyra! And the journey was long and hot.
 3R   Perform
The six questions are usually: Where / Who with / How long / What did you do / Did you like it / How was the journey.
 4   Your turn – sample notes
Where? | When? | Who with? | How? | How long? | Best thing? | Problem?
Suwayda | last month | his wife | by bus | 2 nights | the food – mansaf 3 times | the bus home didn't come; waited 2 hours
 4R   Report – sample
"Tareq went to Suwayda for his cousin's wedding. He travelled by bus with his wife and they stayed two nights. The best thing was the food – he ate mansaf three times! The problem was the journey home: the bus didn't come and they waited two hours."
 5   Read
1 Because it was her father's birthday.  2 At home she sleeps five hours; in Latakia she slept nine.  3 Five hours (instead of three).
 6   Write with help – sample
Our class holidays: Tareq in Suwayda   Last month Tareq went to Suwayda with his wife. It was his cousin's wedding and the whole family came.   They travelled by bus and stayed two nights. "I ate mansaf three times in two days," Tareq told me. He danced until two in the morning – at home he goes to bed at eleven!   The only problem was the journey home. The bus didn't come and they waited two hours at the garage.   But Tareq says it was worth it: "It was the best wedding of my life."
 6P   Check together – sample
Checklist | ✓ / ✗
Capitals and full stops | ✓
Past simple, he/she (no -s) | ✗ – he stayed ✓ but he ates → he ate
did / didn't + base verb | ✓
All parts | ✓
 6R   Share – sample
Before: The bus didn't came.  →  After: The bus didn't come.
 7   Write freely – sample (≈80 words)
Our class holidays: Hiba in BludanLast summer Hiba went to Bludan with her parents and her two brothers. Her uncle has a small house there and the family goes every August.They travelled by car and stayed ten days. "We didn't do anything and it was perfect," Hiba told me. They walked in the mountains every morning and in the evening they sat outside with the neighbours. At home Hiba studies until midnight; in Bludan she slept at ten.The only problem was the water – it stopped for two days.But Hiba says she wants to go back tomorrow: "The air there is different."
 7P   Respond – sample slip
★ Good report – and a nice quote at the end!   ✓ Small correction: we stayed nine days, not ten.   ? Why didn't you write about the night we lost the keys?
 7R   Share – sample
Class travel page: the mountain trips are usually the most popular.
 HW   Homework – sample Q&A
Questions: 1 Where did you go? 2 Who did you travel with? 3 How did you get there? 4 What was the best thing? 5 Did anything go wrong?Answers: 1 I went to Tartus. 2 With two friends from work. 3 We took the bus from the garage at seven. 4 The best thing was the fish restaurant near the port. 5 Yes – I left my jacket on the bus and I never saw it again!

8.3  Where were you?
 1   Get ready
We SAY… | We WRITE…
I was at work. | Yesterday at six I was at home.
Were you here on Tuesday? | The cupboard was open.
No, I wasn't. | The key wasn't in the office.
Who was in the room? | They were here until nine.
b  I was · you were · he was · we were · they were · the shop was · my parents were
 2   Listen and read
a  B (something is missing – the club camera)   b  1 He wasn't at the centre; he was at work until eight.  2 Lina – it was in her car for two weeks.
Language box – Practise:  1 wasn't  2 Were  3 were  4 wasn't  5 were
 3   Practise – sample with card A
A | The projector isn't in the meeting room! Where is it?
B | It was there on Sunday – I used it myself.
A | Were you here on Monday?
B | No, I wasn't. I was at a client's office all day.
A | Who was in the meeting room?
B | The training group. They were there until six.
A | Let's ask them!
 3R   Perform
Card A: the projector was in the meeting room; the training group had it. Card B: the bicycle was outside the building; the cousin borrowed it.
 4   Your turn – alibi game, sample
Interviewer | Where were you at eight o'clock yesterday evening?
Fadi | I was at the café in Shaalan with Rasha.
Interviewer | Was the café busy?
Fadi | Yes, it was. Very busy.
Interviewer (to Rasha) | Where were you at eight?
Rasha | At the café in Mezzeh with Fadi. It was almost empty.
 4R   Report – sample
"Fadi said they were at the café in Shaalan, but Rasha said they were at the café in Mezzeh. Fadi said the café was busy and Rasha said it was empty. They're guilty!"
 5   Read
1 It wasn't in Mr Bassam's office.  2 Because she wasn't at the club last week – she was ill.  3 A borrowing book with name, date and signature.
 6   Write with help – sample
REPORT – THE MISSING KEYS  From: Tareq Mansour  Date: 14 October   On Monday morning the keys of the small meeting room weren't on the board. The board was open and nobody was at the reception desk at eight o'clock.   I wasn't in the office on Friday afternoon – I was at the branch in Baramkeh. The cleaning team were here until seven.   Then Rana remembered: on Friday the auditors used the room and they returned the keys to the drawer, not to the board.   The keys were in the drawer all weekend.   My suggestion: one place for all keys, and a sign on the drawer.
 6P   Check together – sample
Checklist | ✓ / ✗
Capitals and full stops | ✓
was / were (no did) | ✗ – Did the keys were there? → Were the keys there?
wasn't / weren't | ✓
All parts | ✓
 6R   Share – sample
Before: I didn't was in the office on Friday.  →  After: I wasn't in the office on Friday.
 7   Write freely – sample (≈85 words)
REPORT – THE DELIVERY THAT DIDN'T ARRIVE   From: Maya Nasser   Date: 23 OctoberOn Tuesday the medicine delivery wasn't at the pharmacy. The driver usually comes at nine, but at eleven he wasn't here and his phone was off.I wasn't at the pharmacy on Monday – I was at the hospital pharmacy all day – so I called my colleague. She said the boxes were ready on Monday evening.This morning the company called. The driver was ill on Tuesday and nobody took his list. The boxes were in the warehouse for two days.My suggestion: the company sends us a message every morning with the driver's name and number.
 7P   Respond – sample slip (as manager)
★ Clear report – thank you.   ✓ Can you add which medicines were in the delivery?   ? Who had the driver's list on Tuesday?
 7R   Share – sample
Good suggestions are specific and easy (a book, a sign, a morning message), not "everyone must be more careful".
 HW   Homework – sample manager's reply
Dear Maya, Thank you for the clear report. I called the company this afternoon and they apologised. From Monday they will send a message every morning with the driver's name and number – your suggestion exactly. Please tell me immediately if a delivery is more than one hour late. Best regards, Dr Samar

8.4  I was born in Homs
 1   Get ready
We SAY… | We WRITE…
I was born in 1996. | 2014 – started university
We moved here in 2005. | She finished in 2019.
I grew up in Dummar. | In 2020 I got a job at a company.
When did you start work? | She is now the head teacher.
b  nineteen eighty-seven · nineteen ninety-five · two thousand and one · twenty ten (two thousand and ten) · twenty nineteen · twenty twenty-six
 2   Listen and read
a  A (a new colleague / a guest at the centre party – Hani)   b  1 In 2005, when she was seven.  2 Because the company closed.
Language box – Practise:  1 was born  2 in  3 on  4 for  5 when
 3   Practise – sample with life card A
A | Are you from Damascus?
B | No, actually. I was born in Deir ez-Zor in 1990. My family moved to Damascus in 1999.
A | So you grew up there. Where did you go to school?
B | In Mezzeh. Then I studied pharmacy at Damascus University.
A | And did you start work immediately?
B | I worked in a hospital for two years. Then in 2016 I opened my own pharmacy.
A | And now?
B | Now I'm married and I have two sons.
 3R   Perform
Card A years: 1990 born · 1999 moved · 2016 own pharmacy. Card B: 1985 born · 2010–2018 Dubai · 2018 came back.
 4   Your turn – sample timeline
1993 | 2011 | 2016 | 2018 | 2023
born in Damascus | started at the Faculty of Education | first teaching job | got married | moved to a school near her house
 4R   Report – sample
"Rasha was born in Damascus in 1993. She grew up in Jaramana and studied at the Faculty of Education. She started teaching in 2016 and she got married in 2018. In 2023 she moved to a new school near her house because she has two children now."
 5   Read
1 In the Old City, near Bab Touma.  2 She moved to Dubai with her family and worked at an international school.  3 She reads poetry and makes a very good lentil soup.
 6   Write with help – sample
OUR STUDENTS: Fadi Saeed   Fadi was born in Tartus in 1996 and grew up near the sea. He studied mechanical engineering in Homs.   He finished in 2019 and started working in a car garage the same year. "I learned more in six months in the garage than in four years at university," he says.   In 2021 he moved to Damascus, and in 2023 he opened a small workshop with his brother. Then in 2025 he started English classes because his customers are foreign companies.   He is now the best mechanic in Jaramana (he says!). In his free time he plays chess online.
 6P   Check together – sample
Checklist | ✓ / ✗
Capitals and full stops | ✓
was born | ✗ – He born in Tartus → He was born in Tartus
in + year, on + date | ✓
Past for the story, present for now | ✓
 6R   Share – sample
Before: He born in Tartus in 1996.  →  After: He was born in Tartus in 1996.
 7   Write freely – sample (≈90 words)
My grandmother, ZahraMy grandmother was born in a village near Hama in 1948. She grew up with seven brothers and sisters and she left school when she was eleven, because her mother was ill.She got married in 1966 and moved to Homs. She had six children, and my father was the youngest. In 1982 the family moved to Damascus and she worked for twenty years in a sweet factory."I never learned to read," she told me last year, "but I know everybody's stories."She is eighty-two now and she lives with my uncle in Jaramana. Every Friday she makes maamoul for the whole family.
 7P   Respond – sample slip
★ I like the detail about everybody's stories.   ✓ Can you add a quote about the sweet factory?   ? What did she do between 1966 and 1982?
 7R   Share – sample
Guessing works best when the biography keeps one surprising detail for the end.
 HW   Homework – sample own biography
Tareq Mansour was born in Suwayda in 1999 and grew up in a house with a big garden. He moved to Damascus in 2017 to study economics. He finished in 2021 and started work at a bank in Baramkeh the same year. He got married in 2024. He is now studying English because his bank works with foreign companies. In his free time he plays chess and he is learning to cook.

8.5  First… then… in the end
 1   Get ready
We SAY… | We WRITE…
First we took the bus… | In the end, everything was fine.
Then it started to rain. | After that, we walked to the café.
Suddenly, the bus stopped! | Finally, we arrived at nine.
What happened? And then? | It was a disaster – but a beautiful one.
b  First → Then → After that → Finally → In the end
 2   Listen and read
a  B (well)   b  1 The bus wasn't there and they waited an hour.  2 An old woman – she invited them into her kitchen.
Language box – Practise (order):  1 First, I met Hiba at the bus stop.  2 Then we waited twenty minutes for the service.  3 Suddenly, it started to rain.  4 After that, we walked to the café.  5 In the end, we were very late – but it was a great evening.
 3   Practise – sample, story card A
"First, a man left his house with a big cake for a party. Then he got on a very crowded minibus and he held the cake over his head. Suddenly, the minibus stopped and the cake fell on the floor! After that, everybody helped him – they put the cake back in the box with their hands. In the end, he arrived at the party with half a cake, and everybody laughed and ate it anyway."
 3R   Perform
All five sequencers should appear. The listener reacts with Oh no! What happened? And then?
 4   Your turn – sample
"Last Sunday was terrible. First, my alarm didn't ring and I woke up at half past seven. Then the water wasn't working, so I couldn't shower. After that, I ran to the bus stop and the bus left without me. Suddenly my neighbour stopped his car and gave me a lift. In the end I arrived at the bank at ten – and the manager was late too, so nobody said anything!"
 4R   Report – sample
"Tareq had a terrible morning last Sunday. First his alarm didn't ring. Then the water wasn't working, so he couldn't shower. After that, he ran to the bus and it left without him. In the end he arrived at the bank at ten – and the manager was late too!"
 5   Read
1 Four.  2 The second one (and the beginning of the third).  3 With a surprise: they didn't see the famous monastery, but it was the best day of the month.
 6   Write with help – sample (story card B pictures)
Last Thursday, Nour went to the airport to meet her sister. She was very happy and she had a big bunch of flowers.   But when the plane arrived, her sister wasn't there. After that, Nour waited for twenty minutes and she started to worry.   Then she phoned her. Her sister was at the other gate on the other side of the airport! They ran to each other and the flowers fell on the floor.   In the end, a little boy picked up the flowers and gave them back – and everybody in the airport was laughing.
 6P   Check together – sample
Checklist | ✓ / ✗
Capitals and full stops | ✓
Past simple (and was / were) | ✗ – she start to worry → she started to worry
Sequencers | ✓
Matches all the pictures; 35+ words | ✓
 6R   Share – sample
Before: Then she phone her sister.  →  After: Then she phoned her sister.
 7   Write freely – A2 Key Writing Part 7 sample (≈70 words)
Last week a boy was walking home from school when he saw a wallet on the street. He opened it and found a lot of money and a photo of a family.Then he saw a woman in front of him and he ran after her. "Excuse me! Is this yours?" he said. The woman looked in her bag – the wallet wasn't there!In the end, she was so happy that she took the boy to a shop and bought him a big box of sweets. He gave half of them to his sister.
 7P   Respond – sample slip
★ I love the ending with the sister!   ✓ Can you add how the boy felt when he saw the money?   ? What did the woman say to him?
 7R   Share – sample
The best endings add a feeling or a surprise, not just "and then we went home".
 HW   Homework – sample different ending
…Then he ran after the woman, but she got into a taxi and the taxi drove away. In the end, the boy took the wallet to the police station. The policeman wrote his name and address. Two weeks later a letter arrived for him, with a photo of the family and a thank-you card from the woman.

8.6  Really? That's great!
 1   Get ready
We SAY… | We WRITE…
That's great news! | Congratulations! 🎉
Oh no! What happened? | I'm sorry to hear about your car.
Poor you! | I hope the garage finishes it quickly.
Well done! | If you need a lift, just call me.
b  Good news: Congratulations! · That's wonderful! · Well done!   Bad news: I'm sorry to hear that. · Oh no! · Poor you!
 2   Listen and read
a  B (three: Hiba's exam, Fadi's car, Rasha's new niece)   b  1 She passed her exam with 92.  2 His car broke down on the highway and it cost him a lot.
Language box – Practise (sample answers):  1 Congratulations! That's wonderful!  2 Oh no, I'm sorry to hear that. I hope she gets better soon.  3 Congratulations! That's lovely news!  4 Oh no! Poor you.  5 Well done!
 3   Practise – sample
A | I've got some news – my brother got a job in Dubai!
B | Really? That's wonderful! Congratulations to him!
A | Thank you! He starts next month.
B | What happened exactly – did he apply, or did they call him?
A | A friend sent his CV to the company.
B | And is your mother happy, or sad that he's travelling?
 3R   Perform
Listeners tick the reactions; the key point is intonation – a flat "That's wonderful" sounds sarcastic.
 4   Your turn – sample
Real: "My sister had a baby girl on Sunday – her name is Jouri."  Invented: "I won a car in a competition at the supermarket!"
 4R   Report – sample
"Rasha told me her sister had a baby girl on Sunday – that was true. She also said she won a car in a competition. I didn't believe that one!"
 5   Read
1 All the nights in the café with her books.  2 A lift to work before Thursday (he passes Fadi's building at 7.45).  3 The second one (bad news) – because bad news needs more care, an offer of help and a reason not to feel embarrassed about asking.
 6   Write with help – samples
Good news: Rasha! Congratulations! A baby girl is amazing news – and after everything your sister went through this year. I know how much she wanted this. Let's all come and visit on Friday with sweets!
Bad news: Tareq, I'm really sorry to hear about your father's operation. Three hours in the hospital waiting – what a hard day for all of you. I hope he comes home soon and gets strong quickly. If you need anybody to stay with the children in the afternoon, just call me. Don't worry about asking.
 6P   Check together – sample
Checklist | ✓ / ✗
Capitals and full stops | ✓
Right reaction for the news | ✓
Something personal | ✗ – add what you remember about them
Celebration plan OR real offer of help | ✓
 6R   Share – sample
Before: Sorry about your father. Bye.  →  After: I'm really sorry to hear about your father. I hope he comes home soon. If you need help with the children, just call me.
 7   Write freely – sample (≈75 words)
To Hiba (good news): Hiba, congratulations!! 92 – and with a full-time job! I remember you studying in the café every evening last winter with your coat on because the heating didn't work. You earned every mark. Let's celebrate on Thursday – and this time I'm paying.To Fadi (bad news): Fadi, I'm so sorry about the car. Two hours on the highway and then the price – what a week. I hope the garage finishes it before Thursday. I pass your building at a quarter to eight every morning, so call me and I'll take you to work. It's no problem at all.
 7P   Respond – sample slip
★ This made me smile – you remembered the coat!   ✓ Can you add what time on Thursday?   ? Are we going to the same café?
 7R   Share – sample
The better reply is usually the one with a concrete offer (a time, a lift, a visit), not only kind words.
 HW   Homework – sample reply to the reply
Thank you so much, Tareq – that's very kind. Yes please, a lift on Monday and Tuesday would help a lot. I'll be downstairs at a quarter to eight with coffee for both of us. You saved my week!

8.7  Review & Mediate: Sami says he…
 1   Get ready – quiz
1 I went to Homs last week.  2 Did you go to the party?  3 She didn't come.  4 Where were you yesterday?  5 I was born in 1995.  6 I'm sorry to hear that.
 2   Listen and read
b  It broke down again on Wednesday, and it needs a new engine.
 3   Practise – sample (card A told back)
"She says she finished her thesis on Monday – she worked on it for two years! Her defence is on the twentieth and she's terrified. She wants us all to come: it's at the university at ten."
Card B told back: "They moved to the new flat on Saturday, but the lift doesn't work. Her husband carried everything up five floors – twice! She says come and see it on Friday, but bring strong legs."
 3R   Perform
Common things forgotten: the date, the time, and the invitation at the end. Remind students: the message is useless if the time is lost!
 4   Your turn – sample
Rasha | My sister had a baby girl on Sunday. Her name is Jouri.
Hiba | Congratulations! How is your sister?
Rasha | She's tired but very happy. I visited them on Monday.
Hiba | Who does the baby look like?
Rasha | Her father! Everybody says it.
 4R   Report – mediation sample
"Hiba told me about Rasha's news. Rasha's sister had a baby girl on Sunday in Jaramana. The baby's name is Jouri. Rasha visited them on Monday and she says the baby looks like her father!"
Teaching point: compare the final report with the original. What changed? Names, numbers and dates are the first things to disappear.
 5   Read – A2 Key Reading Part 3
1 B  2 A  3 B  4 B  5 A
 6   Write with help – sample
Hi Lina,   I've got news from Sami! He had an interview on Sunday at a company in Mezzeh, and on Tuesday they called him – he got the job! He starts on the first of November – more money, and it's only fifteen minutes from his house.   The bad news: on Wednesday his car broke down again and it needs a new engine, so he has a new job and no car.   He says he's sorry, but he can't come to the dinner on Friday because he's moving his things to the new office. Let's all call him tonight and say congratulations!   Omar
 6P   Check together – sample
Checklist | ✓ / ✗
I → he / she | ✓
Past simple for the events | ✓
Present for what is still true | ✗ – he started on the first of November → he starts
The message to Lina passed on | ✓
 7   Write freely – A2 Key Writing Part 7 sample (≈60 words)
Last Tuesday it was raining hard and a young woman was waiting at the bus stop with two heavy bags. She waited for twenty minutes and she was very cold.Then a car stopped and an old neighbour opened the window. "Where are you going?" he said. "I can take you."In the end, she put her bags on the back seat and they drove home together, laughing about the terrible weather.
Marking note (A2 Key Part 7, 15 marks): the story must cover all three pictures, be 35+ words, use the past simple, and be understandable. A story with only two pictures loses content marks even if the language is good.
 ✓   Unit 8 check
A  1 b  2 a  3 a  4 c  5 b  6 b  7 a  8 b
B  1 A · 2 D · 3 C · 4 G · 5 E   (the extra letters are B and F)
Note: students often choose F for Sunday because they hear "my brother" and think of Homs. Point out that the recording says "I went to my brother's house" for a birthday – no travelling.
C  Sample message (≈45 words):
Hi Dima! Something good happened last month. On the 12th I had an interview at a new school, and a week later they called me – I got the job! I start in November. I was so nervous in the interview, but now I'm really happy.  Rasha
Scoring guide: A = 8 marks, B = 4 marks (question 4 not counted), C = 5 marks (content 2, organisation 1, language 2). Total 17. 12+ = ready for Unit 9.

Answer Key · Unit 9  Work & Study
Proactive English · A2 · Book 1 · Syria Edition
Every task has an answer or a full sample answer. Samples show one good A2 answer; learners' own answers will differ.
9.1  I have to check the machines
 1   Get ready
We SAY… | We WRITE…
I have to check my emails first. | I'm responsible for the stock.
Do I have to work on Saturdays? | Technicians have to be here at eight.
You don't have to come on Fridays. | Write a note for every repair.
What time do I have to start? | Mr Fouad reads all the notes on Thursday.
b  Students' own answers, e.g. wear a uniform – nurses, police, hotel staff · work at night – doctors, drivers, bakers · use a computer all day – accountants, designers · talk to customers – shop assistants, receptionists · carry heavy things – builders, delivery drivers.
 2   Listen and read
a  B (a new trainee)   b  1 Check the machines in the workshop (which are ready, which are waiting for parts).  2 She doesn't have to talk to customers at the desk, and she doesn't have to come on Fridays.
Language box – Practise:  1 has to  2 don't have to  3 Do … have to  4 has to  5 don't have to
 3   Practise – sample with job card A
A | Welcome! Let me explain the work. Every morning I have to check the fridge temperature.
B | Do I have to do that too?
A | No, you don't. You're responsible for the shelves and the prices.
B | OK. And the patients?
A | The pharmacist talks to them. But you have to write the delivery book every day.
B | What time do I have to start?
A | We open at eight, but you have to be here at a quarter to.
B | Do I have to work on Fridays?
A | Only in the afternoon.
 3R   Perform
Card A: has to – shelves and prices, the delivery book; doesn't have to – talk to patients. Card B: has to – keys and breakfast list, wear a uniform; doesn't have to – deal with problems. Typical board-clinic errors: She have to → She has to; don't have to confused with mustn't.
 4   Your turn – sample
Every day | Every week | Every month | I don't have to…
prepare lessons, check the register | meet the other teachers on Sunday | write reports for the parents | wear a uniform (but something smart)
 4R   Report – sample
"Rasha has to prepare her lessons every evening and she has to write reports every month. She has to be at school at half past seven. She doesn't have to wear a uniform, but she has to wear something smart."
 5   Read
1 The problem, the solution and the time.  2 The junior technician has to check the small parts box.  3 Deal with customers at the desk and answer the company phone.
 6   Write with help – sample
AL-SHIFA PHARMACY – JOB DESCRIPTION  Position: Assistant  Reports to: Maya Nasser   Main duties: The assistant is responsible for the shelves and the prices. He or she has to check the dates on the medicines every week and write the delivery book every day. He or she also has to keep the counter clean.   Hours: 8.00–16.00, Saturday to Thursday. The assistant has to work one Friday afternoon a month.   Not part of this job: The assistant doesn't have to give medicines to patients and doesn't have to order from the companies.
 6P   Check together – sample
Checklist | ✓ / ✗
Capitals and full stops | ✓
has to + base verb | ✗ – has to writes → has to write
doesn't have to | ✓
All parts | ✓
 6R   Share – sample
Before: He or she has to writes the delivery book.  →  After: He or she has to write the delivery book.
 7   Write freely – sample (≈85 words)
AL-NOUR SCHOOL – JOB DESCRIPTION   Position: Primary teacher (Grade 3)   Reports to: the head teacherMain duties: The teacher is responsible for one class of about thirty children. He or she has to prepare the lessons, teach Arabic, maths and science, and correct the children's work every day. He or she has to write a short report for the parents every month and has to meet them twice a year.Hours: 7.30–14.00, Sunday to Thursday. Teachers have to arrive fifteen minutes before the children.Not part of this job: The teacher doesn't have to teach English (we have a specialist) and doesn't have to stay after two o'clock.
 7P   Respond – sample slip
★ This sounds interesting – I like the small class!   ✓ Can you add the salary?   ? Do I have to prepare the end-of-year party too?
 7R   Share – sample
Typical class result: the jobs with the earliest start (bakers, teachers, technicians) have the hardest hours.
 HW   Homework – sample application email
Dear Ms Rasha, I am writing to apply for the position of primary teacher at Al-Nour School. I studied education and I taught in a village school for three years, so I have experience with small children. I would like this job because the class is small and the school is near my house. One question: does the teacher have to prepare materials, or does the school provide them? Best regards, Hiba Khalil

9.2  You mustn't use that door
 1   Get ready
We SAY… | We WRITE…
You mustn't smoke in the building. | Staff must wear ID badges.
You must wear your badge – always. | Machines mustn't leave the building.
Do I have to bring my own lunch? | Please use the kitchen.
That one is serious. | Thank you for helping us work safely.
b  Necessary: wear a seat belt, come to the meeting · Forbidden: park in front of the gate · Free: bring your own lunch
 2   Listen and read
a  B (she uses the wrong door)   b  1 It's the emergency exit and it has to stay closed.  2 She must wear it – on her shirt, not in her bag.
Language box – Practise:  1 mustn't  2 don't have to  3 mustn't  4 don't have to  5 mustn't
 3   Practise – sample with rules card A
A | Let me tell you the important rules. First, you must wear a white coat and glasses.
B | OK. And food?
A | You mustn't eat or drink inside. Never.
B | Do I have to work after four?
A | No, you don't have to. But you mustn't touch other people's samples.
B | So: white coat and glasses, no food, no other samples. Did I forget anything?
 3R   Perform
Card A: must – white coat and glasses; mustn't – eat/drink inside, touch other samples; don't have to – work after 4. Card B: must – wash hands, sign children in/out; mustn't – take photos, give food without asking; don't have to – wear a uniform.
 4   Your turn – sample
"At my workshop everybody must wear boots and nobody must use the big machines alone – two people, always. You mustn't use your phone near the machines. You don't have to wear a uniform, but you must wear something you can wash!"
 4R   Report – sample
"At Fadi's workshop everybody must wear boots, and they mustn't use the machines alone. They don't have to wear a uniform. Fadi says the best rule is 'no phones near the machines' – he saw an accident last year."
 5   Read
1 In the office and in the workshop, during working hours.  2 Write your name and the date in the parts book.  3 They must be on silent in the workshop; you don't have to leave them at reception, but you shouldn't answer calls during a repair.
 6   Write with help – sample
FLAT 7 – HOUSE RULES1 Keys. Everybody must take their own key. We mustn't leave the building door open, because the lock is broken.2 Kitchen. You must wash your own plates the same evening.3 Noise. You mustn't play music after eleven – the neighbour downstairs works at five in the morning.4 Guests. You don't have to ask before you invite a friend for coffee, but please tell us if somebody stays the night.Thank you for making this a nice home. – Lama
 6P   Check together – sample
Checklist | ✓ / ✗
Capitals and full stops | ✓
must / mustn't / don't have to + base verb | ✗ – You must to wash → You must wash
A reason for at least one rule | ✓
Numbered sections + friendly ending | ✓
 6R   Share – sample
Before: You must to wash your plates.  →  After: You must wash your plates.
 7   Write freely – sample (≈90 words)
MEDICA TRADING – OFFICE RULES1 Arrival. All staff must be at their desk at eight o'clock. If you are ill, you must call before half past seven, not send a message.2 Documents. Customer files mustn't leave the office, because they contain private information.3 The meeting room. You must write your name in the book before you use it. You don't have to book it for five minutes, but please don't stay two hours without booking!4 Kitchen. You mustn't leave food in the fridge after Thursday.5 Visitors. Visitors must wait at reception. You mustn't bring anybody into the accounts office.Thank you for your help. – Khaled Ahmad, Manager
 7P   Respond – sample slip
★ Clear and fair – and I like the reason in rule 2.   ✓ Can you add why we can't bring visitors into accounts?   ? What happens if I forget to book the meeting room?
 7R   Share – sample
Funniest rules usually come from shared flats and family cars!
 HW   Homework – sample message
Hi Tareq, I read your office rules – they're clear! One thing I don't understand: why mustn't we bring visitors into the accounts office? Is it because of the files? And can I suggest one more rule: the last person to leave must check the air conditioning. Last week it ran all weekend!

9.3  Is it OK if I leave early?
 1   Get ready
We SAY… | We WRITE…
Could I leave early today? | Dear Mr Khaled, I am writing to ask…
Is it OK if I take Monday off? | I will finish the report before I leave.
Have you got a minute? | Rana has all the files.
Of course. / I'm afraid not. | Thank you for considering my request.
b  I'm leaving early. (not polite – it's a statement!) → Can I leave early? → Is it OK if I leave early? → Could I possibly leave early?
 2   Listen and read
a  B (to leave early – at three on Thursday)   b  1 Her niece has a small operation and she wants to be at the hospital with her brother.  2 Finish the supplier report on Wednesday evening (and Rana has the files).
Language box – Practise (sample answers):  1 Is it OK if I leave at four today? I have a dentist's appointment.  2 Could I take Monday off? There's a family wedding.  3 Could I possibly change my shift with Fadi? I have an exam.  4 Is it OK if I use the meeting room at two? I have an online interview.
 3   Practise – sample
A | Mr Fouad, have you got a minute?
B | Yes, come in. What is it?
A | Is it OK if I leave at two on Sunday? My son starts school that day and I'd like to take him.
B | Of course.
A | I'll finish the Thursday repairs on Saturday, and Nour has the parts list if anybody needs it.
B | Perfect.
A | Thank you very much.
 3R   Perform
A good performance always has reason + solution. A request with no solution should get "let's talk about it".
 4   Your turn – sample

Hiba | Could I use the lab on Sunday afternoon?
Teacher | Let's talk about it next week – I need to check the timetable.

The class usually says yes to the emails that solve the problem, not to the ones with the best excuse.

9.5  Can I take a message?
 1   Get ready
We SAY… | We WRITE…
Good morning, Medica Trading. | Mr Chen called at 10.15.
Who's calling, please? | From: Yusuf Haddadin, Delta Medical
I'm afraid he's in a meeting. | Action: please call back today
Let me read that back. | Taken by: Lina
b  Who's calling, please? = asking the name · Hold on, please. = wait · I'm afraid he's in a meeting. = he isn't free · Could you spell that? = asking for the letters · I'll give him the message. = promising
 2   Listen and read
a  B (Mr Khaled)   b  1 He's in a meeting until twelve.  2 Call him back today (it's a little urgent).
Language box – Practise:  1 calling  2 spell  3 take  4 afraid  5 read
 3   Practise – sample with caller card A
A | Good morning, Medica Trading.

B | This is Samar Khoury from Zenith Pharma.
A | Could you spell your family name? … Can I take a message?
B | Yes, please. The delivery will arrive on Tuesday, not Monday. Could you ask him to call me back before two?
A | Of course. What's your number?
B | 0991 223 447.
A | Let me read that back: 0991 223 447. I'll give him the message.
 3R   Perform
Card A: Samar Khoury, Zenith Pharma, delivery Tuesday not Monday, call back before 2, 0991 223 447. Card B: Walid Nassif, the bank, documents missing a signature, needed by Thursday, 011 556 7788 ext. 214.

9.6  Could you send me the file?
 1   Get ready
We SAY… | We WRITE…
Could you send me the file? | Thanks in advance!
Would you mind bringing a spare screen? | Here is the model number: …
Could you ask reception to make a badge? | I will come tomorrow at 9 am.
No problem. | Best regards, Rana · Medica Trading
b  Most polite: Would you mind sending me the file? Then: Could you send me the file, please? → Can you send me the file? → Send me the file. (an order, not a request!)
 2   Listen and read
a  C (three: come tomorrow, bring a spare screen, ask reception for a badge – and she must send the model number)   b  1 It turns on, but the screen stays black.  2 Tomorrow morning at nine.
Language box – Practise (sample answers):  1 Could you send me the report, please?  2 Would you mind changing the meeting to three?  3 Could you possibly help me with this machine?  4 Could you tell Mr Fouad I called?
 3   Practise – sample with problem card A
B | TechCare, good afternoon.
A | Hello, this is Lina from Medica Trading.
B | Yes, speaking. How can I help?
A | We've got a problem with the office printer – it prints everything grey.
B | Could you try one thing before I come? Take the colour cartridge out and put it back.
A | OK, wait… No, the same.
B | Fine. I'll come tomorrow morning.
A | Thank you. We need 60 copies for a meeting at ten, so would you mind coming before nine? And could you ask your office to send me the price of the ink first?
 3R   Perform
A good performance uses a different form for each request: Could you…? / Would you mind …-ing? / Could you ask … to…?
 4   Your turn – sample
"Could you send me the photos from the trip? … Would you mind changing our study day to Sunday? … And could you ask your brother about a part for my car?"
 4R   Report – sample
"Fadi asked me to send him the photos from the trip, to change our study day to Sunday, and to ask my brother about a car part. I said yes to the photos and the car part, but I can't study on Sunday – I'm working."
 5   Read
1 The model number and serial number of the laptop (Dell Latitude 5420, DL-5420-77Q).  2 Back up the important files before he arrives.  3 To bring a machine Lina can use for two days if she needs it urgently.

Subject: Projector for room 4 – model and dates   Dear Omar,   Thank you for your call this morning. The most professional pairs: clear subject lines, both requests answered, and one new request added politely.

9.7  Review & Mediate: Mr Saleh called
 1   Get ready – quiz
1 I must wear a badge.  2 She has to work on Saturday.  3 You mustn't smoke here.  4 Would you mind sending the file?  5 Can I leave early?  6 He doesn't have to come.
 2   Listen and read
b  He gives the facts in order and makes the message professional: he says "he isn't happy" instead of "he is angry!!!", and he ends with the number and how to contact him.
 3   Practise – sample with notes card A
"Dr Samar from Al-Noor Clinic called at a quarter past nine. The delivery arrived, but three boxes are missing. She needs them before Sunday because the clinic is closed on Friday and Saturday. She isn't angry, but she's worried. She asked us to call her back after two – she's with patients until then."
Card B sample: "Umm Yazan, the building owner, called at ten to five. The water pump is broken and there's no water on floors four, five and six. There are two families with small children. She'd like a plumber tonight if possible. She's at her sister's house, so call her mobile: 0955 318 204."
 3R   Perform
Six parts: who · when · problem · feeling · what they want · contact. The most common omission is the time of the call.
 4   Your turn – sample
"My neighbour called my mother yesterday evening. The electricity in our building goes off every night at nine. The company says it isn't their problem. The neighbours want to meet on Friday after prayers to decide what to do, and they want one person from every flat."
 4R   Report – mediation sample
"Hiba's neighbour called her mother yesterday evening. The electricity in the building goes off every night at nine and the company says it isn't their problem. The neighbours want to meet on Friday after prayers to decide what to do. They want one person from every flat."
Missing information students often notice: where the meeting is, and what time "after prayers" means.
 5   Read – A2 Key Reading Part 5
1 had  2 have  3 have  4 to  5 yet  6 to
 6   Write with help – sample
Subject: Machine 3 – visit this afternoon at 3 pm   Dear Mr Saleh,   Thank you for your call this morning. I am very sorry that machine three has stopped working again, and I understand that you waited a long time last month.   My colleague and I will come to you at three o'clock this afternoon. We will bring a spare part with us so that we can finish the repair today if possible.   Could you ask your team not to use the machine before we arrive? It will help us find the real problem.   Again, I apologise for the trouble. We do not want to lose your trust.   Best regards, Omar Darwish · TechCare
 6P   Check together – sample
Checklist | ✓ / ✗
Apology and the time of the visit | ✓
A polite request | ✓
Formal forms (no contractions) | ✗ – We'll come → We will come
Clear subject + professional ending | ✓
 7   Write freely – A2 Key Writing Part 6 sample (≈45 words)
Dear Ms Huda,I am sorry, but I can't come to class next Wednesday because I have to travel to Aleppo for my work. What is the homework for that lesson? Could you send me the listening track by email, please?Thank you,Tareq
Marking note: all three content points (why, asking about the homework, asking her to send something) must be present for full content marks.
 ✓   Unit 9 check
A  1 b  2 b  3 a  4 a  5 c  6 b  7 b  8 a
B  1 Samar Khoury  2 Zenith Pharma  3 thirty / 30  4 five / 5  5 0944 762 310
Note for question 3: the caller says "We asked for forty boxes, but we only need thirty now" – the answer is 30. Students who write 40 heard the first number only; play that line again.
C  Sample email (≈45 words):
Dear Rana, Could you send me the supplier list before Thursday? I need it for the meeting with Mr Khaled. And would you mind booking the small meeting room for 10 am? Thanks in advance. Best regards, Lina
Scoring guide: A = 8 marks, B = 5 marks, C = 5 marks (content 2, organisation 1, language 2). Total 18. 13+ = ready for Unit 10.

Answer Key · Unit 10  Health
Proactive English · A2 · Book 1 · Syria Edition
Every task has an answer or a full sample answer. Samples show one good A2 answer; learners' own answers will differ.
Note for the teacherThis unit teaches the language of health, not medicine. If a student asks a real medical question in class ("How many of these should I take?"), the answer is always: ask your pharmacist or doctor – and that is also what the characters do in every cycle.Some students may want to talk about a serious illness in their family. Let them if they want to, keep it short and kind, and move the lesson on. Nobody has to share anything personal: the tasks always allow an invented example.
10.1  I've got a sore throat
 1   Get ready
We SAY… | We WRITE…
I've got a headache. | I can't come to work today.
My back hurts. | I've had a temperature since Sunday.
How do you feel? | I'm going to see a doctor at eleven.
You don't look well. | Get well soon!
b  stomach – You feel it after a big meal. · head – You think with it. · back – You sit on a chair and it hurts. · throat – You speak with it. · tooth – You eat with it.
 2   Listen and read
a  B (at the pharmacy)   b  1 Since Sunday – three days.  2 See a doctor today (Dr Samar at Al-Noor Clinic, open until six).
Language box – Practise:  1 a  2 hurt  3 – (no article: she's got toothache)  4 well  5 have … Since
 3   Practise – sample with symptom card A
B | You don't look well. What's the matter?
A | I've got a bad stomach ache. And I feel dizzy.
B | Oh dear. How long have you had it?
A | Since yesterday evening.
B | Can you eat?
A | No, I can't eat anything.
B | I think you should see a doctor today, not tomorrow.
A | OK, I'm going.
 3R   Perform
Card A: stomach ache, since yesterday evening, can't eat, dizzy. Card B: toothache, for a week, worse at night, swollen face. Both cards should end with "see a doctor / dentist". Typical board-clinic errors: I have headache → I've got a headache; My feet hurts → My feet hurt.
 4   Your turn – sample
"Last winter I had a bad cough for about two weeks. I didn't sleep well because it was worse at night, and I felt tired all day. First I just drank tea with lemon, but after ten days I went to a doctor. After that I was fine in a few days – I waited too long."
 4R   Report – sample
"Last winter Fadi had a bad cough for two weeks. He didn't sleep well and he felt tired all day. In the end he went to a doctor, and after that he was fine."
 5   Read
1 The first is for his manager, Mr Fouad; the second is for his friend and colleague Omar.  2 The first: Dear Mr Fouad, I'm sorry, but I can't come, I'll call you after the doctor (full sentences, no emojis). The second: short phrases, jokes, emojis.  3 His work – what is finished and who can do the rest.
 6   Write with help – sample
Dear Ms Huda,   I'm sorry, but I can't come to class today. I've had a bad cold and a temperature since Tuesday, and this morning I feel worse. I'm going to see a doctor at four.   I finished the writing task and I'll send it tonight. Rasha has my book and she can give Hiba the photocopies.   I'll message you tomorrow about Thursday.   Sorry again,   Tareq
 6P   Check together – sample
Checklist | ✓ / ✗
Capitals and full stops | ✓
I've got / I've had … since / for | ✗ – I have a cold since Tuesday → I've had a cold since Tuesday
The work section | ✓
All parts | ✓
 6R   Share – sample
Before: I have a cold since Tuesday.  →  After: I've had a cold since Tuesday.
 7   Write freely – sample (≈85 words)
Formal: Dear Mr Khaled, I'm sorry, but I can't come to the office today. I've had a stomach problem since yesterday evening and I didn't sleep. I have an appointment at the clinic at ten. The supplier report is finished and it's in the shared folder, and Rana can join the call at eleven. I'll call you this afternoon. Sorry again, LinaInformal: Rana – not coming in today 🤒 Stomach, since last night, no sleep at all. Going to the clinic at ten. The report is in the folder (final version, the one from yesterday evening). Can you do the eleven o'clock call? Mr Khaled knows. Thank you!! I'll message you after the doctor.
 7P   Respond – sample slip
★ Thank you for organising the report – nothing stops.   ✓ Can you add who has the key for your drawer?   ? Do you need tomorrow too?
 7R   Share – sample
Differences the class usually finds: Dear + name vs just the name · full sentences vs short phrases · no emojis vs emojis · I'll call you vs I'll message you.
 HW   Homework – sample manager's reply
Dear Lina, Thank you for letting me know, and thank you for finishing the report before you left. Please don't worry about the call – Rana can do it and I will be there too. Rest today and take tomorrow as well if you need it. Get well soon. Best regards, Khaled

10.2  You should drink more water
 1   Get ready
We SAY… | We WRITE…
You should drink more water. | Why don't you take a day off?
You shouldn't study on your bed. | First, you should change one small thing.
What should I do? | Second, you shouldn't take your phone to bed.
That's a good idea, actually. | If you still feel tired, see a doctor.
b  Good: You should sleep more. · You should drink water. · You shouldn't sit at the computer for six hours.  Bad: You shouldn't go to the doctor – it's expensive. (Never advise somebody not to get help!)
 2   Listen and read
a  B (her back hurts)   b  1 Because she sits at a computer from eight to four and then studies in the evening – about ten hours on a chair.  2 If it doesn't get better, she should see a doctor and not wait a month.
Language box – Practise (sample answers):  1 You should sleep more / You shouldn't drink coffee in the evening.  2 You should drink warm water with lemon / You should see a pharmacist.  3 You shouldn't look at your phone in bed.  4 You should look away from the screen every twenty minutes.  5 You should drink less coffee and more water.
 3   Practise – sample with problem card A
B | What's wrong?
A | I'm always tired. I sleep five hours and I drink a lot of coffee.
B | You should go to bed one hour earlier – just one hour.
A | I try, but I look at my phone.
B | Then why don't you leave your phone in the kitchen? And you shouldn't drink coffee after four.
A | That's a good idea, actually.
B | And if you're still tired after a month, you should see a doctor.
 3R   Perform
Three different forms should appear: You should… · You shouldn't… · Why don't you…? and a closing if it doesn't get better…
 4   Your turn – sample
Problem: "I never have time to exercise."Advice collected: You should walk to work. · You shouldn't take the service for short journeys. · Why don't you exercise with your children? · You should start with ten minutes, not an hour.
 4R   Report – sample
"My problem was 'I never have time to exercise'. Fadi said I should walk to work. Hiba said I shouldn't take the service for short journeys. Rasha asked why I don't exercise with my children. I think Rasha's advice is the best – my children have a lot of energy!"
 5   Read
1 You should change one small thing, not everything.  2 In the kitchen.  3 He should see a doctor – sometimes being tired is not about habits.
 6   Write with help – sample
Dear H.,   I had the same problem last year, so I understand you.   First, you should put your books on a table, not on your bed. I did this and my back changed in two weeks.   Second, you shouldn't study for three hours without stopping. Study for forty minutes, then stand up and walk for five.   Why don't you also ask a classmate to study with you? You remind each other to take breaks.   If your back still hurts after a month, you should see a doctor.   Good luck! It gets better. – R.
 6P   Check together – sample
Checklist | ✓ / ✗
Capitals and full stops | ✓
should / shouldn't + base verb | ✗ – You should to put → You should put
Advice in order | ✓
Ending says when to get real help | ✓
 6R   Share – sample
Before: You should to put your books on a table.  →  After: You should put your books on a table.
 7   Write freely – sample (≈90 words)
Dear T.,I read your letter and I smiled, because I was exactly the same two years ago.First, you should change your evening, not your whole life. Choose one thing: eat, then go out for twenty minutes. Don't open your phone before the walk.Second, you shouldn't keep your phone next to your bed. Mine is in the kitchen now. The first week is terrible and then you sleep like a child.Why don't you ask your brother or a neighbour to walk with you? It's much easier with somebody.And if you still feel tired after a month, you should see a doctor.Good luck – it really does get better. – H.
 7P   Respond – sample slip
★ I'm going to try the phone in the kitchen!   ✓ Can you add how long it took to feel different?   ? What if I work until nine – when should I walk?
 7R   Share – sample
The advice students say they would really follow is always the smallest one (twenty minutes, one hour earlier, one glass of water).
 HW   Homework – sample thank-you reply
Thank you for your letter! I'm going to try the walk first, because it's the easiest one and I don't need anything for it. The phone in the kitchen is harder – but I'll try that next week. I'll write to you in a month and tell you if I sleep better.

10.3  I'd like to make an appointment
 1   Get ready
We SAY… | We WRITE…
I'd like to make an appointment. | Tuesday 28th, 10.30 – Dr Samar
Is Tuesday at ten OK? | Please bring your ID card.
Can I take your name? | Come 10 minutes early.
How much is the visit? | To change: call before 6 pm the day before.
b  1 Good morning, Al-Noor Clinic.  2 I'd like to make an appointment, please.  3 Is Tuesday at ten OK?  4 Can I take your name?  5 Perfect, thank you.
 2   Listen and read
a  A (today at 11)   b  1 He's at a site in Kafr Sousa.  2 His ID card (and he must come ten minutes early to fill in a form).
Language box – Practise:  1 to  2 on  3 at  4 early  5 come
 3   Practise – sample with card A
B | Good morning, Al-Amal Dental Clinic.
A | Good morning. I'd like to make an appointment, please. I've had toothache for a week.
B | Of course. We have Tuesday after four or Thursday morning.
A | I can't come on Thursday morning because I'm at work. Tuesday at five?
B | Tuesday at five. Can I take your name? … And your phone number?
A | Hiba Khalil, 0936 145 870.
B | Please bring your old X-rays and come ten minutes early. The visit is eighty thousand.
A | Can I pay by card?
B | Yes, we take cards.
 3R   Perform
Card A: dentist, Tue after 4 or Thu morning, bring old X-rays, 80,000, card OK. Card B: garage, Mon 8 am or Wed 2 pm, leave the car 3 hours, check free.
 4   Your turn – sample
Tareq | Good morning. I'd like to make an appointment to check my car – it's making a noise.
Partner | We have Monday at eight or Wednesday at two.
Tareq | Monday at eight, please.
Partner | (second call) I'm sorry – can you come Wednesday instead? Our mechanic is ill on Monday.
Tareq | I'm working on Wednesday at two… Could I come at four?
Partner | Four is fine. Please leave the car for three hours.
 4R   Report – sample
"I made an appointment at the garage for Monday at eight, but then I had to change it because I'm working. Now it's Wednesday at two. I have to leave the car for three hours and the check is free."
 5   Read
1 His ID card and old reports if he has any; ten minutes early.  2 Before 6 pm the day before.  3 On Wednesday (he's free after 3 pm every other day).
 6   Write with help – sample
Good morning. My name is Hiba Khalil and I have an appointment with Dr Nabil on Tuesday 28 October at 5 pm.   I'm afraid I can't come at that time: my college changed our exam to Tuesday afternoon. Could I change the appointment to Thursday, or to any day after 4 pm? I'm free every afternoon except Tuesday.   I'm sorry for the trouble. My phone number is 0936 145 870.   Thank you very much.
 6P   Check together – sample
Checklist | ✓ / ✗
Capitals (days, months) and full stops | ✓
Could I…? / I'd like to… | ✓
The times you ARE free | ✗ – add "I'm free every afternoon except Tuesday"
All parts | ✓
 6R   Share – sample
Before: I can't come on Tuesday.  →  After: I can't come on Tuesday, but I'm free every other afternoon after four.
 7   Write freely – sample
(a) Appointment cardAL-AMAL DENTAL CLINIC · Patient: Hiba Khalil · Dentist: Dr Nabil Aziz · Date: Tuesday 28 October · Time: 5.00 pm · Please bring: ID card, old X-rays · Note: come 10 minutes early. First visit 80,000 SYP, cash or card. · To change: call 011 332 8899 before 7 pm the day before.(b) MessageGood afternoon. Could I come on Thursday 30th instead? I'm free after 4 pm every day except Tuesday. I'm sorry for the trouble. My number is 0936 145 870. Thank you.
 7P   Respond – sample slip (as receptionist)
★ Easy to help you – you gave me three possible times!   ✓ Can you add your phone number at the top too?   ? Is Thursday at half past four OK?
 7R   Share – sample
The message the receptionist can answer in one reply is always the one that says when the person IS free.
 HW   Homework – sample receptionist's reply

10.4  Take one tablet twice a day
 1   Get ready
We SAY… | We WRITE…
Take it after meals. | Twice a day for five days
Finish all of them. | Keep away from children.
Don't take it on an empty stomach. | Do not use after the date on the box.
Shall I write it for you? | Ask your doctor or pharmacist.
b  twice a day = two times a day · three times a day = morning, afternoon, evening · every eight hours = three times in 24 hours · before meals = before you eat · with food = while you eat · at night = before you sleep
 2   Listen and read
a  B (an older customer, Abu Samir)   b  1 Twice a day – morning and evening.  2 He mustn't stop early: he has to finish all seven days, even if he feels better after three.
Language box – Practise (sample answers):  1 Take one tablet three times a day, after meals.  2 Take two spoons – ten millilitres – at night.  3 Take it three times in 24 hours (for example 7 am, 3 pm, 11 pm) for five days.  4 Don't take it when you haven't eaten – always eat something first.
 3   Practise – sample with label card A
"This is an antibiotic. You take one capsule every eight hours – so three times a day: in the morning, in the afternoon and at night. Always after food. It's for six days, and this is important: finish all of them, even if you feel better after three days. One more thing: don't take it with milk. I'll write it on the box in big letters."
 3R   Perform
Card A big letters: 1 CAPSULE – EVERY 8 HOURS – AFTER FOOD – 6 DAYS – FINISH ALL – NO MILK. Card B: 10 ml – 3 TIMES A DAY – AFTER FOOD – MAX 5 DAYS – FRIDGE – DON'T DRIVE.
 4   Your turn – sample
"This letter is from my son's school. The children have to bring sports clothes every Tuesday and a towel on Thursdays for swimming. Parents have to sign the paper and send it back before Sunday. There's also a trip on the 12th and it costs 20,000."Question I couldn't answer: "Do they need a swimming hat?" – "I don't know. I'll ask the teacher tomorrow."
 4R   Report – sample
"Hiba explained a letter from her son's school. The children have to bring sports clothes every Tuesday and a swimming towel on Thursdays. Parents have to sign the paper and send it back before Sunday. She didn't know if they need a swimming hat, so she's going to ask the teacher."
 5   Read
1 Three.  2 Stop and see a doctor.  3 Your doctor or pharmacist – especially if you are pregnant, if you take other medicines, or if you have any questions.
 6   Write with help – sample
THROATCARE – FOR SORE THROATS  LozengesHow to take it: Adults: one lozenge every four hours. Suck it slowly; do not swallow it whole. Do not take more than six in 24 hours.For how long: Up to four days. If your throat still hurts after four days, stop and see a doctor.Important:  • Do not give it to children under six.  • Do not take it with other throat medicines.  • Keep it away from children.Ask your doctor or pharmacist if you take other medicines or if you have any questions.Al-Shifa Pharmacy · Al-Ward Street, Mezzeh · 0933 427 815
 6P   Check together – sample
Checklist | ✓ / ✗
Capitals and full stops | ✓
Imperatives (Take… / Do not take…) | ✗ – You taking one lozenge → Take one lozenge
How much · how often · when · how long | ✓
The "ask a professional" line | ✓
 6R   Share – sample
Before: You taking one lozenge every four hours.  →  After: Take one lozenge every four hours.
 7   Write freely – sample (≈95 words)
HOW TO USE THE WASHING MACHINE (Flat 7)Before you start: Check the pockets – Lama's keys went round fifty times last month. Close the door until you hear "click".How to use it: Put the powder in the left box and the softener in the middle one. For normal clothes choose programme 3 (40°, 1 hour 20).
