For towels and sheets choose programme 5 (60°, 2 hours).Important:  • Do not open the door when the machine is working.  • Do not put more than 6 kg inside.  • Turn the tap off after you finish.If there is a problem: if water stays inside, don't open the door – call the owner, Abu Nabil: 0955 300 214.
 7P   Respond – sample slip
★ Very clear – and the keys story made me laugh!   ✓ Can you add where the powder is?   ? How often should I clean the filter?
 7R   Share – sample
A sheet is good if a stranger can follow it without asking anything. Ask the class: which question did you still have?
 HW   Homework – sample instructions for a child
Washing machine – for Yazan (10 years old)  1 Look in the pockets. Take out everything! 2 Put the clothes in and close the door – listen for the click. 3 Put one spoon of powder in the left box. 4 Press number 3, then press START. 5 Don't open the door. The machine finishes in one hour and twenty minutes. If something is strange, call me first! ❤

10.5  I try to walk every day
 1   Get ready
We SAY… | We WRITE…
I try to walk every day. | I don't drink enough water.
I need to sleep seven hours. | Most students sleep five or six hours.
I spend too much time on my phone. | Four people do no exercise at all.
The truth is… | Our conclusion: we need to walk more.
b  I don't sleep enough. · That's too much coffee. · I don't move enough.
 2   Listen and read
a  A (a class survey)   b  1 He tries to walk home from the bank twice a week (forty minutes).  2 She wants to go to the gym, but she says she has no time – the truth is she spends too much time on her phone.
Language box – Practise:  1 to walk  2 too much  3 enough  4 to take  5 too many
 3   Practise – sample with survey card A
A | Do you sleep enough?
B | No. I need to sleep seven hours, but I sleep six, because I work late.
A | Do you exercise?
B | I try to walk at the weekend, but I don't do anything in the week.
A | Do you drink enough water?
B | No, I drink too much tea – about eight glasses a day!
A | What do you want to change?
B | I'd like to swim again. I swam every summer when I was younger.
 3R   Perform
Card A: habit – walks at the weekend; problem – sleeps 6 hours / drinks tea not water. Card B: habit – sleeps 8 hours and drinks water; problem – no exercise at all.
 4   Your turn – sample group table
Question | Hiba | Fadi | Tareq | Rasha
Do you sleep enough? | no – 6 h | yes – 8 h | no – 5 h | no – 6 h
Do you exercise? | walks to college | no time | walks home 2×/week | with her children
Do you drink enough water? | no | yes | no – 5 coffees | yes
Do you take breaks at work? | no | yes | no | yes
Time outside every day? | 30 min | 10 min | 45 min | 2 hours
 4R   Report – sample
"In our group nobody sleeps enough – between five and six and a half hours. Two people try to walk every day. Three people drink too much coffee. Everybody wants to spend less time on their phone, but nobody knows how!"
 5   Read
1 Only two.  2 "I don't have time."  3 We don't need a gym; we need to walk, to drink water and to put our phones in another room.
 6   Write with help – sample
Group 2 – How healthy are we?   This week we asked four students about their everyday habits. The results are interesting.   Sleep. Nobody sleeps enough. Most students sleep six hours, and only one person sleeps eight.   Exercise. Half of the group walk every day. Two people do no exercise at all.   Water and coffee. Two people drink enough water; one person drinks five coffees a day.   What we want to change. Everybody wants to spend less time on the phone.   Our conclusion: we need to walk more and sleep one hour earlier.
 6P   Check together – sample
Checklist | ✓ / ✗
Capitals and full stops | ✓
nobody / everybody + -s | ✗ – Everybody want → Everybody wants
enough / too much / too many | ✓
All parts | ✓
 6R   Share – sample
Before: Everybody want to change something.  →  After: Everybody wants to change something.
 7   Write freely – sample (≈95 words)
Group 3 – How healthy are we?We asked four students five questions about their habits. Some answers surprised us.Sleep. Nobody in our group sleeps eight hours. Three people sleep five or six, and the reasons are work, study and small children.Exercise. Two people walk every day, one walks twice a week, and one does nothing at all. Nobody goes to a gym – it's too expensive.Water and coffee. Two of us drink enough water. Tareq drinks five coffees a day and then he can't sleep!Time outside. Rasha spends two hours outside with her children. The rest of us spend less than thirty minutes.Our conclusion: we don't need a gym – we need to go outside more and drink less coffee after four o'clock.
 7P   Respond – sample slip
★ Your conclusion is true for me too – especially the coffee!   ✓ Can you add how many people take breaks at work?   ? How many hours does Rasha sleep?
 7R   Share – sample
Typical class conclusion: Nobody sleeps enough, everybody sits too much, and the phone is the biggest problem.
 HW   Homework – sample personal plan
This month I want to sleep one hour more. I'm going to put my phone in the kitchen at ten o'clock, not next to my bed. I need to stop drinking coffee after four – I'll drink water or tea with no sugar. I'm also going to walk home from the bank twice a week, on Monday and Wednesday. In four weeks I'll read this again and see.

10.6  Get well soon!
 1   Get ready
We SAY… | We WRITE…
Get well soon! | I hope you feel better soon.
How are you feeling? | The whole class is thinking of you.
Is there anything I can do? | Please don't worry.
Take care of yourself. | If anything changes, I will call you.
b  To the ill person: Get well soon · I hope you feel better · Take care of yourself.   To the family: How is he today? · Is there anything I can do? (both are possible).
 2   Listen and read
a  B (at Rami's flat)   b  1 It wasn't a simple cold; she gave him something and told him to rest for five days.  2 Soup (Lina's mother made it) and a card from the class.
Language box – Practise (sample answers):  1 I'm sorry to hear that – get well soon!  2 I'm sorry to hear about your mother. I hope she gets better soon. Is there anything I can do?  3 How are you feeling? Take care of yourself and don't get up too early.  4 I hope your voice comes back soon! Shall I read the text for the class?
 3   Practise – sample with card A
A | How are you feeling?
B | A little better, thank you. But I'm so bored!
A | I'm sorry to hear that. What did the doctor say?
B | Rest for three more days.
A | Is there anything I can do? Shall I bring you some books from the centre?
B | That's very kind of you. Yes, please!
A | Take care of yourself. And get well soon!
 3R   Perform
All six phrases should appear. The key teaching point is the voice: soft and falling, not fast and bright.
 4   Your turn – sample
"When my neighbour broke her arm, the women in the building organised everything. One cooked on Monday, one took the children to school, one did the shopping on Thursdays. Nobody asked her – they just made a list and told her."
 4R   Report – sample
"When Hiba's neighbour broke her arm, the women in the building made a list: one cooked on Monday, one took the children to school, one did the shopping. Hiba says nobody asked – they just organised it."
Teaching point: the most useful offer is concrete ("I'll bring bread on Tuesday"), not general ("tell me if you need anything").
 5   Read
1 Don't come back before the doctor says yes – they can wait.  2 Because Rami's mother lives in Homs and will worry; Lina gives her the news and her number.  3 That she will call immediately if anything changes.
 6   Write with help – samples
Card: GET WELL SOON, HIBA! Everybody in Class 4B is thinking of you. The lessons are much quieter without your questions – and nobody else brings sweets! Rest properly and don't come back before you feel strong. With best wishes from all of us. 🌼
Message to the family: Dear Umm Hiba, I hope you are well. I wanted to tell you that we visited Hiba this evening. She is much better than yesterday – she was sitting up and complaining about the food, which is a very good sign! The doctor gave her something and told her to rest until Sunday, and her flatmate is looking after her well. Please don't worry. If anything changes, I will call you immediately. My number is 0991 445 360. With warm regards, Rasha
 6P   Check together – sample
Checklist | ✓ / ✗
Capitals and full stops | ✓
Get well soon / I hope you feel better | ✗ – I hope you will feeling better → I hope you feel better
Good news first | ✓
A promise to call + a number | ✓
 6R   Share – sample
Before: I hope you will feeling better soon.  →  After: I hope you feel better soon.
 7   Write freely – sample (≈85 words)
(a) Card from the officeGET WELL SOON, TAREQ! The whole branch is thinking of you. Nobody can find anything in your drawer and the coffee machine misses you. Rest properly – the end-of-month accounts can wait (we said that, and we mean it). With best wishes from everybody at Baramkeh branch.(b) Message to his wifeDear Lamia, I hope you are well. I wanted to tell you that we spoke to Tareq this morning and he sounded much better than on Sunday. The doctor is happy with him and he says he can go back to work next week – but we told him not to hurry. If you need anything at all, or if you want somebody to bring shopping, please call me: 0944 318 260. With warm regards, Omar
 7P   Respond – sample slip (as the family member)
★ This made me feel better – thank you for writing.   ✓ Can you add when his colleagues will visit?   ? Is he eating properly at lunch?
 7R   Share – sample
The line that makes an ill person smile is usually the personal joke: the empty drawer, the quiet classroom, the coffee machine.
 HW   Homework – sample family reply
Dear Omar, Thank you so much for your message – it was very kind. Tareq is eating better and he slept well last night. Could you tell me which day the branch meeting is? He worries about it. And if you are near the pharmacy on Thursday, could you bring his medicine? I can't leave the children. Thank you again. Lamia

10.7  Review & Mediate: What does the box say?
 1   Get ready – quiz
1 I've got a headache.  2 My feet hurt.  3 You should rest.  4 I'd like to make an appointment.  5 I don't drink enough water.  6 I hope you feel better.
 2   Listen and read
b  "VITAMIN – AFTER LUNCH" in big letters on every Friday.
 3   Practise – sample with card A (eye drops)
"Let's do it step by step. These are drops for your eyes.Two drops in each eye – so four drops altogether – three times a day.This is important: don't touch your eye with the bottle. Hold it a little above the eye, like this.And one more thing: write today's date on the box. Four weeks after you open it, we throw it away, even if there is medicine inside.If your eye is red after two days, we don't buy another box – we go back to the doctor. Shall I write all this in big letters?"
 3R   Perform
Card A big letters: 2 DROPS EACH EYE – 3 TIMES A DAY – DON'T TOUCH THE EYE – THROW AWAY AFTER 4 WEEKS. Card B: SIT 5 MINUTES – BAND ABOVE THE ELBOW – DON'T TALK – WRITE THE 2 NUMBERS EVERY MORNING – BOOK TO THE DOCTOR EVERY MONTH.
 4   Your turn – sample
"I explained the bank app to my father. First you open the app and you put your number. Then a code comes by message, and you have two minutes to write it. After that you see your account. He always forgets the code is only for two minutes, so I wrote the steps on a card and put it in his wallet."
 4R   Report – mediation sample
"Tareq explained the bank app to his father. First you open the app and you put your number. The code comes by message and it only works for two minutes. His father always forgets the code, so Tareq wrote the steps on a card and put it in his wallet."
What makes an explanation clear: one step at a time · a comparison with something known · a written reminder · and saying what to do when it goes wrong.
 5   Read – A2 Key Reading Part 3
1 B  2 A  3 B  4 B  5 B
 6   Write with help – sample
TETA – YOUR TWO BOXES   Box 1 (throat): One tablet every 4 hours. Never more than 6 a day. Suck it slowly, like a sweet – do not swallow it whole. For 3–4 days only.   Box 2 (vitamin): One capsule once a week – Friday! Take it with food, after lunch. I wrote it on your calendar.   Important: if your throat still hurts after four days, we go back to the doctor – we don't buy another box.   Call me any time, Teta. ❤ Maya · 0933 427 815
 6P   Check together – sample
Checklist | ✓ / ✗
Both boxes explained simply | ✓
How much · how often · when · how long | ✓
A warning + when to go back to the doctor | ✓
A phone number | ✗ – add it at the end
 7   Write freely – A2 Key Writing Part 6 sample (≈45 words)
Hi Tom,I'm sorry you're ill – get well soon! In class today we practised making appointments and we started Unit 11. The homework is the writing task on page 24.Shall I bring you my notes on Saturday? I can also explain the new grammar.Take care,Lama
Marking note: all three content points (sorry, what you did in class, an offer of help) must be present for full content marks.
 ✓   Unit 10 check
A  1 b  2 b  3 a  4 a  5 b  6 b  7 a  8 a
B  1 B  2 B  3 B  4 C  5 C
Note for question 4: the pharmacist says driving is fine and doesn't mention coffee – the thing the customer must not do is stop early. Students who chose A heard "Can I drive?" and stopped listening to the answer.
C  Sample message (≈45 words):
Hi Rana, I'm so sorry to hear you're ill. How are you feeling today? Please don't worry about the Thursday report – I finished it this morning and sent it to Mr Khaled. I pass your building every evening, so shall I bring you bread and soup tomorrow at six? Lina
Scoring guide: A = 8 marks, B = 5 marks, C = 5 marks (content 2, organisation 1, language 2). Total 18. 13+ = ready for Unit 11.

Unit 11  Travel & Holidays — Answer Key
Proactive English · A2 · Book 1 · Syria Edition · Answer Key
How to use this keyEvery task has an answer or a full sample, including brainstorms, free speaking, reports, peer-review slips and homework. Samples are models of what a good A2 student can produce – not the only right answer.Where students invent their own content, accept any answer that uses the target language correctly and answers the task.

11.1  We're going to spend a week in Latakia
Stage 1 · Get ready
a Typical board answers: the sea (Latakia, Tartus, Banias) · the mountains (Bludan, Slunfeh, Kassab) · relatives in another city · the village in the summer · Damascus or Aleppo for a few days. Accept any place with a reason.
b Open. In most classes the top three are visit relatives · swim · rest. Accept the class's own result.
Stage 2 · Listen and read
a B – next month.
b 1 Maya, Lama and Lina (Omar comes for the weekend only).   2 Rami isn't going to take a holiday this year – he's going to work.
Language box · Practise
1 I 'm going to visit my cousins next month.   2 She isn't going to take a holiday.   3 What are you going to do in the summer?   4 We 're going to travel by bus.   5 They aren't going to fly – it's too expensive.
Stage 3 · Practise (sample – Trip card A)
Sample answerA: So it's decided: we're going to spend three days in Bludan in August.B: Who's going?A: Me and my two cousins. My brother is going to join us for Friday only.B: What are we going to do there?A: We're going to walk in the mountains and drink tea outside.B: Where are we going to stay?A: We're going to stay in my uncle's house.B: And how are we going to travel?A: By car.
Stage 3R · Perform – listener task (sample)
going to spend three days in Bludan · going to walk in the mountains · going to stay in my uncle's house · going to travel by car.
Stage 4 · Your turn (sample)
Sample answerIn July I'm going to visit my family in Tartus. I'm going to stay for ten days. I'm going to swim every morning and help my father in the shop in the afternoon. My cousins are going to come from Homs for the last weekend. I'm not going to take my laptop – I need a real holiday.Partner's questions: How are you going to travel? Who are you going to stay with? Are you going to see your old school friends?
Stage 4R · Report (sample)
Sample answerFadi is going to visit his family in Tartus in July. He's going to stay for ten days with his parents. He's going to swim every morning and help his father in the shop. His cousins are going to come for the last weekend. He isn't going to take his laptop, because he says he needs a real holiday.
Board clinic – typical mistakes
✗ I going to visit → ✓ I 'm going to visit.   ✗ We going to booking → ✓ We're going to book.   ✗ He don't going to come → ✓ He isn't going to come.
Stage 5 · Read
1 They're going to travel on Saturday 15 November on the morning bus (it leaves at eight), and come back on Saturday 22nd in the evening.
2 She's going to call two hotels and tell them the prices.
3 They aren't going to plan every day.
Stage 6 · Write with help (sample)
Sample answerAleppo – two days 🏛Hi everyone! Here's the plan for the first weekend in March.We're going to travel on Friday morning (the 6th) and come back on Saturday evening.We're going to stay in a small hotel near the centre. It's about 250,000 a night.We're going to see the citadel, walk in the souq and eat kebab. We aren't going to visit five museums in two days.Can everybody confirm by Monday?Rana
Stage 7 · Write freely (sample, 78 words)
Sample answerA day in Maaloula 🚐Hi all! Here's the plan for Friday.We're going to leave from the university gate at seven in the morning in Samir's car and come back at about eight in the evening.We aren't going to book a hotel – it's one day only.We're going to visit the old churches, walk up to the top, and have lunch in the village. In the afternoon we're going to sit and drink tea.We aren't going to run from place to place.Can everybody tell me by Wednesday? I need to know how many we are.Nour
Stage 7P · Respond (sample slip)
★ I'm coming! The timing is perfect.   ✓ Can you add how much the petrol is going to cost each?   ? What time are we going to be back exactly – I have a class at nine.
Homework (sample reply, 41 words)
Sample answerHi Nour, yes, I'm coming! Friday is free for me and I love Maaloula. Are we going to take our own food or buy lunch in the village? I'm going to bring tea, cups and water for everybody – that's my job.

11.2  I'd like to book a double room
Stage 1 · Get ready
a You ask: Have you got a room? How much is it? Is breakfast included? Have you got parking / Wi-Fi? Can I pay when I arrive? What time is check-in?   The hotel asks: For how many nights? What kind of room? How many people? Can I take your name and phone number?
b single = one bed for one person · double = one big bed · twin = two separate beds · half board = breakfast and dinner · full board = all three meals · breakfast included = breakfast only.
Stage 2 · Listen and read
a B – two rooms.
b 1 320,000 a night (for a week; the normal price is 350,000).   2 Breakfast – made by his wife, and "nobody leaves hungry". (Accept: a better price for a week.)
Language box · Practise
1 for three nights.   2 300,000 a night.   3 from Monday to Friday.   4 Is breakfast included?   5 What time can we check in?
Stage 3 · Practise (sample – Hotel card A)
Sample answerB: Hotel Al-Qalaa, good morning.A: Good morning. I'd like to book a double room, please, from the 3rd of May to the 6th.B: Three nights. What kind of room?A: A double, please. Have you got anything with a balcony?B: Let me look… Yes, I have one on the third floor.A: How much is it per night?B: 250,000, and 40,000 more for the balcony – so 290,000. Breakfast is included.A: Can I pay when I arrive? What time can we check in?B: Cash on arrival, please. Check-in is from two and check-out is at twelve.
Stage 3R · Perform – listener task (sample)
3–6 May, 3 nights · double room with balcony · 290,000 a night · breakfast included · check-in from 14:00 · pay cash on arrival.
Stage 4 · Your turn (sample for b – something changes)
Sample answerA: Hello, this is Rana Khoury. I booked a double room for the 3rd to the 6th of May. I'm very sorry, but can I change it? We need one more night – to the 7th.B: Let me look… Yes, that's fine. The 3rd to the 7th, four nights.A: Is the price the same?B: Yes, 290,000 a night.A: Thank you very much. See you in May.
Stage 4R · Report (sample)
Sample answerI booked a double room with a balcony in Aleppo from the 3rd to the 6th of May – three nights, 290,000 a night with breakfast. Then my brother decided to come, so I phoned again and added one night. Check-in is at two and I'm going to pay cash when I arrive.
Board clinic – typical mistakes
✗ I want book a room → ✓ I'd like to book a room.   ✗ from 15 until 22 → ✓ from the 15th to the 22nd.   ✗ How much the room? → ✓ How much is the room?   ✗ I stay 7 days = 6 nights – always count the nights.
Stage 5 · Read
1 Four – two sisters and two friends.
2 The price per night; whether breakfast is included; whether she can pay on arrival.
3 They can leave their bags at the hotel.
Stage 6 · Write with help (sample)
Sample answerSubject: Booking request – 3–6 May (1 room)Dear Sir or Madam,I would like to book one double room from Friday 3 May to Monday 6 May – three nights.We would like a room with a balcony if possible. There are two of us.Could you confirm the price per night and tell me if breakfast is included? We would also like to know if we can pay when we arrive.My phone number is 0944 312 876.Thank you very much.Best regards, Rana Khoury
Stage 7 · Write freely (sample, 86 words)
Sample answerSubject: Booking request – 12–15 August (2 rooms)Dear Sir or Madam,I would like to book two rooms at your guesthouse from Monday 12 August to Thursday 15 August – three nights.We would like one double room and one twin room. There are four of us: my wife, our two children and me. The children are nine and twelve.Could you confirm the price per room per night and tell me if breakfast is included? We would also like to know if there is parking for one car.My phone number is 0955 220 441.Thank you very much.Best regards, Samer Haddad
Stage 7P · Respond – the hotel's reply (sample)
Sample answerSubject: RE: Booking request – 12–15 August (2 rooms)Dear Mr Haddad,Thank you for your email. We are happy to confirm one double room and one twin room from 12 to 15 August, three nights.The price is 230,000 SYP per room per night and breakfast is included. There is free parking in front of the guesthouse.Check-in is from 2 pm and check-out is at 11 am. The children can use the garden at any time.We look forward to welcoming you.Best regards, Hala Ibrahim · Cham Guesthouse · 043 221 700
Homework (sample, 38 words)
Sample answerSubject: Change to our booking (12–15 August)Dear Ms Ibrahim,I am sorry to write again. Could we stay one more night, from 12 August to 16 August – four nights? Everything else is the same.Thank you very much. Best regards, Samer Haddad

11.3  Is there a lift?
Stage 1 · Get ready
a Open – the class's own order. A common result: price · breakfast · a quiet room · near the centre · Wi-Fi · air conditioning · a view · parking.
b Is there a restaurant? · Are there any shops near the hotel? · Is there air conditioning in the rooms? · Are there towels in the room?
Stage 2 · Listen and read
a A – Hotel Al-Bahr.
b 1 Al-Bahr is cheaper (320,000 against 480,000).   2 The Blue Coast is nearer the centre and has a lift, a pool and a restaurant – but Lama prefers quiet, and she would rather spend the money on fish than on a pool.
Language box · Practise
1 Is there a lift?   2 Are there any shops near the hotel?   3 Is there Wi-Fi in the rooms?   4 Is there parking?   5 Do the rooms have air conditioning? (or: Is there air conditioning in the rooms?)
Stage 3 · Practise (sample – Compare card A)
Sample answerB: Is there a lift in the City Inn?   A: Yes, there is. The rooms are on the fourth floor.B: Is breakfast included?   A: No, it isn't. In the Garden House it is.B: Are there any quiet rooms in the City Inn?   A: Not really – it's on a noisy street.B: Does the Garden House have parking?   A: Yes, it does.B: Is there a lift in the Garden House?   A: No, but the rooms are on the ground floor.B: Which is cheaper?   A: The City Inn – 200,000 against 300,000.Decision: We choose the Garden House. First, the rooms are bigger and quieter, and they're on the ground floor. Second, breakfast is included, so it isn't really more expensive.
Stage 3R · Perform – listener task (sample)
Garden House: (1) quieter and bigger rooms on the ground floor, (2) breakfast included so the real price is similar.
Stage 4 · Your turn (sample for b)
Sample answerA: Your guesthouse has no lift but the rooms are on the ground floor, so it's fine for an older person. Mine is on the fourth floor with a lift.B: But your hotel is in the centre, so students can walk everywhere and spend nothing on taxis.A: And mine has no garden. For a family with children, your guesthouse is better.Together: Families → the guesthouse. Students → the city hotel. Older people → the guesthouse, ground floor.
Stage 4R · Report (sample)
Sample answerHiba stayed in a guesthouse in Bludan. There isn't a lift and the rooms are small, but there's a big garden and the breakfast is famous. It's cheaper than my hotel in Tartus, but my hotel is nearer the sea. We decided that the guesthouse is better for families and my hotel is better for young people.
Board clinic – typical mistakes
✗ Is there shops? → ✓ Are there any shops?   ✗ Have it Wi-Fi? → ✓ Does it have Wi-Fi? / Has it got Wi-Fi?   ✗ It's more cheap → ✓ It's cheaper.   ✗ more quiet → ✓ quieter.
Stage 5 · Read
1 Twenty minutes on foot.   2 The breakfast (fresh bread, olives, cheese, eggs, tea on the terrace).   3 People who want a modern hotel with a swimming pool.
Stage 6 · Write with help (sample)
Sample answerGARDEN HOUSE, BLUDAN ⭐⭐ Our review: 7/10Garden House is a small guesthouse in Bludan, fifteen minutes from the centre.The rooms are big and quiet, on the ground floor, with air conditioning. There isn't a lift, but you don't need one.Breakfast is included and there is a lot of it.What isn't here: there's no restaurant in the evening and the Wi-Fi is slow.Price: from 300,000 a night.Perfect for: families with children.  Not for: people who want to go out at night.
Stage 7 · Write freely (sample, 95 words)
Sample answerAL-MADINA HOTEL, HOMS ⭐⭐⭐ Our review: 7/10Al-Madina is a medium-sized hotel five minutes on foot from the clock tower, in the middle of everything.The rooms are clean and warm in winter, and they all have air conditioning and good Wi-Fi. There is a lift. Ask for a room at the back: the rooms on the street are noisy until midnight.Breakfast is simple – bread, cheese, jam and tea – but it starts at six, which is good if you have an early bus.What isn't here: there's no parking, no restaurant and no hot water between two and four in the afternoon.Price: from 190,000 SYP a night.Perfect for: people travelling for work and for one or two nights.  Not for: families with small children.
Stage 7P · Respond (sample slip)
★ I trust this review – you say what is wrong!   ✓ Can you add if there is parking near the hotel?   ? Is it noisy on Friday night too?
Homework (sample, 42 words)
Sample answerAl-Madina Hotel – for a family. The hotel is clean and in the centre, but I don't recommend it for a family. The rooms are small, there is no garden and no parking for your car, and the street is noisy until midnight. Choose a hotel outside the centre.

11.4  It's going to rain
Stage 1 · Get ready
a Open. Typical: Damascus – hot and dry in summer (35–40°), cold at night in winter, a little rain. Latakia – warm and wet. Bludan and Slunfeh – snow in winter. Deir ez-Zor – very hot.
b Open – the class's own three words for today.
Stage 2 · Listen and read
a A – the weather is going to be bad in Latakia (at the weekend).
b 1 Saturday and Sunday are going to be wet and windy; then sunny all week.   2 A jacket, her book – and (on Maya's advice) an umbrella.
Language box · Practise
1 it's going to snow   2 it's going to rain   3 it's going to be   4 it's going to be   5 itisn't going to rain (= it's not going to rain)
Stage 3 · Practise (sample – Forecast card A, Aleppo)
Sample answerA: It's going to be sunny on Monday, twenty-two degrees. We don't need jackets.B: But look at Wednesday – rain all day, fifteen degrees.A: So we're going to need an umbrella and something warm for Wednesday.B: What are we going to do on Wednesday? We can't walk round the citadel in the rain.A: We're going to go to the museum in the morning and sit in a café in the afternoon.B: Good. And on Thursday and Friday it's going to be sunny and twenty-four, so we're going to do the old city then.
Stage 3R · Perform – listener task (sample)
Take: an umbrella, a warm jacket, comfortable shoes. Bad-day plan: the museum in the morning and a café in the afternoon.
Stage 4 · Your turn (sample for a)
Sample answerIn my city, spring is the best season: it's warm in the day and cool at night, and everything is green. Summer is very hot and dry – about thirty-eight degrees – so people go out in the evening. Autumn is short and beautiful. In winter it's cold, about five degrees at night, and it rains, but it doesn't snow. My favourite is spring, because you can sit outside all day.
Stage 4R · Report (sample)
Sample answerTareq says Suwayda is beautiful in spring – warm in the day and cool at night. In winter it's very cold and sometimes it snows. If I visit him in December, I'm going to need a heavy coat and good shoes, and he says we're going to sit by the stove and eat all day.
Board clinic – typical mistakes
✗ It's rainy now, look! → ✓ It's raining now.   ✗ Tomorrow it rains → ✓ Tomorrow it's going to rain.   ✗ There is cold → ✓ It's cold.   ✗ twenty degree → ✓ twenty degrees.
Stage 5 · Read
1 Saturday – rain, wind and only 17 degrees.   2 A jacket and an umbrella for the first two days, and swimming things for the rest of the week.   3 They're going to sit on the balcony with tea and watch the rain on the sea.
Stage 6 · Write with help (sample)
Sample answerHi everyone! Here's the forecast for our weekend in Bludan.The first day is going to be cold – Friday is the worst: wind and only eight degrees. But from Saturday it's going to be sunny, between twelve and fifteen degrees.So: bring a heavy jacket and a hat for Friday, and good shoes for the walk on Saturday.And don't worry about Friday. We're going to stay inside, play cards and cook.
Stage 7 · Write freely (sample, 82 words)
Sample answerHi Sara! Here's the weather for your week in Damascus. ☀️The first two days are going to be the difficult ones – Monday and Tuesday are going to be very hot, about thirty-eight degrees, and dry. From Wednesday it's going to be cooler, between thirty and thirty-two, with some wind in the evening.So: bring light clothes, a hat and a bottle of water for every day. The nights are about twenty degrees, so you don't need a jacket.And don't worry about the hot days. We're going to stay inside between twelve and four, and go out in the evening like everybody here.
Stage 7P · Respond (sample slip)
★ Now I know what to pack!   ✓ Can you add the temperature at night?   ? Do I need a coat or just a jacket in the evening?
Homework (sample, 34 words)
Sample answerThanks! I'm going to bring light clothes, a hat and my big water bottle. Is it going to be windy? And if it's too hot one afternoon, can we go to a museum or a café with air conditioning?

11.5  We're having a great time!
Stage 1 · Get ready
a Open. Typical: my mother (she worries!) · the family group · close friends · the class group. People usually send a photo, where they are, the weather, the food, and "we miss you".
b NOW: I'm sitting on the balcony · The sun is going down.   YESTERDAY: We swam this morning · We ate the best fish.
Stage 2 · Listen and read
a B – she's sitting on the hotel balcony.
b 1 They walked on the sea road for two hours and ate at a small fish restaurant.   2 Because Maya says, "Next year you're coming with us. That isn't a question." (He answers "Yes, boss.")
Language box · Practise
1 'm sitting   2 visited   3 're going   4 is shining   5 swam
Stage 3 · Practise (sample – Holiday card A, Aleppo)
Sample answerB: Where are you? What can I hear?A: I'm sitting in a café in the old souq. Look – can you see the ceiling?B: How is it? Tell me everything.A: It's wonderful. We're having a great time. Yesterday we visited the citadel and we walked for five hours.B: Five hours!A: This morning we ate the famous kebab. Tonight we're meeting an old friend from university.B: And what's the weather like?A: Sunny, twenty-four degrees. Perfect.
Stage 3R · Perform – listener task (sample)
Now: sitting in a café in the souq. Yesterday: visited the citadel, walked five hours. Tonight: meeting an old friend.
Stage 4 · Your turn (sample)
Sample answerI'm in Tartus. Right now I'm walking on the corniche and the sun is going down – it's orange on the water. Yesterday we took a boat to Arwad and we ate fish on the island. This morning I slept until ten, which never happens at home. Tonight we're going to my aunt's house for dinner, and tomorrow we're going to the old city.
Stage 4R · Report (sample)
Sample answerHiba is in Tartus. Right now she's walking on the corniche and watching the sunset. Yesterday she took a boat to Arwad and ate fish on the island. This morning she slept until ten, and tonight she's going to her aunt's house for dinner.
Board clinic – typical mistakes
✗ Now I sit on the balcony → ✓ Now I'm sitting.   ✗ Yesterday we are walking → ✓ Yesterday we walked.   ✗ I am having a sister → ✓ I have a sister (but We're having a great time is correct!).   ✗ We eat fish yesterday → ✓ We ate fish yesterday.
Stage 5 · Read
1 Text 1 is for Teta (an older relative) – warmer, complete sentences, Dear… With all our love. Text 2 is for the class group – short lines, jokes, capital letters for emphasis (NOVEMBER), emojis, no Dear.
2 They're going to take a boat, if the weather stays good.
3 Something from the market.
Stage 6 · Write with help (sample)
Sample answerDear Grandma,Greetings from Tartus! I'm sitting on the corniche and the sun is going down. The weather is warm and there is a light wind.Yesterday we took a boat to Arwad and we ate fish on the island. This morning I slept until ten.Tonight we're going to Aunt Nadia's house for dinner.We're thinking of you and we're going to bring you sweets from the old city.With love, Hiba
Stage 7 · Write freely (sample, 112 words for the two texts)
Sample answer(a) Postcard to my grandmotherDear Teta,Greetings from Tartus! I'm sitting on the corniche with a cup of tea and the sun is going down over the sea. It's twenty-four degrees, warm but not hot.Yesterday we took the boat to Arwad and we ate fish on the island. This morning I slept until ten – don't tell my mother!Tonight we're going to Aunt Nadia's house, and tomorrow we're going to walk in the old city.We're thinking of you every day and we're going to bring you sweets.With all our love, Hiba(b) Group message to friendsHi everyone! 🌊 We're having the best time. Right now I'm on the corniche, sun going down, tea in my hand, zero problems.Yesterday: Arwad, fish, sunburn. 😎 This morning: slept till TEN.Tonight: dinner at my aunt's (= too much food). Tomorrow: old city.Rami, we talked about you again. Next year you're coming. See you Sunday! 😄
Stage 7P · Respond (sample slip, as the relative)
★ This made my day – I can see the sea from your words!   ✓ Can you add how the food is and if you are eating enough?   ? When are you coming home?
Homework (sample reply, 44 words)
Sample answerMy dear Hiba, your card made me very happy. I read it twice. Are you sleeping well? And is the sea cold now? Here everything is quiet. Your uncle painted the kitchen and now the whole house smells of paint. Come back safely. Teta

11.6  You should visit the old city
Stage 1 · Get ready
a Open. A typical class top five: Old Damascus and the Umayyad Mosque · Palmyra · Aleppo citadel · Krak des Chevaliers · Maaloula / Bosra / the coast.
b Open – accept any superlative that is formed correctly.
Stage 2 · Listen and read
a B – in the spring (April).
b 1 Start early in the Old City, before nine, when the streets are empty and the light is beautiful; the souq in the afternoon.   2 Go to Souq Al-Hamidiyah in the afternoon, when it's full of people – that's the real experience – and eat the ice cream.
Language box · Practise (samples)
1 You should go to the Old City early in the morning, because it's quiet and the light is good for photos.
2 You should go to the park on Friday, when there are families and music.
3 You should book the fish restaurant first, because it's very popular at the weekend.
Stage 3 · Practise (sample – Visitor card A: two days, old buildings, no crowds, alone)
Sample answerYou should start in the Old City very early – before eight the streets are empty and the light is perfect for photos.You should see the Umayyad Mosque, but go when it opens, not at midday.You shouldn't go to the souq on Thursday evening – it's the busiest time of the week.On the second day you should go to Maaloula. It's only an hour away and it's very quiet.You should eat in the small places in the Old City, because you can sit alone and nobody disturbs you.And don't carry your big camera in a crowd – use the small one.
Stage 3R · Perform – listener task (sample)
Old City (early, empty streets) · Umayyad Mosque (at opening time) · Maaloula (quiet, one hour away) · small restaurants (calm). Yes – the advice fits a photographer who doesn't like crowds.
Stage 4 · Your turn (sample)
Sample answerDay 1: you should walk in the old market in the morning, when the shops are opening and everybody is friendly. You shouldn't go in the afternoon – it's too hot and too full.Day 2: you should take a car to the villages outside the city. The view from the hill is the best in our region.Day 3: you should rest, buy presents and eat in my aunt's restaurant – it's the cheapest good food in the city.And you shouldn't drive in the centre. There is nowhere to park.
Stage 4R · Report (sample)
Sample answerFadi says I should go to Tartus in June, not August, because August is too crowded. I should eat fish at the port, but in a small place, not in the big restaurants. On the second day I should take the boat to Arwad early in the morning. And he says I shouldn't drive, because the parking is impossible.
Board clinic – typical mistakes
✗ You should to visit → ✓ You should visit.   ✗ the most old city → ✓ the oldest city.   ✗ the more beautiful place → ✓ the most beautiful place.   ✗ You should going → ✓ You should go.
Stage 5 · Read
1 Because before nine the streets are empty and the light is perfect for photos.
2 So that he doesn't have to carry them for a week.
3 People inviting you for coffee – and saying yes.
Stage 6 · Write with help (sample)
Sample answerTHREE DAYS IN HOMS – a short guide for AnnaDay 1 · The city centre. Start at the clock tower in the morning, because the cafés are full and it's the best place to see normal life here.Day 2 · Krak des Chevaliers. One hour by car. Go early and stay for lunch in the village below.Day 3 · The market and the river. Take a hat – there is no shade.Eating: small places, not hotels. Ask for kibbeh and ask the people next to you what they are eating.One last thing: say yes when somebody invites you for coffee. That's the real visit.
Stage 7 · Write freely (sample, 138 words)
Sample answerTHREE DAYS IN LATAKIA – a short guide for a visitorDay 1 · The city and the sea road. Start with a walk on the sea road before the heat, then have breakfast in a small place near the port. In the afternoon you should sit in a café and do nothing – that's what people here do. Don't try to see "everything" on the first day.Day 2 · The old port and the market. Go to the fish market early, at about seven, when the boats come back. The market in the afternoon is the best place for presents, but it's crowded, so keep your bag in front of you.Day 3 · Kassab and the mountains. One hour by car. Take a jacket – it's cooler up there even in summer – and stay for lunch in a village restaurant.Eating: fish at the port, but choose the small places. Ask the owner what is fresh today and order that.One last thing: don't plan every hour. People are going to invite you for tea, and you should say yes.
Stage 7P · Respond (sample slip, as the visitor)
★ I want to do all of this, especially Day 3!   ✓ Can you add how to travel from the city to Kassab and how much it costs?   ? Is the fish market open on Fridays?
Homework (sample reply, 41 words)
Sample answerThank you so much for this! I'm most excited about Day 3 in the mountains – I love walking and cool air. Two questions: how do I get to Kassab without a car, and is there anywhere to stay there for one night?

11.7  Review & Mediate: What does the leaflet say?
Stage 1 · Unit 11 quiz
1 I 'm going to visit my uncle.   2 We're going to book a hotel.   3 Are there any shops near the hotel?   4 Look at the clouds – it's going to rain.   5 Yesterday we walked on the sea road.   6 You should visit the Old City.
Stage 2 · Listen and read
b They choose the sunset trip (Trip 2). Reasons: it's only one hour; the light is beautiful; tea and coffee are included; it's cheap; and the island trip is risky because it only runs on Fridays, needs at least six people, and they travel home on Saturday.
Stage 3 · Practise (sample – Leaflet card A, bus tours in Aleppo)
Sample answerA: There are three tours. The first one is the city centre. It takes three hours, it's every day at nine, and it costs fifty thousand.B: And the second?A: The citadel and the museum – five hours, but only Tuesday and Thursday at nine. It's ninety thousand, and the entrance tickets are included.C: What about the third?A: That's the big one: the villages outside the city, all day, Fridays only at eight. A hundred and forty thousand, with lunch.B: Is there anything we have to do first?A: Yes – you have to book two days before, and they need at least eight people. And if it rains, the tour doesn't go.C: Then let's take the citadel tour on Thursday. The entrance is included, so it isn't really more expensive, and five hours is enough for us.
Stage 3R · Perform – listener task (sample)
Tour 1: 3 h, daily, 50,000 · Tour 2: 5 h, Tue & Thu, 90,000 with entrance · Tour 3: all day, Fridays, 140,000 with lunch. Decision: Tour 2, because the entrance tickets are included and five hours is enough.
Stage 4 · Your turn (sample)
Sample answerThere are three phone packages. The first is twenty thousand a month with five gigabytes. The second is thirty-five thousand with fifteen gigabytes and free calls at night. The third is sixty thousand with unlimited internet.You have to pay at the beginning of the month, and if you finish your gigabytes you can buy more, but it's expensive. You can change the package after three months, not before.
Stage 4R · Report – mediation (sample)
Sample answerTareq explained three phone packages. The cheapest is twenty thousand a month with five gigabytes. The middle one is thirty-five thousand with fifteen gigabytes and free calls at night. The big one is sixty thousand with unlimited internet. You have to pay at the beginning of the month and you can only change after three months. I chose the middle one, because I don't need unlimited internet, but five gigabytes isn't enough for me.
NoteMediation – what to assess here. Did the student (1) give all the options, (2) explain the conditions (book before, minimum number, what happens if…), (3) use their own words rather than reading the card, and (4) give a reason for the decision? Students very often forget the conditions – that is the main teaching point of this cycle.
Stage 5 · Exam practice – A2 Key Reading Part 2
1 A  (Nabil and his wife are over seventy and can't climb stairs.)
2 B  (Dalia has very little money, wants to meet other young people and can sleep anywhere.)
3 C  (The children need space to run and the parents can sit in the café.)
4 B  (Dalia loves old buildings and photography and doesn't mind walking all day.)
5 C  (The Haddads have a car and want to see something interesting; Bosra is a long drive.)
Stage 6 · Write with help (completed)
Sample answerHi Lina!There are three boat trips here. The coast trip takes two hours and costs 60,000 each – there are two every day, at 10:00 and 16:00. The sunset trip is only one hour and it's 40,000, with tea and coffee. The island trip is seven hours, but it's on Fridays only and you have to take your own food and water.We're going to take the sunset trip because it's short, it's cheap and the light on the sea is beautiful. You have to book one day before, at the hotel reception, and they need at least six people. If the weather is bad, the trip doesn't go and we get our money back.Maya
Stage 7 · Write freely – A2 Key Writing Part 6 (samples)
Sample answerStrong sample (42 words)Hi Sam! I'm in Latakia with my sister. The weather is beautiful – twenty-two degrees and sunny. Yesterday we walked on the sea road and ate the best fish of my life. Tomorrow we're going to take a boat trip at sunset. Wish you were here!
Sample answerWeaker sample (26 words) – why it only just passesHi Sam. I am in Latakia. The weather is good. Yesterday I go to the sea. Tomorrow I visit my friend. Bye.It answers all three points and is long enough, so it passes – but: I go should be I went; I visit should be I'm going to visit; "the weather is good" gives no information; and there is no friendly opening or closing. Show students the difference between answering the three points and writing well.
Unit 11 check · A  Grammar and vocabulary
1 a are   2 b to take   3 a Is   4 c Does   5 a is going to   6 a from / to   7 b am sitting   8 a should
Unit 11 check · B  Listening (Track 11.14)
1 B near the sea   2 B the breakfast   3 A there's no lift   4 B by bus   5 B go to the same hotel
NoteThe full audio script for Track 11.14 is printed in the Student's Book after the questions, so students can read it when they check. If there is no audio in your class, read it aloud twice at normal speed.
Unit 11 check · C  Writing (sample, 48 words)
Sample answerHi Anna! Next week in Homs it's going to be hot – about thirty-four degrees in the day and twenty at night. You should bring light clothes, a hat and good shoes for walking. You should visit the old market early in the morning, because it's cooler and the sellers have time to talk to you.
"I can…" checklist
Students tick for themselves. If several students tick 😐 for the same line, take five minutes at the start of the next lesson: the two lines that usually need it are going to for plans (Stage 1 of 11.1) and explaining the conditions in a leaflet (11.7, Stage 3).

Unit 12  Celebrations & Customs — Answer Key
Proactive English · A2 · Book 1 · Syria Edition · Answer Key
How to use this keyEvery task has an answer or a full sample, including brainstorms, free speaking, reports, peer-review slips and homework.This unit is about customs, so many answers depend on the students' own families and regions. There is no single right custom. Accept any answer that is accurate for the student's own experience and uses the target language correctly — and use the differences in the room as the lesson.

12.1  What do people do at Eid?
Stage 1 · Get ready
a Typical board list: Eid al-Fitr · Eid al-Adha · Ramadan · Christmas and Easter · New Year · Mother's Day (21 March) · Teachers' Day · weddings and engagements · a new baby · graduations · the olive harvest. Accept every celebration the class names, religious or not.
b Open. Most classes choose visit · cook · wear · give · clean — the five verbs that run through the whole unit.
Stage 2 · Listen and read
a B – a visitor from another country (Mr Lang).
b 1 They clean the house and buy new clothes, especially for the children; Lina's mother cooks for two days (for about forty people).   2 The young people visit the older people first – grandparents, uncles and aunts.
Language box · Practise
1 Before the guests arrive, we clean the house. (or: We clean the house before the guests arrive.)
2 After we eat, we drink coffee.   3 When the children see their grandfather, they kiss his hand.   4 My mother cooks for two days before the Eid starts.
Stage 3 · Practise (sample – Celebration card A, Mother's Day)
Sample answerA: Can I ask you something? What is Mother's Day here?B: It's on the twenty-first of March, the first day of spring. It means something like a day to thank your mother.A: And what do people actually do?B: Before the day, children buy flowers or a small present, and the schools prepare songs.A: And on the day?B: When the family comes together, we eat lunch at my mother's house. After that, everybody phones the mothers who live far away.A: Who visits who?B: The children go to the mother – never the other way.A: How long does it last?B: One day only, but the radio plays old songs about mothers for a week!
Stage 3R · Perform – listener task (sample)
Before the day, children buy flowers. · When the family comes together, they eat lunch at the mother's house. · After lunch, everybody phones the mothers who live far away.
Stage 4 · Your turn (sample)
Sample answerIn our village the olive harvest is like a celebration. Before it starts, the whole family comes from the city, even the cousins. When they arrive, everybody works – the men on the trees, the women and children with the boxes. After the day in the field, we eat together outside, and somebody always plays music. It isn't a holiday, but it feels like one.
Stage 4R · Report (sample)
Sample answerNour told me about the olive harvest in her village. Before it starts, the whole family comes from the city, even the cousins who live far away. When they arrive, everybody works, and the children carry the small boxes. After the day in the field, they eat together outside and somebody plays music. She says it isn't a holiday, but it feels like one.
Board clinic – typical mistakes
✗ Everybody clean the house → ✓ Everybody cleans.   ✗ When the guests will arrive → ✓ When the guests arrive.   ✗ People visits → ✓ People visit.   ✗ We are eating sweets every Eid → ✓ We eat sweets every Eid.
Stage 5 · Read
1 Three days.   2 Because families are buying new clothes and sweets, so the markets stay open very late in the last days of Ramadan.   3 You don't have to bring a present, but sweets are welcome; say yes if somebody invites you; and don't eat too much in the first house.
Stage 6 · Write with help (sample)
Sample answerMOTHER'S DAY IN SYRIA – what a visitor should knowMother's Day is on 21 March, the first day of spring. It lasts one day.Before. In the week before, children buy flowers or a small present, and schools prepare songs and cards.The day. When the family comes together, everybody eats lunch at the mother's or the grandmother's house. Then people phone the mothers who live in another city.What people eat: a big family lunch and sweets in the afternoon.For a visitor: you don't have to bring anything, but flowers are always right.
Stage 7 · Write freely (sample, 104 words)
Sample answerA SYRIAN WEDDING – what a visitor should knowA wedding here is not one evening: it is two or three events. It usually starts with the engagement party some weeks before, in the family's home.Before. The two families visit each other, and the bride's family prepares the house. Everybody buys new clothes – guests too.The day. When the guests arrive at the hall, they greet the families at the door first, and then they sit. There is music, and later a big dinner. The couple arrive after the guests, not before.Special word: "zaffeh" – the music and singing that bring the couple into the hall. It is the loudest and the happiest part of the evening.For a visitor: you don't have to dance, but you should stand up during the zaffeh. Nobody stays sitting then.
Stage 7P · Respond (sample slip)
★ Now I understand why the couple arrive late!   ✓ You wrote "zaffeh" and explained it – good – but you didn't explain "engagement party".   ? What should I wear?
Homework (sample, 36 words)
Sample answerIn our house we always clean the whole kitchen the night before a feast, even at two in the morning. My grandmother says a feast cannot start in a dirty kitchen. Nobody sleeps, but nobody complains either.

12.2  You are invited
Stage 1 · Get ready
a The five things: who is inviting · what the event is · when (date and time) · where (the place and the address) · how and by when to answer (RSVP). Accept "how much / what to bring" as a sixth.
b Friendly: Come at eight! · See you there 😊 · Bring the kids.   Formal: We are pleased to invite you · We look forward to seeing you.
Stage 2 · Listen and read
a B – a wedding.
b 1 Friday 24 October at six in the evening, at the Rose Hall in Homs.   2 Because the bus from Damascus takes two hours, so they have to leave at three at the latest.
Language box · Practise
1 f   2 F   3 f   4 F   5 f
Stage 3 · Practise (sample – Invitation card A)
Sample answerA: Look what came today! An invitation to the centre's certificate evening.B: Read it!A: "The Al-Yasmin Centre is pleased to invite you to the end-of-year certificate evening, on Thursday the 19th of December at half past five, in the big hall on the second floor."B: And do we have to answer?A: Yes – RSVP to Mr Bassam by the 12th of December.B: How are we going to get there?A: It's at the centre, so the same as every lesson.B: Then say yes from me – and I'm bringing my mother.
Stage 3R · Perform – listener task (sample)
Who: the Al-Yasmin Centre · What: the end-of-year certificate evening · When: Thursday 19 December, 5.30 pm · Where: the big hall, second floor · Answer: to Mr Bassam by 12 December.
Stage 4 · Your turn (sample for b – saying no)
Sample answerB: Thank you very much for the invitation – that's really kind of you.A: So you're coming?B: I'm afraid I can't. I work until seven on Saturdays and I can't change it this month.A: That's a pity.B: I'm sorry. I hope you all have a wonderful evening, and I'm going to send a card with my sister.
Stage 4R · Report (sample)
Sample answerSamir invited me to his sister's graduation party on Saturday the 7th at five, at their house in Jaramana, and he asked me to answer by Wednesday. I said I'm afraid I can't come, because I work until seven on Saturdays, but I'm going to send a card and I hope they have a wonderful evening.
Board clinic – typical mistakes
✗ I will be pleasure to attend → ✓ I will be pleased to attend.   ✗ Thank you for your invite → ✓ Thank you for your invitation.   ✗ I can't come because busy → ✓ because I'm busy / because I work until seven.   ✗ Mixing registers: Dear Mr and Mrs Dabbagh, count me in!
Stage 5 · Read
1 Any two of: are pleased to invite you · We look forward to seeing you · RSVP (also the full names and titles, and no contractions).
2 How many people are coming (six) and what time they will arrive.
3 So that she knows how much cake to make.
Stage 6 · Write with help (sample)
Sample answerFormal replyDear Mr and Mrs Haddad,Thank you very much for your kind invitation to Lara's graduation party.We will be pleased to attend. Three of us are coming, and we will arrive at about five o'clock.We wish Lara every success in her new work.Best regards, Rana KhouryFriendly replyOf course we're coming! 🎓 Three of us, and I'm bringing the sweets. See you at five!
Stage 7 · Write freely (sample, 96 words)
Sample answer(a) The invitation (friendly)Hi everyone! 🎉 We're opening our little shop on Saturday 12 April at eleven in the morning, in Al-Zahra Street next to the bakery. Come and drink coffee with us and see the place. Bring the children – there's cake. Just tell me by Thursday so I know how many cups to buy. Hiba(b) The reply I would likeCongratulations, Hiba! Of course we're coming – four of us, and we'll be there at eleven. We're so happy for you. Tell us if you need help with the tables in the morning. See you Saturday!
Stage 7P · Respond (sample slip)
★ Everything is clear – I know where to go and when to answer.   ✓ You didn't say how long it lasts.   ? Can I bring my sister?
Homework (sample "no" reply, 44 words)
Sample answerHiba, thank you so much for inviting us. I'm really sorry – I'm in Aleppo that weekend for my brother's exam results and I can't change the tickets. I hope the opening is full of people. Can I come on the Monday and see the shop quietly?

12.3  Congratulations!
Stage 1 · Get ready
a birthday → Happy birthday! / Many happy returns! · wedding → Congratulations! / Best wishes! · new baby → Congratulations! · new job → Congratulations! / Well done! · passing an exam → Well done! / Congratulations! · new house → Congratulations! · a feast → Happy Eid! / Happy holidays!
b Open. Many students mention Mabrouk (for anything new or any good news) and hamdillah 3a salamtak (safe return / after an illness). English has no single phrase for either: the closest are Congratulations and Welcome back / I'm glad you're home safely. This gap is worth five minutes — it is exactly the kind of thing a mediator has to explain.
Stage 2 · Listen and read
a B – two (Nour's contract and Hiba's cousin's wedding).
b 1 She finished her training at TechCare and starts on Sunday as a junior technician with a real contract.   2 Everybody writes one line for Nour and one for Hiba's cousin.
Language box · Practise
1 Congratulations! / Well done!   2 Congratulations!   3 Happy Eid! (Eid Mubarak)   4 Not congratulations – Get well soon. I'm sorry to hear that.   5 Congratulations! / Best wishes! / I hope you both have a wonderful day.
Stage 3 · Practise (sample – Situation card A, line 1)
Sample answerA: My colleague passed a very difficult exam last week.B: Congratulations! Tell him well done from me – I know he studied for months.A: I will. He was so nervous.B: Then he deserves it even more.
Stage 3R · Perform – listener task (sample)
Phrase: "Congratulations – well done." Extra sentence: "I know he studied for months." Yes, it fits: the exam is something he did, so Congratulations is right.
Stage 4 · Your turn (sample for b)
Sample answerNour: a work contract → Congratulations, Nour! You really worked for that.Samir: his brother is getting married in the spring → Best wishes to them both.Hiba: she moved into a new flat → Congratulations on the new flat! I hope you're very happy there.Fadi: his daughter started school → Well done to her! Is she happy?
Stage 4R · Report (sample)
Sample answerNour finished her training and she has a contract now – congratulations, Nour. Samir's brother is getting married in the spring, so best wishes to him. Hiba moved into a new flat last month, and Fadi's daughter started school this year – well done to her.
Board clinic – typical mistakes
✗ Congratulation! → ✓ Congratulations! (always plural)   ✗ Congratulations for your new job → ✓ Congratulations on your new job.   ✗ I hope you will have a nice day → ✓ I hope you have a nice day.   ✗ Congratulations for the baby of your sister → ✓ Congratulations on your sister's baby.
Stage 5 · Read
1 Card 3 (Jana) – short lines, a joke ("that's very old"), an emoji, simple words, "Auntie Lina".
2 Card 1, for Nour. It shows that the writer knows her, not just the news – and it says the success was earned.
3 Cards 1 and 2 (from everybody in 4B / from Class 4B).
Stage 6 · Write with help (sample)
Sample answerDear Ms Huda,Congratulations on twenty years of teaching! Half of this class came here because somebody in their family was once in your classroom.I hope the centre gives you a very long holiday.With our thanks, Class 4B
Stage 7 · Write freely (sample, 97 words for the three cards)
Sample answer1 · Something somebody didDear Fadi, congratulations on passing your driving test! Third time, and you told the whole class the examiner was more nervous than you. Well done – and please drive slowly for the first month. From Nour2 · A family occasionDear Rasha and Samer, congratulations to you both on your wedding. We wish you every happiness and a home that is always full of people. With our warmest wishes, Class 4B3 · A feastDear Mr Khaled, wishing you and your family a very happy Eid. I hope you have a few quiet days with the people you love, and that the office is silent until Monday. Best regards, Lina
Stage 7P · Respond (sample slip)
★ Card 1 is really for Fadi – nobody else could get it.   ✓ Card 2 could be for anybody; add one line about the couple.   ? Is "congratulations" right for a feast, or is "wishing you" better?
Homework
Students write a real card to a real classmate and give it in the next lesson. Collect nothing; read nothing aloud unless the writer offers. Check only that the phrase matches the occasion.
NoteTeacher note. In a class of adults there is almost always somebody with no good news this year, or with news they don't want to share. Stage 4 says "one piece of good news" — let students invent one, or talk about somebody else in their family, with no explanation asked for.

12.4  What should I bring?
Stage 1 · Get ready
a Open, and the answers vary a lot by region and family. Typical Syrian answers: wedding → money in an envelope, or gold for close family; new baby → money, clothes, or something for the baby's room; new house → sweets, a plant, something for the kitchen; birthday → a present or money for children; a first visit → sweets from a good shop, or fruit.
b Open. Expect disagreement about money (normal and useful at weddings here; impossible in some other countries) and about plants (lovely for some, "more work for the mother" for others). The disagreement is the point.
Stage 2 · Listen and read
a B – something for the house: a printed photo album.
b 1 Because they are eighteen people and eighteen small envelopes would be strange.   2 A photo album of Omar's pictures from the engagement party, with a card signed by everybody.
Language box · Practise
1 What should I bring?   2 What about a plant?   3 Why don't we give one present from everybody?   4 Do you think we should bring the children?   5 It's something that / which he can use every day. (Accept the sentence with no word: something he can use.)
Stage 3 · Practise (sample – Advice card A, line 1)
Sample answerA: I need some advice. My manager invited me to his house for the first time. What should I bring?B: What about flowers for his wife?A: I know, but I've never met her and I don't want it to look strange.B: Then why don't you take sweets from a good shop? Everybody shares them and nobody feels uncomfortable.A: That's a good idea. Do you think I should take something for the children too?B: Something very small, yes. Not a big toy.A: Perfect. Sweets and something small for the children.
Stage 3R · Perform – listener task (sample)
Ideas: flowers for the wife / sweets from a good shop. The first was refused because he has never met her and it could look strange.
Stage 4 · Your turn (sample for b – three rules for a visitor)
Sample answer1 Bring sweets from a good shop. They are always right, everybody shares them, and nobody has to find a place for them afterwards.2 Don't bring anything expensive. The family will feel they have to give something back, and that spoils the evening.3 Bring something small for the children if there are children. That is the thing the family remembers.
Stage 4R · Report (sample)
Sample answerWe think a visitor should bring sweets from a good shop – that's always safe and everybody shares them. They shouldn't bring something very expensive, because then the family feels they have to give something back. And they should bring something small for the children if there are children, because that is the thing people remember.
Board clinic – typical mistakes
✗ What about to bring sweets? → ✓ What about bringing sweets? / What about sweets?   ✗ Why don't we to give…? → ✓ Why don't we give…?   ✗ I suggest you to bring → ✓ I suggest bringing / You should bring.   ✗ Do you think should I bring? → ✓ Do you think I should bring?
Stage 5 · Read
1 It's personal; nobody else is going to give them that; and it isn't expensive.
2 The time – whether Omar can finish it before the 24th.
3 He says that if somebody can't pay this month it's absolutely fine – they just sign the card.
Stage 6 · Write with help (sample)
Sample answerHi everyone! About Ms Huda's last lesson before the holiday. What about one present from all of us instead of twelve small ones?I think it's a good idea because she would have to carry twelve bags home, and because one good thing is better than twelve small ones.My idea: a framed photo of the whole class, with our names on the back. It costs about 1,200 each. If this month is difficult for somebody, that's absolutely fine – just sign the back.Please answer by Sunday evening so I can order the frame.Lina
Stage 7 · Write freely (sample, 103 words)
Sample answerHi everyone 👋 — about Abu Samir, our neighbour on the ground floor.He has carried everybody's bags up the stairs for fifteen years and he never takes anything for it. He is leaving next month to live with his daughter in Tartus.What about one present from the whole building? My idea is a good coat for the winter – he wears the same one every year – and a card with a line from every family.It's about 3,000 from each flat. If that is too much this month, please write your line in the card anyway: the card is the real present.Tell me yes or no by Friday so I can buy it in time.Rana (flat 4)
Stage 7P · Respond (sample slip)
★ I'm in – and the card idea is better than the coat.   ✓ Can you say by when we should pay, not only answer?   ? Can I give something instead of money – I have a good scarf, new?
Homework (sample follow-up, 37 words)
Sample answerThank you to the nine families who answered! We have 27,000, which is enough for the coat. If flats 2, 7 and 9 still want to join, there is time until Wednesday – and the card is still open for everybody.

12.5  While we were waiting, the lights went out
Stage 1 · Get ready
a Open. Typical one-liners: It rained on my sister's wedding day. · The electricity went off. · The cake arrived with the wrong name on it. · Forty people came and we had chairs for twenty.
b We were eating started first (the long action); my uncle arrived happened during it.   In the second sentence the music started first, and then everybody stood up (two short actions, one after the other).
Stage 2 · Listen and read
a B – the electricity went off, in the whole street.
b 1 They were waiting for the dinner; Rasha's uncle was making a speech; later everybody was singing.   2 He ran to his car and brought his camera light.
Language box · Practise
1 While we were eating, my uncle arrived.   2 I was taking photos when the music started.   3 What were you doing when the lights went off?   4 She wasn't listening – she was talking to her sister.
Stage 3 · Practise (sample – Story card A, the birthday)
Sample answerA: It was my mother's birthday and we wanted everything to be perfect. At six we were decorating the room, and while we were putting up the lights, the cake fell on the floor.B: Oh dear! What did you do?A: Nothing, at first. We just looked at it. Then at seven the guests were arriving and we were still cleaning the kitchen.B: Did your mother see it?A: She saw everything. She always sees everything.B: And the cake?A: My brother ran to the shop and bought sweets. By eight everybody was laughing about it, and my mother said it was the best birthday for years.
Stage 3R · Perform – listener task (sample)
While we were putting up the lights, the cake fell on the floor. · The guests were arriving when we were still cleaning the kitchen.
Stage 4 · Your turn (sample)
Sample answerIt was my sister's graduation. The whole family was waiting outside the hall and my mother was taking photos of everybody. While we were waiting, it started to rain – really hard – and two hundred people ran inside at the same time. My brother was getting his certificate when my phone died, so we have no photos of that moment. But a man next to us was filming, and the next day he sent us everything. We still have his number.
Stage 4R · Report (sample)
Sample answerFadi was at his sister's graduation. While the family was waiting outside the hall, it started to rain and everybody ran inside. His brother was getting his certificate when Fadi's phone died, so they have no photos of the moment. But a man they didn't know was filming, and the next day he sent them everything.
Board clinic – typical mistakes
✗ While we ate, the lights went off → ✓ While we were eating.   ✗ When I was arriving, the music was starting → ✓ When I arrived, the music started.   ✗ He was make a speech → ✓ He was making.   ✗ We was waiting → ✓ We were waiting.
Stage 5 · Read
1 They arrived at six; the hall was full of flowers; Rasha looked beautiful.
2 He was making a speech – and he stopped in the middle of a sentence.
3 With a feeling, not a fact: "It was the best wedding I have been to", and an invitation to come next time.
Stage 6 · Write with help (sample)
Sample answerThe graduation – and the rainDear Lama,You have to hear about Saturday.Everything started well. We arrived at ten, and the whole family was outside the hall taking photos.Then, while we were waiting to go in, it started to rain. My mother was standing in her new shoes when it happened.Two hundred people ran to the door at the same time. While we were pushing inside, my phone died.When my brother got his certificate, I had no camera and I just watched him instead. Maybe that was better.It was a wet day and a very happy one.Love, Hiba
Stage 7 · Write freely (sample, 141 words)
Sample answerThe night the bus didn't comeDear Rana,I'm still laughing about this, so you have to hear it.Last Thursday we were going to my cousin's engagement party in Homs. Everything was fine at the beginning: six of us, good clothes, presents, and we were at the bus station an hour early.Then, while we were drinking tea in the station café, somebody said that our bus was cancelled. The next one was at nine – three hours later, and the party started at seven.Everybody was looking at me, because I booked the tickets. I was phoning every bus company in Damascus when an old man at the next table said, "I'm driving to Homs now. I have four seats."We looked at each other for about two seconds. Then four of us went with him and two stayed for the nine o'clock bus.We arrived at half past seven, before the food. The old man refused to take any money and wished my cousin a happy life.Now when anything goes wrong in our family, somebody says: "Don't worry, a man with a car will come."Love, Lina
Stage 7P · Respond (sample slip)
★ I could see it – especially the two seconds when you looked at each other.   ✓ Add what the other people in the café were doing.   ? What did your cousin say when you arrived?
Homework (sample paragraph, 38 words)
Sample answerWhile we were standing at the station door, the driver was shouting the names of cities and the café man was washing the same glass again and again. Nobody was helping us. Then the old man stood up.

12.6  It's polite to take your shoes off
Stage 1 · Get ready
a Typical five: arrive a little late, not exactly on time · bring sweets, not something expensive · look at the door for shoes · expect a lot of food and refuse gently · stay for coffee after the meal.
b Open, and students will disagree — that is useful. Common Syrian answers: arrive exactly on time = usually false (fifteen minutes later is normal) · refuse twice = often true, the host insists · a present for the children = true and much appreciated · stay two hours = true or longer; leaving straight after the food is the mistake visitors make.
Stage 2 · Listen and read
a B – to Omar's family home, for lunch.
b 1 At about one fifteen, although they said one o'clock (fifteen minutes late is perfect; half an hour is also fine).   2 It's normal to say "no thank you, it was delicious" two or three times – and normal for the hosts to continue. Put your hand on your heart and smile.
Language box · Practise (samples)
1 It's polite to take your shoes off.   2 It's normal to arrive fifteen minutes late.   3 It isn't usual to leave after the food.   4 You don't have to bring a present, but it's nice to bring sweets.
Stage 3 · Practise (sample – Situation card A, line 1)
Sample answerA: They invited me for lunch at one. What should I know?B: It's normal to arrive about fifteen minutes late. If you come at one exactly, the family is still cooking.A: Really? And a present?B: You don't have to bring anything, but it's nice to bring sweets. It isn't a good idea to bring something expensive.A: And at the door?B: Look at the floor. If you see shoes there, it's polite to take yours off.A: And when can I go home?B: Not immediately after the food! It isn't polite to leave before the coffee.
Stage 3R · Perform – listener task (sample)
Arrive fifteen minutes late · take your shoes off if you see shoes at the door · stay for coffee after the meal. The one that surprises visitors most is usually arriving late on purpose.
Stage 4 · Your turn (sample for b – two differences)
Sample answerDifference 1 – time. Here it's normal to arrive fifteen or twenty minutes after the time. In Germany, Mr Lang says, you arrive at the exact minute, and being late is impolite. Neither is wrong: here "one o'clock" means "we start at one"; there it means "be here at one".Difference 2 – food. Here the host puts food on your plate and you refuse two or three times before they stop. In some countries you serve yourself and "no thank you" means no the first time. A visitor from there thinks we don't listen; we think they aren't eating enough.
Stage 4R · Report – mediation (sample)
Sample answerI explained a first visit to a Syrian home. I said it's normal to arrive about fifteen minutes late, and it's polite to take your shoes off if you see shoes at the door. You don't have to bring a present, but it's nice to bring sweets. Hiba told me that in some countries people arrive exactly on time and the visit is shorter, so a visitor from there might leave straight after the food – and here that looks cold.
NoteMediation – what to assess here. Did the student (1) describe the custom accurately, (2) give the reason behind it, (3) use It's + adjective + to and you don't have to… but… instead of "you must" everywhere, and (4) present the other country's custom as different, not wrong? Point 4 is the real mediation skill in this cycle.
Board clinic – typical mistakes
✗ It's polite taking your shoes off → ✓ It's polite to take.   ✗ You mustn't bring a present (= it's forbidden!) → ✓ You don't have to bring a present.   ✗ Is polite to… → ✓ It's polite to…   ✗ It's normal arrive late → ✓ It's normal to arrive late.
Stage 5 · Read
1 Because the family then feels they have to give something back.
2 Say "no thank you, it was delicious" two or three times, put your hand on your heart and smile.
3 Because a nervous visitor is afraid of making a mistake; the last line tells them that the welcome matters more than the rules.
Stage 6 · Write with help (sample)
Sample answerVISITING A SYRIAN OFFICE – a short guide for a foreign colleagueThe time. For a meeting, arrive on time. For a visit to somebody's desk, any time before two is fine.A present. You don't have to bring anything, but it's nice to bring sweets on your first day or after a holiday. It isn't a good idea to give one person an expensive present, because the others see it.Coffee. It's polite to accept the first coffee, even a small one. Saying no to all of it looks cold.The food. If people are eating at a desk, they will invite you. It's normal to say yes and take a little.And finally. Nobody is going to be angry if you make a mistake.
Stage 7 · Write freely (sample, 152 words)
Sample answerA SYRIAN WEDDING – a short guide for our foreign guestsBefore. If you are invited, answer early. The family is counting chairs and food, and "maybe" is the most expensive word at a wedding.What to wear. Guests dress well – better than you think. It isn't polite to come straight from work in ordinary clothes, and nobody minds if you are the best-dressed person in the room.The time. The card says six. Guests arrive between six and seven, and the couple arrive later. It's normal to wait; it isn't a mistake in the organisation.The present. Most guests give money in an envelope, and for a young couple that is the most useful thing. You don't have to do that – a present from your country is lovely – but don't worry that money looks cold. Here it doesn't.At the hall. Greet the two families at the door before you sit down. When the music brings the couple in, everybody stands up – so stand up too.And finally. You will be photographed. Many times. Smile and enjoy it.
Stage 7P · Respond (sample slip)
★ This makes me feel ready – especially the part about money not being cold.   ✓ Add why guests arrive late, not only that they do.   ? What happens if I leave early – is that rude?
Homework (sample, 36 words)
Sample answerIn Japan you give and take a present with two hands, and people don't open it in front of the guest. A Syrian visitor should know that – here we open it immediately and show everybody.

12.7  Review & Mediate: The invitation and the programme
Stage 1 · Unit 12 quiz
1 When the guests arrive, we eat.   2 It's polite to take your shoes off.   3 While we were eating, the lights went off.   4 Congratulations on your new job.   5 What about giving a photo album?   6 Everybody cleans the house before the feast.
Stage 2 · Listen and read
b She tells him to come at six o'clock, because her level (A2) gets the certificates at 6.15 and the paper says come fifteen minutes before your level. (He doesn't have to stay for the whole evening.)
Stage 3 · Practise (sample – Event card A, the school open day)
Sample answerA: It's the school open day, Saturday the 8th of March, from nine to one.B: All morning?A: You don't have to stay all morning. The classrooms open at nine, the children sing at half past ten, there's tea with the teachers at half past eleven, and photographs at half past twelve.C: We have an A2 child, so the singing is the important part for us.A: Then come at about ten – you see a little of the classroom and then the singing.B: Can we take the car?A: No cars in the school street. Leave it on the main road and walk.C: And does it cost anything?A: It's free, and they give tea and sweets. So we don't have to take anything.B: Good. Ten o'clock, no car, nothing to carry.
Stage 3R · Perform – listener task (sample)
Arrive at 10.00 (the singing is at 10.30) · take nothing (free, tea and sweets provided) · condition remembered: no cars in the school street.
Stage 4 · Your turn (sample)
Sample answerIt's my nephew's graduation party, on Saturday the 14th at five, at their house in Jaramana – the building behind the pharmacy, third floor.You don't have to answer formally, but tell my sister by Thursday so she knows the numbers.It's free, obviously, but everybody takes something sweet. There's no parking in their street, so leave the car on the main road and walk five minutes.Children are welcome; there will be six or seven of them. It says five, but nothing starts before half past.
Stage 4R · Report – mediation (sample)
Sample answerSamir explained his nephew's graduation party. It's on Saturday the 14th at five, at their house in Jaramana, behind the pharmacy. It's free, but everybody takes something sweet. There's no parking in their street, so you leave the car on the main road and walk five minutes. Children are welcome. I decided I would go at about half past five, because nothing starts at five, and I would take sweets from the shop near my house.
